No bar value or meaning changed, no measured run is reinterpreted, SL-14-owned behavior and a fresh independent holdout stay closure-gating, and a failure on a mechanically proven byte-identical pre-SL-14 prompt is attributed to the pre-existing SL-4-era finding instead of to this Slice. The rule is deterministic code with seven mutation tests and **was frozen by commit before any closure evidence existed**. The re-scoped weakness is **carried forward as §8 Q11, not closed**, for disposition before R1 graduation. **SL-14 IS DONE, effective on the merge of v1.43 after human review** (§6). **§8 Q12 and Q13 are both RESOLVED by the Accountable on 2026-09-16.** Q12 corrected attribution once more, prospectively, from **scenario-level to assertion-level** ownership — a contract-modeling correction, not a bar change, and explicitly not a reinterpretation of holdout 7, which is preserved exactly as observed at 9 of 10 with its fabrication still recorded as a real SL-4-era judge failure. The corrected rule stays fail-closed, carries **nine mutation tests all caught**, is **proven verdict-neutral against 350 recorded scenario-runs**, and **was frozen by commit before any evidence under it existed**; **holdout 8** was then authored and frozen unrun before it ran. **The closure evidence at the unchanged implementation `4298818` MEETS the Definition of Done: 0 of 20 runs are closure-gating on both sets**, blind retries, stranded failures and re-asks are **zero in all twenty**, and ordinary rate held in all twenty. Three rounds of pre-ratification review followed, and on 2026-09-17 **both contracts were ratified — §8 Q14 (ADR-111 rev 2) and §8 Q15 (ADR-112 rev 4) are both RESOLVED**, TD-13 is no longer TENTATIVE, and **no decision blocks C1, C2 or C6 any longer**. Each round returned a mechanically provable defect rather than a wording preference: rev 2 corrected a durability guarantee its mechanism could not provide, rev 3 separated the event-obligation durability boundary from delivery-readiness and removed a racing sequence counter, and rev 4 retracted rev 3's own claim that the drain preserves array order — **removing the per-appointment delivery-order guarantee** because no consumer needs it and enforcing it would stall later events behind one unresolved entry — and made finalization a **conditional compare-and-set** so the writer and the recovery sweep cannot both succeed. **Ratification authorizes no implementation**: no Slice contract, bar, stage, estimate or Release Scope changed, no Slice became READY, and no key exists until **§8 Q16** names a provisioning owner, which is the only open dependency the ratified contracts carry. **The ask is now Traditional Gu implementation planning rather than a decision** (§1, §3, §8). **The loop then ran again and replenishment finally produced a finding instead of a boundary report: SL-6 was elaborated and evaluated NOT READY, and its recorded gate names the wrong contract** (§9). > **Artifact role:** Owns the **durable Slice contracts** for R1 and their order. It does **not** own intended behavior (the four approved Specs do), consequential architecture (the Architecture Analysis and ADR-106…110 do), approved technical realization and sequencing (the Technical Plan does), just-in-time implementation Tasks and pre-PR execution context (the agent runtime does), or recorded execution state — branch, commits, PR, CI, merge, Actions, environment approvals (GitHub does).

This was the first Gu OS Roadmap Increment migrated to the Slice planning model. It now operates under **Methodology v0.4.7** — Slice planning and readiness §10–§10.4, the **READY Horizon** and readiness replenishment §10.5, Execution Cycle planning §12.1–§12.2, the **Development Continuity Loop** §12.3, dependency-safe planning §12.4, Release Scope and the Done boundary §14.2 (with its accepted-deviation rule), **Roadmap Increment graduation** §17.2, and the authority boundaries §19.1–§19.2. Slice contracts here were **extracted** from approved Technical Plan v1.4 §9; no scope, behavior, acceptance meaning, architecture decision or sequencing was changed in the move. Section 8 records what the migration exposed and needs human resolution.

## 1. How to read this plan

- The **Specs** own intended behavior. A Slice owns a bounded increment that proves part of it, or a required enabling capability.
- Slice contracts are written **rolling wave**: completed Slices preserve the durable contract they were held to; elaborated and READY Slices carry their full durable Slice contracts; later Slices may remain shallow stubs carrying only enough to sequence, size and prioritize them until their dedicated elaboration and readiness pass. "Full" describes depth relative to a stub, not finality — a Slice contract may still be refined when evidence exposes an owning-artifact issue. The current per-Slice snapshot is owned by the header, the §3 index and the READY Horizon above, not by this rule.
- **Tasks are not written here.** They are derived by the coding agent once a Slice is Ready, Planned and Executable.
- **Readiness lives here; execution stage does not.** A Slice can be READY with nobody assigned; the Accountable / DRI is confirmed at planning time and recorded in §5.
- Nothing in this document is a live status board. See §5.
- Stage gates (**shadow** SL-0…SL-8b → **assisted** SL-9…SL-10 → **selective live** SL-11+) are business/authority gates per Methodology §12, **not** per-Slice approvals, and are not readiness conditions. They live in Technical Plan §5.
- **This is the integrated Slice Plan for the R1 Roadmap Increment** (Methodology §10.1) — it holds the Slices realizing R1, not a perpetual list of every Slice Relationship Operations will ever have.
- **Closing Slices does not graduate R1.** R1's graduation evidence is owned by the [Roadmap](../../../roadmap/gu-os-evolution-roadmap.md) §4, and graduation is evaluated against that declared evidence, not against an emptied Slice list (Methodology §17.2). Read this plan together with those criteria.

### READY Horizon

The **READY Horizon** is the amount of genuinely READY work maintained ahead of execution so development does not stall between Slices or Cycles (Methodology §10.5). It is maintained **proactively**, not only once current work runs out.

- It is **capacity-based, not a fixed Slice count** — roughly one to two Execution Cycles of plausible agent-assisted capacity as an operating default, revised from calibration evidence (§5, Methodology §17.1).
- **Only genuinely READY Slices count toward it.** The horizon is an output of valid readiness, never a quota.
- **Maintaining the horizon never lowers the Definition of Ready** (Methodology §10.2). A Slice is not labelled READY to fill the horizon.
- **READY ≠ PLANNED ≠ EXECUTABLE.** Readiness makes a Slice eligible for Cycle planning; planning confirms Cycle inclusion and a human Accountable / DRI; executability additionally requires prerequisites actually satisfied and capacity available (Methodology §10.2, §12.1).

**Current READY Horizon: empty again — because SL-15 was Planned the same day it became READY** *(as of v1.58)*. Created and elaborated 2026-09-18 after the Accountable resolved §8 Q18 as alternative 3. It entered the horizon in v1.57 as READY and not Planned, and left it the same day when admitted to **Cycle 3 as order 8** with **Alejandro Torres Padilla** as the confirmed Accountable / DRI (§5). That is the horizon working as intended: §10.5 maintains READY work *ahead of execution*, and work that becomes executable immediately does not wait in it. **SL-6 remains Planned in Cycle 3 as order 7 and is not Done**; T1–T6 are on main; **T7 is not started** and waits on this Slice's binding path. **Replenishment therefore continues** under case D.

**Previous state: SL-15 — Contact / legacy-lead identity seam (repair)** *(as of v1.57)*. Created and elaborated 2026-09-18 after the Accountable resolved §8 Q18 as alternative 3. It was **READY and not Planned** (§4, §9). SL-6 remained Planned in Cycle 3 as order 7 and was **not Done**; T7 was **not started** and waited on this Slice's binding path.

**Previous state: empty again — because SL-6 was Planned the same day it became READY** *(as of v1.51)*. SL-6 entered the horizon and left it within hours, admitted to **Cycle 3 as order 7** with a confirmed Accountable / DRI (§5). That is the horizon working as intended rather than failing: §10.5 maintains READY work *ahead of execution*, and work that becomes executable immediately does not wait in it. **Replenishment therefore continues** under case D, and the first candidates are the dependency markers SL-6 proved can be wrong.

**Previous state: SL-6 — Bindings + authority (advisory)** *(as of v1.50)*. **The horizon was empty from SL-12's closure on 2026-09-14 until 2026-09-17**, across three replenishment attempts that each reported the same boundary. It was replenished not by a new Slice but by **elaborating SL-6 far enough to discover that its recorded cross-repo gate was the wrong contract** (§9), and by the Accountable resolving the real blocker as §8 Q17 the same day. SL-14 had left the horizon with its Done record (§6), as SL-3, SL-4, SL-7 and SL-12 did before it.

**Replenishment re-tested on 2026-09-16, without result — case D of the continuity loop** (Methodology §12.3, §10.5). SL-14's closure changes no dependency fact among the candidates, because nothing depended on SL-14: it was created to close SL-4's carry-forward finding and no later Slice waits behind it. Every verdict in the §3 table stands, so **no candidate can be made READY by readiness work**, and the blocking set is unchanged from the 2026-09-15 re-test:

- no artifact records C1, C2 or C6 as available, and the Technical Plan's §4 contract table still names their fallbacks as the current state;
- SL-5 waits on C1, SL-6 on C2 advisory, SL-9 on the C6 hard gate — **and SL-6's entry here was wrong.** Its elaboration of 2026-09-17 (§9) established that C2 is not its gate and that the conversation-authority evidence path is; the Accountable resolved that as §8 Q17 the same day, **SL-6 is now READY** (§4), and it is the one candidate this blocking set misread. The line is kept as the dated record of the set as it was read at the time;
- SL-8 and SL-8b wait behind SL-5's C1, and SL-13 behind SL-9.

**C1, C2 and C6 are owned by the Traditional Gu team, not by this one**, so advancing them is a human action and not readiness work an agent can perform. That is the boundary, and it is the same one SL-12's closure reached. **What the loop produced instead of a READY Slice is the request itself**, stated precisely enough to be acted on: [`cross-repo-contract-requests.md`](cross-repo-contract-requests.md) derives the concrete per-contract ask from the Technical Plan, TD-5, TD-3, TD-13 and the legacy-source audit, records what this team does when each contract lands, and names the decisions that block the ask. That document owns no truth and ratifies nothing.

**The loop ran once more on 2026-09-17 and reduced the ask from a description to a specification — again without producing a READY Slice, because the boundary is unchanged.** A read-only cross-repository revalidation against `UnggaMX/ungga-full` @ `gcp/main` `3fdb16ca` and `UnggaMX/ungga-landing` @ `main` `ce60cb22` ([`legacy-source-audit.md`](legacy-source-audit.md) §24) established what the other team would actually have to build, and the two consequential choices inside it were extracted into ADRs so that ratification is a decision about a contract rather than about an intention: **[ADR-111](../../../adr/ADR-111-legacy-service-auth-v1.md)** fixes TD-13's wire protocol to implementation precision and adds the `legacy-read` purpose C6 needs, with a shared interoperability fixture both repositories can prove themselves against; **[ADR-112](../../../adr/ADR-112-cross-repo-integration-events.md)** decides C1's event identity, envelope, normalized semantics and durable producer-side publication. [`technical-plan.md`](technical-plan.md) v1.19 Appendix D maps both onto named legacy seams. **The pass also corrected one Gu OS defect it found** — the legacy appointment normalizer discarded Calendar effect evidence from the canonical store, which is precisely the orphan case the audit warns about — repaired with fixture and selftest coverage.

**Three rounds of pre-ratification review followed, and on 2026-09-17 both contracts were ratified.** **[ADR-111](../../../adr/ADR-111-legacy-service-auth-v1.md) is ratified at rev 2** (§8 Q14) and **[ADR-112](../../../adr/ADR-112-cross-repo-integration-events.md) is ratified at rev 4** (§8 Q15); both are now **Accepted direction**. **TD-13 is no longer TENTATIVE, and no decision blocks C1, C2 or C6 any longer** — all three are settled specifications whose next lifecycle step is Traditional Gu implementation planning. Each review round held the architecture direction and returned a specific, mechanically provable defect rather than a wording preference, which is the reason the ratified contract is worth more than the original description was. Rev 2 had proved that the canonical Mongo appointment mutation and its event obligation are single-document atomic — and then treated that as making the event *deliverable*, when a logical appointment operation also produces Calendar and Firestore effects that the payload reports and that may still be unknown at that instant. Rev 3 separates the durability boundary from the delivery-readiness boundary, specifies recovery that records unresolved outcomes as `unknown` rather than guessing, and **removes the sequence counter rev 2 had introduced**, whose client-side increment could race and whose two jobs the pending array already does. Re-verifying the legacy store ordering for that revision also made it cheap: thirteen of the fourteen appointment writers already have every secondary outcome in hand when the canonical write commits. **Rev 4 then retracted one of rev 3's own claims** — that the drain preserves the pending array's order, which cannot hold when a later entry finalizes in one write and an earlier one is still pending — and **removed the per-appointment delivery-order guarantee rather than enforcing head-of-line delivery**, on the evidence that no Gu OS consumer of these events exists and that the inbox and specs never assumed ordering. It also made finalization a conditional compare-and-set, so the writer and the recovery sweep cannot both finalize one event with different outcomes. **This changes the §3 verdicts about decisions and not the conclusion:** C1, C2 and C6 are no longer blocked on any decision, and **no Traditional Gu implementation of anything exists yet**, which is the boundary this team cannot cross. **The ask is now the implementation itself rather than a decision about it**, and that is a human action across a team boundary.

**The loop ran again immediately after ratification, and this time replenishment produced a finding rather than another boundary report.** SL-6 was elaborated (§9) — an existing stub, so no approval was needed — to settle the two entries §4 carried in tension: what *"resolver answers match observed reality"* is measured against, and whether C2 advisory availability is genuinely a prerequisite. **It is not.** The legacy router calling `POST /api/legacy/authority` produces router-side recording that belongs to C2 and to SL-11's enforcement, and **no part of SL-6's inspectable outcome requires it**; the endpoint is buildable and provable Gu OS-side against the ratified ADR-111 fixture with nothing running on the other side. The measurement is settled as a semantic equivalence test on `human_active` against independently observed legacy takeover state, which required ruling out the two readings that look plausible and are not: comparing the resolver against its own inputs is **circular**, and the runtime-authority half is **vacuous**, because every relationship Case is created `legacy` and no code anywhere writes `gu_os` before SL-11. **SL-6 is NOT READY, and the blocker is a different one than the plan recorded:** the conversation-authority evidence path, now §8 **Q17**. TD-3 routes legacy takeover signals in through **C1 ingestion**, so as written SL-6 waits on C1 and not on C2; a bounded read is mechanically available and changes what an authority gate can be stale about, which makes the choice consequential architecture rather than an implementation detail. **The outcome was not weakened to obtain readiness** — the vacuous measurement was exactly the weakening available, and it was rejected.

**The unbuilt Gu OS-side seam was then assessed as a candidate Slice, and it is not one.** The question was fair to ask, because the seam is real and entirely ours: `apps/web/src/app/api/legacy/` does not exist at all, and Appendix D.1 records **one** TD-13 signer/verifier bridge serving C1 inbound, C2 inbound and callbacks — so that single component sits on the critical path of all three contracts, and ratification made it specifiable for the first time. It could be built and deterministically proven today. **That is not sufficient, and "the code can now be written" is not a Slice.** It fails on the two properties §10.1 requires. **There is no inspectable outcome:** a verifier that authenticates a signed request and refuses an unsigned or replayed one is a technical deliverable, not a business one — no prospect, advisor, Opportunity or Case changes state, and nothing wakes, because no counterparty emits. Starting from a route and a table rather than from a business responsibility is the decomposition the doctrine rejects (root `AGENTS.md` §4). **And no Release Scope is declarable:** the available evidence is local and deterministic, which is proper for a Task and thin for a Definition of Done, and the integration evidence that would make it meaningful cannot exist until Traditional Gu emits. **The correct home is inside the Slices that already own it** — SL-5's contract names *TD-13 auth, webhook ingestion, dedup/normalization* and SL-6's names the authority endpoint — as in-Slice enabling work, which §10.2 admits and which SL-7 and SL-14 both used. Recorded here so the candidate is not re-proposed on the same reasoning.

**Q17 was resolved the same day, and the READY Horizon is no longer empty.** The Accountable chose **option (c) — both evidence paths, with roles explicitly separated**: C1 answers what changed, when, with what provenance and what to wake; a new bounded semantic read answers what is operationally true *now* for the authority gate. Neither substitutes for the other, there is **no last-write-wins reconciliation** between them (EC-09), and no silent fallback to a stale projection. The decision also confined legacy's five-minute window to SL-6's independent oracle as an **observation of legacy behavior rather than a Gu OS invariant**, required each resolution to be **persisted** as the `authority_resolution` evidence SL-7 has been waiting for, required the equivalence oracle to share no decision code with the resolver under test, and stated the `bootstrap_direct` → least-privilege-legacy-side handover condition before the read may become load-bearing for prospect-facing effects. **SL-6 is now READY** (§4, §9), and **its `[L:C2 advisory]` marker is removed** — the only marker ever removed from §3, and removed by decision at the layer that owns the dependency. **A finding worth keeping from this:** the elaboration also established that **AC-1 §6.5 and AC-4 §9.5 already required re-reading current authoritative state before acting**, so Q17 reconciles TD-3's narrower ingestion-only wording with approved architecture rather than departing from it.

**Three governed questions are outstanding, three are resolved, and none blocks a Slice today:** §8 **Q14, Q15 and Q17 are all RESOLVED**; §8 Q16, the operational ownership of the credentials the ratified contracts need, remains deferred by choice now that its original sequencing reason has lapsed, and is the **only open dependency of those contracts** — SL-6 does not need it, because its endpoint is proven against the ratified fixture; §8 Q11 must be dispositioned before R1 graduation; and §8 Q6.

**The continuity loop ran again on 2026-09-18 after PR #75 merged** (`7a71351`; T1–T6 checkpoint SHAs preserved, including review SHA `1a0f09d`). SL-6 is **not Done**. T7 is **not started**. Independent A–D revalidation of the T7 binding-path finding established that the missing runtime Contact / conversation-binding materialization is **not already governed** inside the approved SL-6 / TD-4 boundary, and is **not** automatically a C1 / SL-5 dependency — Q17 still holds. That was **§8 Q18**. **Q18 is now RESOLVED as alternative 3** (§8): a new bounded repair Slice, **SL-15**, lands the omitted TD-8 admission seam under the Accountable's Contact materialization rule. SL-2's Done record is unchanged. No Contact is fabricated for hosted evidence. No Traditional Gu customer state is seeded. T7 stays unstarted until SL-15's binding path exists. **The Accountable then confirmed SL-15 into Cycle 3 as order 8** with himself as Accountable / DRI; SL-15 is **Planned and Executable** (§5). Implementation follows this record's merge.

**Graduation evidence evaluated 2026-09-16, and R1 does not graduate — for reasons that have nothing to do with either open question** (Methodology §17.2, §12.3 case G). The roadmap's declared outcome for R1 is that *in a pilot brokerage, Gu can keep opportunities alive across sessions/events and multiple advisor assignments, choose/execute allowed next work, route/escalate to the correct human, and link progression to visit request/attended-visit evidence without manual pipeline operation* ([`gu-os-evolution-roadmap.md`](../../../roadmap/gu-os-evolution-roadmap.md) §4). Against that, **a Slice backlog is not empty here and emptiness would not be graduation anyway**:

- **choosing** next work situationally is delivered and evidenced — SL-4, the Cycle 3 repair, SL-14 — and so is **routing and escalating to the correct human**, through SL-7 and SL-12's Portfolio;
- **executing** allowed next work reaches nothing outside Gu OS: every stage to date is **shadow**, and the first prospect-facing effect is SL-9;
- **event-driven** liveness is still the interim polling adapter, because SL-5 needs C1;
- **multi-advisor authority** is unresolved in the running system, because SL-6 needs C2 advisory;
- **visit-progression evidence** — the outcome the roadmap names first among equals — is SL-8, behind SL-5's C1;
- *without manual pipeline operation* is therefore not reached, and is not close.

**Eight Slices of declared outcome are unbuilt, and each is blocked on C1, C2 or C6.** The missing evidence is not additional verification of what exists; it is those Slices. **So §8 Q11 stays an input to graduation and is not dispositioned now**, which is the question this evaluation was run to answer: it is a real finding that must be settled before R1 graduates, and nothing about it is on the critical path to anything currently possible. **The increment-level blocker and the horizon-level blocker are the same one**, which is why this evaluation changes no plan and adds no work — it establishes that the boundary is genuine rather than a gap in this team's sequencing.

*The v1.36 state, preserved as it was recorded:*

- **How SL-14 entered.** It entered the horizon by a route other than replenishment. The Accountable's disposition of SL-4's carry-forward finding (option 2, 2026-09-15) created it inside already-approved R1 intent, and it was elaborated to READY the same day (§4, §9). It was **Planned into Cycle 3 as order 6** the same day (§5); a Planned Slice stays in the horizon until it is Done.
- **Its capacity.** Its one frozen range is `2–3 days` / **Low**, which is below the one-to-two-Cycle operating default.
- **Every other candidate stays blocked.** SL-14 depends on nothing the candidates below depend on, so every verdict in the table stands: they remain blocked on C1, C2 or C6, or on unbuilt Slices.

*The v1.31 state, preserved as it was recorded:* the horizon was empty. **SL-12 leaves the horizon with its Done record** (§6), as SL-3 did on 2026-09-07, SL-4 with v1.24 and SL-7 with v1.27. It had entered the horizon on 2026-09-14. SL-0, SL-1, SL-2, SL-3, SL-4, SL-7 and SL-12 are then Done, and SL-5, SL-6, SL-8…SL-11 and SL-13 remain NOT READY stubs. **The horizon is measured in capacity, not Slice count** (Methodology §10.5), and it now holds none.

**Replenishment re-tested on 2026-09-15, without result — case D of the continuity loop** (Methodology §12.3, §10.5). SL-12's closure changes no dependency fact among the candidates, because nothing depended on SL-12. Every verdict in the table below stands, so **no candidate can be made READY by readiness work**:

- no artifact records C1, C2 or C6 as available;
- SL-8 and SL-8b still wait behind SL-5's C1;
- SL-13 waits on SL-9, which waits on the C6 hard gate.

The systemic finding below is therefore now the whole of R1's remaining path. **Widening the horizon needs a human action:** moving C1, C2 and C6 with the Traditional Gu team. The development system can neither take that action nor lower the Definition of Ready to substitute for it. What stays executable inside existing authority is repair work, not Slices. The pre-existing audit gap SL-12 surfaced is one such item: it is dispositioned in §6 and scheduled as Cycle 3 order 4, which the Accountable confirmed on 2026-09-15 (§5). **It closed with v1.32** (§6). **Order 5** implemented the Accountable's decisions of 2026-09-15 on §8 Q7, Q8 and Q9, and closes with v1.34 (§6). After it, Cycle 3 holds no open work.

**Why SL-12, and why the horizon cannot be widened further.** SL-7's closure left the horizon empty — case D of the continuity loop (Methodology §12.3, §10.5) — and changed exactly one dependency fact among the candidates below: SL-12's hard dependency, SL-7, was satisfied. SL-12 was the one candidate gated on neither a Traditional Gu cross-repo contract nor an unbuilt Slice. Its elaboration classified the softer *"+SL-8 for richer evidence"* as enrichment, not a prerequisite (§4). **Every other verdict in the table below stands**, so the horizon is one Slice again, for the same systemic reason. The **usable calibration dataset is now five Slices — SL-1, SL-2, SL-3, SL-4 and SL-7** (SL-0's estimate predates the calibration model, §5), four of them *behavior* Slices. All five show the same finding: **frozen engineering estimates biased high, while human/external wait — and, for SL-4, required elapsed observation — dominated calendar time** (§5, §6).

**Replenishment was attempted on 2026-09-07, and it could not widen the horizon.** Recording the attempt and its result, because "the horizon is thin" and "the horizon cannot currently be widened" are different facts and only the second is actionable. Every remaining candidate was tested against the Definition of Ready (Methodology §10.2):

| Candidate | Dependency situation | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **SL-5** Event wake-ups | **C1** — event forwarding, owned by the Traditional Gu repo/team | **case C — NOT READY.** Outside our control. Also carries the unresolved §8 Q2 Release Scope question |
| **SL-6** Bindings + authority | **C2 advisory** — authority-aware routing, same external owner | **case C — NOT READY** |
| **SL-7** Work Portfolio v1 | SL-4 only, which is **ours** and already READY | **NOT READY — but for a different and fixable reason:** its Release Scope is not derivable, and the advisor-facing reading needs a deployed staging application runtime that does not exist and that no artifact owns. Recorded as **§8 Q5** |
| **SL-8** Visit evidence | SL-4 and **SL-5** | blocked behind SL-5's C1 |
| **SL-8b** Transaction seam | **SL-3 ✓** and SL-8 | SL-3's half is now satisfied; blocked behind the SL-8 → SL-5 → C1 chain |
| **SL-9…SL-11** | **C6 hard gate** (no production waiver), plus C3/C7/C2-enforcing | **case C — NOT READY** |
| **SL-12, SL-13** | SL-7 / SL-9 | far work; elaborating them now is exactly the waste rolling-wave planning avoids |

**The systemic finding.** Every path past SL-4 is gated either on a **cross-repo contract owned by the Traditional Gu team (C1, C2, C6)** or, for SL-7 alone, on a **release-boundary decision with an infrastructure consequence (§8 Q5)**. That is not an execution problem and it is not something readiness work can dissolve: **C1/C2/C6 availability is the single largest determinant of whether R1 can continue past SL-4**, and it needs a human to move it with the Traditional Gu team. Settling Q5 would return exactly one Slice — SL-7 — to elaboration, and its contract is otherwise derivable from S4, AC-9, TD-9, TD-15 and M-PRESENTATION. Nothing here lowers the readiness bar to widen the horizon (Methodology §10.5), and nothing here is a product, architecture or release decision taken by the development system.

**Correction recorded 2026-09-17 — one row of the table above was wrong, and the systemic finding was right about the set but wrong about SL-6.** The verdict *"**SL-6** Bindings + authority — **C2 advisory**, same external owner — case C, NOT READY"* was repeated unchanged through the replenishment attempts of 2026-09-07, 2026-09-13, 2026-09-15 and 2026-09-16. SL-6's elaboration on 2026-09-17 (§9) established that **C2 was never its gate**: the legacy router calling the endpoint produces router-side recording that belongs to C2 and to SL-11's enforcement, and nothing in SL-6's inspectable outcome requires it. The real blocker was the conversation-authority evidence path, which the Accountable resolved the same day as §8 **Q17**, and **SL-6 is now READY** (§4). **The rows are left exactly as recorded**, because they are the dated evidence of what was believed and they show something worth keeping: a dependency marker is a **claim**, and this one was inherited four times without being tested. What exposed it was not new information from either repository but elaborating the Slice far enough to ask what its acceptance assertion would be measured against. **The systemic finding stands for the rest of the set** — SL-5, SL-8, SL-8b, SL-9, SL-10, SL-11 and SL-13 remain gated on contracts owned by the other team.

**Update — 2026-09-09: §8 Q5 was settled, and the SL-7 row above is superseded.** Every other verdict in the table stands. The human Accountable decided SL-7's Release Scope is **RS-2**, evidenced through the real application run in the operator's process against hosted staging state with a real authenticated advisor session — so the **deployed staging application runtime that the row named as an unowned prerequisite is not required by it** (§4, §7 v1.23). The row was right that nothing owns creating that runtime; it was too strong in holding that RS-2 requires one. Underneath the question sat a **Technical Plan ambiguity**, repaired in **Technical Plan v1.11** before anything was recorded here. SL-7 then passed a full elaboration and readiness pass and is **READY** (§4, §9), which is what the row predicted settling Q5 would return. **The systemic finding is unchanged**: the Slice it returned is ours, and every other path past SL-4 still waits on C1/C2/C6.

**Update — 2026-09-14: the SL-12 half of the table's last row is superseded.** That row called SL-12 far work because its dependency, SL-7, did not yet exist. SL-7 is now Done, and SL-12 has been elaborated and is **READY** (§4, §9). SL-13 still waits on SL-9, and every other verdict stands. **The systemic finding stands as well**, and it is now the whole of what blocks R1 beyond SL-12: C1, C2 and C6 are owned by the Traditional Gu team, and moving them needs a human.

**Current planning draw — Cycle 3.** SL-7 was Cycle 3's only Planned Slice, admitted on 2026-09-13 (§5); like SL-4's admission to Cycle 2, it consumed the one-Slice horizon rather than widening it. It reached evidence-ready on the Cycle's first day, 2026-09-14, and closes with v1.27 (§6). **Cycle 3 then holds no Planned Slice** — only the supervisor repair item SL-7 surfaced, scheduled as its order 2 (§5). **Replenishment has since produced one READY Slice, SL-12** (v1.29), and **the human admitted it to Cycle 3 on 2026-09-14** as order 3, with a confirmed Accountable / DRI (§5). Like SL-7 before it, it consumes the one-Slice horizon rather than widening it. **SL-12 reached evidence-ready on 2026-09-15, the Cycle's second day, and closes with v1.31** (§6). After it, Cycle 3 holds no Planned Slice and there is no READY Slice to admit. What remains in the window is repair work: order 4, confirmed on 2026-09-15 and closed with v1.32; then order 5, which implemented the Accountable's decisions on §8 Q7–Q9 and closes with v1.34 (§5, §6). **Cycle 2's draw, for the record.** Cycle 2 drew SL-2 and SL-3 as Planned work, and **both reached Done** — SL-2 on 2026-09-06 and SL-3 on 2026-09-07 — leaving six days of the window (to 2026-09-13) open with nothing Planned in them. **That gap is now closed: SL-4 was admitted to Cycle 2 by the human Accountable on 2026-09-08** (§5), holding its frozen `3–5 days` / Low. Planned Slices are **not removed from the READY Horizon**: READY is a property of the Slice and it does not lapse on entering a Cycle — but reaching Done does remove it, which is what happened to SL-2 and SL-3 and, when v1.24 merged, to SL-4 (§6). At the time of that decision the horizon was therefore unchanged and remained one Slice: **admitting the only READY Slice to a Cycle consumes the horizon rather than widening it**, and the replenishment finding below is not weakened by having work to do.

**Sequencing, stated precisely.** SL-3 and SL-4 each carried a **case-B dependency on SL-2**, which required satisfaction through SL-2 *delivery* rather than SL-2 merely having started. **SL-2 delivered and closed on 2026-09-06 (§6), so that prerequisite is satisfied for both.** A second “half” SL-3 once stated — the settled `opportunity.closure` fact key and `closure_reason` enums — was a **misassignment**, since SL-2's contract *consumes* rather than settles that vocabulary; by the human ownership decision of 2026-09-06 it is **SL-3's own in-Slice work**, already implicit in its frozen acceptance contract and Definition of Done, and no longer a dependency at all (§4). What SL-2's closure does not do is make SL-3 and SL-4 equivalent: **SL-3 is already Planned** — Cycle 2, with a confirmed Accountable / DRI from the v1.11 decision — so nothing further is owed on the planning side, and its executability now turns on the remaining current facts, principally available capacity. **SL-4 is now Planned as well**: the 2026-09-08 planning decision supplied the two facts it was missing — Cycle inclusion and a confirmed Accountable / DRI (§5). Executability itself is **derived** from those facts together with prerequisite satisfaction and available capacity, and is never maintained as a value in this document (Methodology §12.1, §19.2).

## 2. Shared baseline

Every non-trivial R1 Slice inherits the following, so individual contracts record only their delta. Extracted verbatim in substance from Technical Plan §9 legend, §6 and §8 — neither strengthened nor weakened here.

- **Baseline Definition of Done** (Technical Plan §9 legend): type-check / lint / validators green; module selftests wired into `test:selftests` and green; **flags off ⇒ inert**; correlation-coverage check (applies from SL-2); docs-sync note.
- **Implemented-architecture documentation impact — applies from SL-4 onward, not retroactively.** At Slice closure, record in the Done record (§6) **which claims in documents that own current / implemented architecture truth this Slice invalidated, made incomplete or changed in meaning**. Identify the owning artifact through [`docs/README.md`](../../../README.md). Both outcomes are legitimate: **A — changed** (name the owning artifact, what the implementation invalidated, and where the corrected truth landed) or **B — no material impact** (name the plausible owning artifacts inspected, and why no current claim was invalidated). A Slice is **not** expected to edit architecture documentation; the requirement is an accurate assessment of claim impact, not documentation churn. `docs-sync note` and `no docs changed` do not satisfy it, because neither names an artifact or a claim.

  **Why it is prospective.** This control was added on 2026-09-07 (v1.20) after the architecture documentation coherence audit found canonical current-state documents describing a system that had moved on. **SL-0, SL-1, SL-2 and SL-3 are Done and were never held to it.** Their closure contracts and Done records stand exactly as written; back-filling the field would fabricate evidence of a control that did not exist when they closed. The audit repaired the resulting documentation defects directly, in its own PRs, under its own authority — that repair is complete and is not reopened here.
- **Cross-tenant negative suite** (Technical Plan §8): the two-orgs read-and-write fixture suite is required from SL-0 and **gates every multi-seat surface (SL-7+)**. Landed at SL-0 as `npm run test:rls` with its own CI job.
- **Security / tenancy invariants** (Technical Plan §6): membership-EXISTS RLS plus restrictive org-tenancy guards; `is_active_org_member` hardening; explicit `organizationId` on every service-role helper; Case-child tenancy **derived from the parent Case**; `authorizeOrgAction` on every server-route mutation; authorization resolved **before** any model context or ranking; opaque external ids only; gateway checks the org external binding **before every legacy read**.
- **Shadow-stage constraint** (Technical Plan §5): for SL-0…SL-8b there are **no prospect-facing effects**; supervisor decisions are logged and compared only.
- **Compatibility**: additive-only migrations; rollback for every table = flag off ⇒ rows inert audit data.
- **Evidence discipline** (Methodology §14): an agent assertion does not close a Slice; the declared Release Scope's evidence does.

Migration numbers are **never pre-reserved** — Technical Plan §3 defines symbolic units claimed in landing order at implementation time. Slice contracts therefore name the symbolic unit, never a number.
