### SL-6 — Bindings + authority (advisory)

**Elaborated 2026-09-17, after the Accountable's resolution of §8 Q17 settled the conversation-authority evidence path.** The elaboration ran in two passes on the same day: the first established that the Slice's recorded gate named the wrong contract and that its measurement question and its evidence-path question were the same question (§9, the dated NOT READY record, which is kept); the second is this contract, authored once Q17 answered it.

- **Type:** behavior
- **Stage:** **shadow.** The §2 shadow-stage constraint applies in full: no prospect-facing effect is reachable from anything here.
- **Inspectable outcome / value:** For a pilot conversation, Gu OS can answer **who currently holds the conversation** — Gu, or a human who has taken over — and that answer is **provably the same verdict Traditional Gu's own behavior reaches**, computed independently from the same observed legacy reality. Where the answer is unknown or the evidence conflicts, Gu OS says so durably instead of guessing, and the unresolved authority **surfaces to the advisor** through the Work Portfolio path that has been waiting for a producer since SL-7. Nothing obeys the answer yet: no legacy reply is suppressed, no authority moves, and no effect is gated on it. What the Slice buys is the ability to be **wrong out loud and cheaply** before SL-11 makes the same answer load-bearing.

- **Governing behavior / traceability:**
  - **ADR-107** (Accepted direction) — the four authority concepts kept distinct; `LEGACY | GU_OS` identifies **decision authority, not transport**; Decision §4 *"if conflicting authority is detected, fail safe by suppressing the new external effect and surfacing/reconciling the conflict"*; Decision §5, human takeover sets `human_active`, Gu stops speaking, and it *"does not automatically pause the Case or transfer durable responsibility"*.
  - **Architecture AC-4** §9 — §9.1's four dimensions, which *"can differ at the same time"*; §9.2, *"Case existence does not transfer runtime authority"* and the fail-safe on concurrent autonomous authority; §9.3's **"Pause speaking ≠ pause thinking ≠ pause the Case"**; §9.7, the external conversation binding is generic and source-referenced, legacy `lead_id` *"is not the Gu OS conversation identity"*, and conversation authority *"should not be collapsed into"* the binding where lifecycle and audit semantics differ.
  - **Architecture AC-4 §9.5, AC-2 §7.8 and AC-1 §6.5 — the freshness requirement, which is why Q17's answer is an alignment rather than a departure.** AC-4 §9.5 and AC-2 §7.8 require prospect-facing effects to *"deterministically revalidate immediately before execution"* the current runtime authority, the current conversation authority, policy and *"any materially relevant fresh state"*; AC-1 §6.5 already says consequential decisions *"should re-read the current authoritative operational state through the bounded capability layer before acting."* **The approved architecture therefore already required the read.** TD-3's ingestion-only wording was the narrower statement, and Q17 reconciles the two.
  - **S2** [`situational-progression-next-work-human-authority.md`](../../operating-domains/relationship-operations/specs/situational-progression-next-work-human-authority.md):
    - **AC-05** — runtime authority `LEGACY` with a Case in existence: *"Gu OS does not independently decide/send"*;
    - **AC-07** — advisor intervenes in the same conversation: Gu speaking suppresses, *"supporting work may continue"*;
    - **AC-09 / EC-03** — the pre-effect check blocks or reconsiders a stale send, and must not *"rely only on authority state from planning time"*;
    - **EC-04** — runtime authority remains Legacy while a Gu OS Case exists: shadow and observe, and must not *"treat Case existence as cutover"*;
    - **EC-12** — where an advisor acts off-platform and contents cannot be observed, use known signals and conservative reconciliation; must not *"assert no human interaction occurred"*;
    - **AC-29** — a takeover with unrelated internal reconciliation: non-conflicting internal work may continue;
    - **EC-09** — legacy sources that disagree: *"preserve uncertainty/provenance; reconcile"*, never *"generic last-read/last-write truth"*. This is what forbids last-write-wins between the two Q17 paths.
  - **Technical Plan TD-3 and TD-4** (v1.25) — the two representations, the resolver, the TD-4 binding with `thread_kind` and the `advisor_wa` null-authority CHECK, and TD-3's Q17 section: the two complementary evidence paths with separated roles, the `legacy_conversation_authority_get` capability, the no-silent-fallback and no-last-write-wins rules, and the `bootstrap_direct` → `legacy_read_api` handover condition.
  - **Legacy reality**, [`legacy-source-audit.md`](legacy-source-audit.md) §8, §8.3, §9.1.1 and §24.6 — echo-driven `bypass_bot` with `last_owner_interaction_wba`; the sweep that clears it after five minutes, which §8.3 records as *"an implementation/UX approximation, not an R1 policy invariant"*; the separate per-Gu-number kill switch; and §9.1.1's rule that `advisor_wa` captures *"do not set `bypass_bot`"*, *"must not automatically suppress Gu speaking"*, and that any authority effect from observed advisor activity *"requires explicit, ADR-107-conformant policy plus current authority resolution in Gu OS."*
  - **SL-7 hand-off (2026-09-13)** — wire unknown or conflicting authority into SL-7's authority-conflict must-surface rule and prove it live. The consuming contract already exists and is inert: `authorityConflict()` (`must-surface.ts:428`), `PortfolioAuthorityConflict` requiring `resolution_id`, `state` of `unknown | conflicting`, and `detected_at` (`snapshot.ts:133`), the `authority_resolution` `DurableRef` marked *"No producer before SL-6"* (`human-interaction.ts:52`), and live assembly hard-wired to `null` (`snapshot.ts:352`).

- **In scope.**
  - `external_conversation_bindings` (TD-4) with the TD-3 conversation-authority fields, the partial-unique active binding, and the **CHECK enforcing null authority columns on `thread_kind='advisor_wa'`**.
  - `resolveInteractionAuthority(org, externalRefs)` (TD-3), with the fail-safe semantics and the four-concept separation.
  - `legacy_conversation_authority_get` — one **bounded semantic** gateway capability over the named authority inputs, on the `bootstrap_direct` adapter, with allowlist entry, source contract, fixtures and drift coverage like the existing four.
  - **Durable `authority_resolution` records**, and the snapshot assembly that replaces `authority_conflict: null` with a real producer.
  - The **advisory equivalence harness**: the independent source-audited oracle, the comparison, and its divergence record.
  - `POST /api/legacy/authority`, log-only, authenticated per ADR-111 — **shared enabling work with SL-5**, listed here because SL-6 arrives first. It brings the `apps/web/src/app/api/legacy/` tree, the TD-13 verifier and the raw-body-preserving middleware that audit §24.6 records as existing nowhere in either repository.

- **Out of scope, explicitly.** Enforcement of any kind — no legacy reply is suppressed and `runtime_authority` is written by nothing, which stays SL-11's with its own governed decision. **Fabricating a case event with `payload.kind = "authority_changed"` from an advisory resolution** — that discriminator belongs to an actual governed authority transition (TD-11), not to SL-6's persist-and-surface path. SL-6 writes neither `runtime_authority` nor another authoritative transition. The first sequenced writer of `runtime_authority` is SL-11; that stub lists `runtime_authority=gu_os`, legacy suppression and takeover suppress/resume, and **does not yet enumerate** `authority_changed`. Ownership of the event is therefore **deferred to the first governed `runtime_authority` transition** and is not invented as an SL-11 acceptance item here. Any `advisor_wa`-derived suppression. Conflating the per-lead takeover flag with the per-number kill switch. Treating Case existence as authority. A generic legacy-state capability. Making legacy's five-minute window a Gu OS invariant. Reconciling the two evidence paths by recency. C1 or C2 implementation on the Traditional Gu side.

**Slice Acceptance Contract.**

| # | Assertion | Governing source | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| SA-6.1 | An `external_conversation_bindings` row binds Organization, Case, contact, provider and an **opaque** external conversation ref, with at most one active binding per (case, provider, ref). Legacy `lead_id` is a reference, never the Gu OS conversation identity | AC-4 §9.7; TD-4 | migration + selftest |
| SA-6.2 | Conversation-authority columns are **null by CHECK** on every `thread_kind='advisor_wa'` row, and the resolver ignores advisor threads for authority. An `advisor_wa` observation cannot mint authority through any path | audit §9.1.1; TD-4 | migration CHECK + rejected-insert test + resolver selftest |
| SA-6.3 | The resolver answers from **current** legacy reality through `legacy_conversation_authority_get`, not from a projection, whenever current conversation authority matters | AC-1 §6.5; AC-4 §9.5; TD-3 Q17 | selftest + provenance assertion on the answer |
| SA-6.4 | The capability exposes **only** per-lead takeover, `last_owner_interaction_wba`, the distinct per-number kill switch, provenance and freshness. No raw Firestore or Mongo document reaches a caller | TD-3 Q17 | type contract + selftest + allowlist test |
| SA-6.5 | The per-lead takeover flag and the per-Gu-number kill switch are **distinct fields with distinct meanings** throughout, and neither is read as the other | audit §24.6; TD-3 | selftest over all four combinations |
| SA-6.6 | On read failure, ambiguity or materially conflicting evidence, the resolver returns unknown or conflicting and **never** a confident answer, and **never** silently falls back to a stale projection. **Accountable-ratified clarification (2026-09-18):** uncertainty is **dimension-specific**. A failure to obtain one authority dimension makes that dimension unresolved; it does not erase a separately and successfully observed authority dimension. Specifically, a runtime-authority read failure may leave `runtimeAuthority = null` and `runtimeAuthorityReadFailed = true` while a successfully observed current conversation authority may still be `gu` or `human_active`. No confident value may be invented for the failed dimension. Any later governed decision that requires multiple authority dimensions must still fail safe if any required dimension remains unresolved. ADR-107's four distinct authority concepts are preserved and can differ at the same time (AC-4 §9.1) | ADR-107 §1, §4; AC-4 §9.1; TD-3 Q17 | selftest, including an induced read failure |
| SA-6.7 | Every unknown or conflicting resolution whose Organization **and** applicable legacy source scope are established server-side is **persisted** as an `authority_resolution` record carrying resolution identity, state, `detected_at` and provenance. Returning C2 data and recording an internal fail-safe are distinct operations, but both require that owner/source scope. An active Lead→Case→Organization binding does **not** prove ADR-111 `legacy_owner_ref` / `ownerRefs` scope. Repository truth stores no durable Traditional Gu owner UID on the identity or conversation binding; the only owner-scope evidence is the current server-observed source owner. If that scope cannot be established: return 403, record a security/operational audit, do not return C2 authority data, and do not create a Case-scoped `authority_resolution` for that Lead. Persist is not authorized when the observed owner is outside the key's scope. No out-of-scope legacy data is disclosed. Logging alone fails this assertion when persist is authorized | SL-7 hand-off; ADR-111 §6; `snapshot.ts:133` | selftest + DB assertion |
| SA-6.8 | That record reaches the advisor through SL-7's existing `authority_conflict` must-surface path, which is no longer hard-wired to null | SL-7 hand-off; `must-surface.ts:428` | portfolio selftest + hosted evidence |
| SA-6.9 | For pilot conversations, the resolver's `human_active` verdict **matches the independent oracle's** verdict computed from the same observed legacy inputs. The oracle reuses **neither** the resolver **nor** its normalization or decision function | §8 Q17; TD-3 Q17 | equivalence run over pilot conversations + a structural test that the two share no decision code |
| SA-6.10 | A divergence is recorded with both verdicts, the observed inputs and provenance, and **changes no business truth** | EC-09; §2 shadow | selftest + divergence record inspection |
| SA-6.11 | The answer **pauses nothing**: no Case is paused, no durable responsibility moves, no `runtime_authority` is written, and no legacy reply is suppressed | AC-4 §9.3; ADR-107 §5; EC-04 | selftest + `gu_os`-writer absence test + hosted containment check |
| SA-6.12 | `POST /api/legacy/authority` accepts only requests valid under ADR-111 — rejecting a bad signature, a stale timestamp, a re-serialized body and a mismatched key binding — and resolves the Organization **server-side from the key**, never from a payload claim | ADR-111 §2, §7 | route selftest against the ratified fixture |
| SA-6.13 | Authority reads and resolutions are Organization-scoped, and the cross-tenant suite proves no cross-Organization read or record is reachable | AC-4 §9.1; SL-0 | `npm run test:rls` |
| SA-6.14 | Legacy's five-minute window appears **only** inside the oracle as an observation of legacy behavior, and no Gu OS product or architecture path depends on the number | audit §8.3; §8 Q17 | grep-style structural selftest, as `admission.selftest.ts:1564` already does for `bypass_bot` |

**Definition of Done.** All fourteen assertions hold; `npm run type-check`, `npm run lint`, `npm run test:selftests` and `npm run test:rls` pass; the RS-2 evidence below is produced and recorded; and the equivalence run over pilot conversations shows **either agreement or recorded, explained divergence** — a divergence is a legitimate outcome of an advisory Slice and not automatically a defect, but an **unexplained** one is.

**Release Scope: RS-2 hosted.** Derivable rather than open, which is what changed since the first pass: the Slice sits in the shadow stage with no prospect-facing effect, and the precedent is SL-1, SL-2, SL-3 and SL-7 — the application in the operator's process against hosted staging, with real pilot Organization credentials. **Minimum evidence is four distinct conditions; none substitutes for another:**

1. An equivalence run over **named real pilot conversations**, using real legacy reads and their provenance.
2. **Meaningful pilot variation**, including a real same-thread takeover / `human_active` state if one is available in the pilot set, so an all-agreement result is not presented as stronger evidence than it is.
3. At least one **real** `unknown` or `conflicting` authority result that produces a durable `authority_resolution` and is surfaced through the existing Portfolio `authority_conflict` path. A normal real takeover is **not** this condition.
4. Hosted containment proving **no write reached Traditional Gu** and **no `runtime_authority` changed**.

Traditional Gu customer state is **not** manufactured, seeded or synthetically mutated to satisfy any of these. **Explicit non-claims:** it does not claim enforcement works, that the legacy router can call the endpoint, that C2 is live, or that the verdict is correct for conversations outside the pilot set.

**T7 binding-path finding (2026-09-18) — implementation gap, not an environment prerequisite. T7 is not started.** This Slice creates `external_conversation_bindings` and the governed helper `attachExternalConversationBinding`. No non-test runtime call site attaches a binding. Admission writes Case `context.legacy_lead_id` and an admission fact carrying the same opaque ref; it creates **no Contact, no `legacy_lead` identity binding, and no conversation binding**. `createContact` and `attachExternalIdentityBinding` have no non-test runtime caller. The SL-1/SL-2 pre-read gate permits unbound leads (ownership containment, not a lead binding). The helper requires `organizationId`, `caseId`, `contactId`, provider and an opaque ref. An already-admitted pilot Case therefore cannot feed the helper without asserting a Contact. No hosted procedure that only calls the helper against admitted Case/contact/conversation state is currently legitimate, because the contact does not exist. Do not use an ad-hoc SQL insert. Do not seed or modify Traditional Gu customer state. **A–D revalidation (v1.56) established that Contact materialization is not already governed inside SL-6 / TD-4**; that was §8 Q18, not an automatic C1 / SL-5 dependency. **Q18 is RESOLVED (v1.57) as alternative 3:** SL-15 repairs the omitted TD-8 admission seam and backfills already-admitted real Cases with the same primitive, then `attachExternalConversationBinding()`. C1 / SL-5 remains the eventual normal producer for **new** inbound conversation transitions. **SL-15 is Planned in Cycle 3 as order 8** (§5). T7 stays **not started** until SL-15 reaches its Done boundary; after that, T7's other hosted evidence prerequisites are reassessed rather than assumed executable. Those other prerequisites remain distinct.

**Risk assessment.**

| Dimension | Assessment |
|---|---|
| External effects | **None.** Shadow stage, no effect capability, and reads are read-only against legacy |
| Authority | The central risk, and the Slice is built so the answer is inert: nothing obeys it. SA-6.11 tests that directly |
| Tenancy | Organization-scoped reads and records; the cross-tenant suite gates it (SA-6.13) |
| Data | **New authority-bearing data in the gateway's read surface** — the one genuine widening. Bounded by SA-6.4's semantic contract, and by the Q17 condition that `bootstrap_direct` must give way to a least-privilege legacy-side implementation before the read is load-bearing for effects |
| Credentials | Reuses SL-1's `organization_tool_secrets` path; no new secret class. The ADR-111 key is **not** provisioned here — §8 Q16 |
| Fail-safe correctness | Fails closed by construction: unknown and conflicting are answers, not exceptions (SA-6.6) |
| Legacy coupling | Reproducing legacy's window inside the oracle risks becoming a Gu OS invariant; SA-6.14 is the structural guard |
| Cross-repo | The endpoint is built and proven against the ratified fixture, so nothing depends on the other team's schedule |

**Estimate: `4–6 days`, confidence Medium.** Drivers named. Above SL-7's range because it carries **three first-times**: the first authority-bearing gateway capability, the first `apps/web/src/app/api/legacy/` route with the TD-13 verifier and raw-body middleware that exist nowhere yet, and the first equivalence harness with an independently authored oracle. Held off High because the consuming contracts already exist and are typed — SL-7's rule, the `DurableRef`, the `PortfolioAuthorityConflict` shape and the copy keys are all in place — and because the gateway gives four worked capability precedents. The three-Slice calibration dataset's consistent over-estimation (§1) is a reason to read the lower bound as likelier. **Frozen at READY.**

- **Dependencies.** **SL-2 satisfied** (Done) — admission creates the relationship Cases. **Correction recorded 2026-09-18:** the earlier wording that admission also creates their `external_identity_bindings` was false. Admission records `context.legacy_lead_id` and admission facts only; it creates no Contact and no `legacy_lead` identity binding. That omitted TD-8 seam is **§8 Q18 / SL-15** and does not reopen SL-2's Done record. **SL-7 satisfied** (Done) — the consuming must-surface rule exists. **SL-1 satisfied** (Done) — the gateway, its adapters, allowlist, provenance envelope and drift harness are the pattern the new capability extends. **§8 Q17 resolved** — the evidence path is settled, which was the blocker. **§8 Q18 resolved 2026-09-18 as alternative 3** — SL-15 owns the Contact / `legacy_lead` materialization path. **T7 waits on SL-15**, not on C1. **No `[L:Cn]` cross-repo contract gates this Slice**, which is the correction Q17 carried: C2 advisory remains required for router-side recording and for SL-11's enforcement, and is **not** a prerequisite here.
- **Readiness: READY** — see §9.
- **Planned 2026-09-17** — admitted to **Cycle 3 (2026-09-14 → 2026-09-20) as order 7** with **Alejandro Torres Padilla** as the confirmed Accountable / DRI (§5), effective on the merge of this record. **Executable**: prerequisites actually satisfied, capacity free. **v1.51 recorded that the implementation branch would follow the merge**; that calendar order did not occur (v1.52 / §5).

### SL-15 — Contact / legacy-lead identity seam (repair)

**Created 2026-09-18 by the Accountable's resolution of §8 Q18 (alternative 3), and elaborated the same day.**

Q18 asked who may assert a canonical Contact for an already-admitted real Lead Opportunity. The Accountable chose **alternative 3**: repair the omitted TD-8 `contact/legacy-lead dedup via bindings` seam as a **new bounded R1 repair Slice**, not by absorbing it into SL-6 and not by assigning C1. The next valid Slice identity from repository truth is **SL-15** (SL-0…SL-14 are taken).

This is Methodology **§15.2 case B** (pre-existing omission discovered incidentally) and §15 *missing/misordered work* owned by Technical Plan TD-8. **SL-2's historical Done record is not rewritten** (§6). The frozen SL-2 contract did not detect the omitted seam; that is recorded here as the reason a repair Slice exists.

Everything else here is derived from the governing sources and the Accountable's identity rule; nothing else is invented.

- **This Slice is READY** — see *Readiness* and §9.
- **Planned 2026-09-18** — admitted to **Cycle 3 (2026-09-14 → 2026-09-20) as order 8** with **Alejandro Torres Padilla** as the confirmed Accountable / DRI (§5), effective on the merge of this record. **Executable**: prerequisites actually satisfied, capacity available. Implementation follows this record's merge.

- **Type:** enabling capability (repair)
- **Stage:** **shadow.** The §2 shadow-stage constraint applies in full: no prospect-facing effect is reachable.
- **Inspectable outcome / value:** For an Organization-scoped real Legacy Lead that has been governed-admitted, Gu OS **idempotently resolves an existing Contact or establishes one provisional Contact anchor** through the typed opaque `legacy_lead` external binding, with provenance, no cross-lead identity inference and no Traditional Gu write.

- **Classification / owning artifact:**
  - TD-8 specified admission as: model interpreter → structured proposal → **deterministic executor** including **"contact/legacy-lead dedup via bindings"**.
  - Realized SL-2 writes Case `context.legacy_lead_id` and admission facts only. It creates no Contact and no `legacy_lead` identity binding. `createContact` and `attachExternalIdentityBinding` have no non-test runtime caller.
  - SL-6 owns conversation-authority advisory resolution and `attachExternalConversationBinding()`. It does **not** own Contact identity.
  - C1 / SL-5 may remain the normal eventual producer of **new** inbound conversation events/bindings. They **consume** governed Contact identity; they do not mint it. **Q17 is not reopened.** C1 is not an SL-6 advisory prerequisite.

- **R1 Contact materialization rule** — Accountable, 2026-09-18; binding on this Slice:

  For a **real** Legacy Lead that has passed governed admission into a Lead Opportunity:

  1. Treat the opaque Traditional Gu `legacy_lead` id as a **source-scoped external identity only**.
  2. **Never parse** `lead_id` to infer person identity, phone identity, owner, conversation identity or cross-lead equivalence.
  3. Within the resolved Organization, first resolve `traditional_gu` + `binding_kind = legacy_lead` + the opaque external lead id.
  4. If that binding already resolves to a canonical Contact, **reuse** that Contact.
  5. If no binding exists, admission is permitted to create a **minimal provisional Contact** and bind that opaque `legacy_lead` identity to it. This assertion means only: *"For this Organization, this admitted real Legacy Lead has a provisional Contact anchor."* It does **not** mean that another legacy lead belongs to the same person; that phone/name similarity proves identity; that this Contact has been globally deduplicated; or that the opaque lead id itself is the person identity.
  6. Do **not** automatically merge or deduplicate two different legacy lead ids into one Contact without stronger governed identity evidence.
  7. Preserve provenance on the external identity binding: source system; opaque legacy lead ref; admission / source evidence basis; provisional-materialization basis; Organization.
  8. If an existing binding is incompatible, ambiguous or cross-Organization, **fail closed** rather than creating another Contact or silently rebinding it.

- **Atomicity / idempotency.** Do not implement create-Contact-then-separately-attach-binding if a crash can leave an orphan Contact. Use the smallest database-owned atomic resolve-or-create mechanism consistent with `bootstrapOrganizationFromLegacyKey` / `bootstrap_organization`: a SQL function that inserts Contact + binding in one transaction and, on unique violation, rolls back and converges on the existing typed `legacy_lead` identity. Repository-truth name: `resolveOrCreateContactForLegacyLead(...)` wrapping `resolve_or_create_contact_for_legacy_lead(...)`. Service-role only.

- **Future admission path.** The TD-8 admission executor applies this identity rule as part of the existing deterministic materialisation of an **admitted** real Lead. Unadmitted / deferred / inert paths create no Contact. C1 is not responsible for minting canonical Contact identity.

- **Existing admitted Cases / T7 backfill.** The **same** governed identity rule applies to already-admitted real pilot Cases whose `context.legacy_lead_id` / admission provenance identifies the real admitted Legacy Lead. This is a bounded backfill/repair, not an ad-hoc T7 exception. For such a Case: admitted real Legacy Lead → resolve/create provisional Contact under the rule → typed `legacy_lead` identity binding → `attachExternalConversationBinding()` (no ad-hoc SQL) → SL-6's authority path can then be exercised. Fail closed if the opaque lead id maps to more than one admitted Case in the Organization, or if Case Organization does not match. Do not modify, seed or fabricate Traditional Gu customer state.

- **Governing behavior / traceability:**
  - **§8 Q18** (this version) — alternative 3 and the identity rule above.
  - **S1** [`lead-opportunity-lifecycle.md`](../../operating-domains/relationship-operations/specs/lead-opportunity-lifecycle.md) §6.1 (sufficient identity or allowed provisional identity under policy); §8.5.2 (one contact, multiple Opportunities); EC-04 (resolve identity before creating another Opportunity).
  - **ADR-106** — Prospect/Contact, Legacy Lead and Lead Opportunity remain distinct; `lead_id` is an opaque external Legacy Lead/context identifier; legacy identifiers are bridged, not promoted.
  - **Architecture AC-4** §9.7 — legacy `lead_id` is not the Gu OS conversation identity.
  - **Technical Plan TD-1** — contacts have no legacy-lead mirror fields; one contact ↔ n `legacy_lead` bindings ↔ n Opportunities; `legacy_lead` is `global_routing`.
  - **Technical Plan TD-8** — admission's deterministic executor includes contact/legacy-lead dedup via bindings.
  - **Technical Plan TD-4** — conversation binding requires Organization, Case, contact, provider and an opaque ref; historical backfill calls the existing helper.
  - **Legacy audit** §5.1 — `lead_id` is a composite operational-context key; persist opaquely; do not parse.

- **In scope.**
  - The atomic resolve-or-create primitive and its TypeScript wrapper.
  - Future admission integration on the admitted materialisation path only.
  - Deterministic idempotency / concurrency tests; cross-Organization negatives; incompatible-binding fail-closed behavior.
  - Historical/pilot backfill using the same primitive, then `attachExternalConversationBinding()`.
  - RLS / service-role EXECUTE restriction matching `bootstrap_organization`.

- **Out of scope, explicitly.** A general Contact merge / master-data subsystem. Implicit cross-lead person deduplication. Parsing `lead_id`. C1 minting Contact identity. Reopening Q17 or assigning C1 as an SL-6 advisory prerequisite. Starting T7. Reopening or rewriting SL-2's Done record. Provisioning a real TD-13 key. Any prospect-facing effect. Enforcement (still SL-11). Changing Q11 or Q16.

**Slice Acceptance Contract.**

| # | Assertion | Governing source | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| SA-15.1 | The opaque `legacy_lead` id is compared whole. No path parses it into person, phone, owner, conversation identity or cross-lead equivalence | ADR-106; audit §5.1; Q18 rule 1–2 | structural selftest |
| SA-15.2 | Within the resolved Organization, `traditional_gu` + `legacy_lead` + opaque id that already points at a Contact is **reused**; no second Contact is created | Q18 rule 3–4; TD-1 | selftest |
| SA-15.3 | Absent a binding, exactly one minimal provisional Contact is created and bound. The assertion is only that this Organization's admitted real Legacy Lead has a provisional Contact anchor | Q18 rule 5; S1 §6.1 | selftest |
| SA-15.4 | Two different opaque legacy lead ids are never auto-merged or treated as the same person | Q18 rule 6 | selftest |
| SA-15.5 | The identity binding carries provenance: source system, opaque lead ref, admission/source evidence basis, provisional-materialization basis, Organization — written in the same operation as the binding | Q18 rule 7; `bootstrap_organization` precedent | selftest |
| SA-15.6 | An incompatible, ambiguous or cross-Organization existing binding **fails closed**: no second Contact, no silent rebind | Q18 rule 8; ADR-106 | selftest + `npm run test:rls` |
| SA-15.7 | Resolve-or-create is database-atomic. A crash cannot leave an orphan Contact. Concurrent and retried calls converge on the existing typed `legacy_lead` identity | Q18 atomicity; `bootstrap_organization` | SQL unique-violation + concurrency selftest |
| SA-15.8 | Future **admitted** real Leads apply this rule inside the existing deterministic admission executor. Unadmitted / deferred / inert paths create no Contact. C1 does not mint Contact identity | TD-8; Q18 | admission selftest |
| SA-15.9 | Historical/pilot backfill of already-admitted real Cases uses the **same** primitive, then `attachExternalConversationBinding()`. No ad-hoc SQL. Ambiguous Case mapping fails closed | Q18 backfill; TD-4 | selftest + hosted |
| SA-15.10 | No Traditional Gu write and no prospect-facing effect | §2 shadow | structural + hosted containment |
| SA-15.11 | Organization-scoped. Cross-tenant suite proves no cross-Organization Contact or binding is reachable | SL-0; TD-1 | `npm run test:rls` |

**Definition of Done.** All eleven assertions hold; `npm run type-check`, `npm run lint`, `npm run test:selftests` and `npm run test:rls` pass; the RS-2 evidence below is produced and recorded. SL-2's Done record is unchanged.

**Release Scope: RS-2 hosted.** Derived: the inspectable outcome is a real governed-admitted Legacy Lead; SL-2's admission evidence was RS-2; Methodology §14.2 treats Organization-scoped identity writes against hosted staging as hosted evidence. **Minimum evidence:**

1. Deterministic proof of SA-15.1–SA-15.8, SA-15.10 and SA-15.11, including concurrency convergence and fail-closed negatives.
2. One hosted backfill of an **already-admitted real pilot Case** producing a Contact, a typed `legacy_lead` identity binding, and a conversation binding via `attachExternalConversationBinding()`, with provenance.
3. Hosted containment: **no write reached Traditional Gu**.

**Explicit non-claims:** it does not claim person-level deduplication; that C1 is live; that T7 has run; that SL-2 is reopened; or that conversation bindings for *new* inbound traffic are produced here.

**Risk assessment.**

| Dimension | Assessment |
|---|---|
| External effects | **None.** Shadow; Gu OS-side identity only |
| Authority | Creates no runtime or conversation authority. A binding is never an authority grant (TD-1) |
| Tenancy | Organization-scoped; `legacy_lead` is `global_routing`; cross-Organization fail-closed (SA-15.6, SA-15.11) |
| Data | First runtime producer of `contacts` and `legacy_lead` identity bindings. Bounded by the provisional-anchor assertion and by atomicity |
| Identity | The central risk, and the Slice exists to keep it narrow: one opaque lead → one provisional Contact; no parse; no merge |
| Credentials | None new. No ADR-111 key — §8 Q16 |
| Fail-safe | Incompatible / ambiguous / cross-Organization bindings fail closed |
| Legacy coupling | Zero Traditional Gu writes |
| Cross-repo | None. C1 is not assigned |

**Estimate: `1–2 days`, confidence Medium.** Frozen at READY. The `bootstrap_organization` precedent and the existing helper make the primitive ordinary; Medium rather than High because fail-closed binding shapes and admission crash-safety must be gotten right, and hosted backfill needs a real admitted Case. The estimate is a prediction, never a pace (Methodology §10.4).

- **Dependencies.** **SL-0 satisfied** (Done) — `contacts`, `external_identity_bindings`, `bootstrap_organization`. **SL-2 satisfied** (Done) — admission and admitted Cases exist; this Slice repairs the omitted seam without reopening that Done record. **`attachExternalConversationBinding` exists on `main`** (SL-6 T1–T6, merge `7a71351`) — required only for historical backfill, not for the identity primitive. **§8 Q18 resolved.** **No `[L:Cn]` contract.** Independent of SL-5 / C1 for Contact minting.
- **Readiness: READY** — see §9.
- **Planned 2026-09-18** — Cycle 3 order 8; Accountable / DRI Alejandro Torres Padilla (§5). **Executable.** Implementation follows this record's merge.

## 5. Execution register (transitional)

Present only while no Development Control Plane exists, and deliberately minimal. It records the Cycle, the confirmed Accountable / DRI, the frozen estimate and the actual metrics **after** execution.

**This section is not authority for live state, and carries no execution stage.** The agent runtime owns just-in-time Tasks and pre-PR implementation and local verification; GitHub owns branch, commits, PR, CI results, merge state, Actions and environment approvals. Do not edit this document to move a Slice through `Proposed → Planned → Implementing → Local Verify → PR / CI`.

A Slice enters this register when the planning facts it records actually exist, not before. SL-1's Execution Cycle and confirmed Accountable / DRI were filled in by the **Cycle 1 planning decision of 2026-09-03**; its actuals were filled in at Done. Nothing else about SL-1 belongs here: the durable contract in §4 remains the single owner of its scope, acceptance and readiness, and the execution *stage* is never transcribed here (Methodology §19.2). **A Slice does not enter this register merely by becoming READY.** The register receives a row only when the planning and execution facts it owns actually exist — Cycle membership, a confirmed Accountable / DRI, a frozen estimate carried into planning, and actuals after Done. **Absence of a row is therefore not evidence that a Slice is NOT READY**: readiness is owned by the Slice contract in §4 and its evaluation in §9, while Cycle and Accountable facts appear here only after planning. **SL-4's row was opened by the planning decision of 2026-09-08**, which supplied exactly the two facts that were missing — Cycle membership and a confirmed Accountable / DRI — alongside the estimate frozen at READY on 2026-09-05. Its actuals were filled in at Done (v1.24) rather than maintained as live state, including the Release Scope column's *required* half, which records what the evidence turned out to demand, not what was declared. SL-0, SL-1, SL-2, SL-3 and SL-4 are completed historical rows. **SL-7's row was opened by the planning decision of 2026-09-13**, which supplied the two facts it lacked — Cycle 3 membership and a confirmed Accountable / DRI — alongside the estimate frozen at READY on 2026-09-09; its actuals were filled in at Done (v1.27), and SL-7 is now a completed historical row as well. **SL-12's row was opened by the planning decision of 2026-09-14**, which supplied Cycle 3 membership and a confirmed Accountable / DRI, alongside the estimate frozen at READY the same day. Its actuals were filled in at Done (v1.31). **SL-15's row was opened by the planning decision of 2026-09-18**, which supplied Cycle 3 order-8 membership and a confirmed Accountable / DRI, alongside the estimate frozen at READY the same day.

**Cycle 3 — sequenced plan.** Window **2026-09-14 → 2026-09-20**, Monday to Sunday under this plan's cadence from Cycle 3, confirmed by the human Accountable on 2026-09-13. This planning decision confirms Cycle inclusion and the Accountable / DRI; it re-approves no product behavior, architecture, Slice scope or code (Methodology §12.1).

**Sequencing of orders 7 and 8, stated precisely.** SL-6 remains Planned as Cycle 3 order 7. That earlier planning history is not rewritten. T1–T6 of SL-6 are already delivered on main. T7 remains unstarted and is now blocked on SL-15. **SL-15 may therefore execute before the remaining T7 portion of SL-6.** "Order 8" records when SL-15 entered Cycle planning and must not be misread as requiring completion of all remaining order-7 work first.

**One thing the planning weighed, stated plainly.** SL-7 is the first advisor-facing surface and the first run of the application against staging. Its RS-2 run waits on three human boundaries — the `staging` environment approval for M-PRESENTATION, authorization of the controlled seed, and the advisor's own sign-in — each recorded as human/environment wait, not engineering time (Methodology §17.1).

**Cycle 3 close-out — SL-7 (recorded with its Done record, v1.27).** SL-7 reached evidence-ready on 2026-09-14 at 15:01:35 (−06:00), the Cycle's first day. Under the rule above, it closes inside Cycle 3 if its Done record merges by the end of Sunday 2026-09-20 (−06:00), and the register's re-planning count is **0** on that condition; a later merge is a re-planning event, recorded here with the actual facts in the same merge. The three human boundaries the planning weighed are what calendar time went to, as it predicted: the `staging` approval took 35 m 20 s, and the advisor-session boundary ≤ 4 h 16 m 41 s (§6). **After SL-7, Cycle 3 holds no Planned Slice and has no READY Slice to admit** (§1). **It does hold order 2**: the supervisor repair SL-7 surfaced, scheduled explicitly under §15.2 case B, as the Accountable instructed on 2026-09-14 when accepting this record. A repair item is not a Slice, so it widens neither the READY Horizon nor any Slice's scope. It had no prerequisite outside the development system and needed no hosted run (§6), so it was the next executable work. **It closed on 2026-09-14**, when the Accountable ratified its eval bars over the evidence recorded in §6. Readiness replenishment, starting with SL-12, proceeds alongside it. If replenishment produces a READY Slice, admitting it to the rest of the window, or to a Cycle 4, is a planning proposal for the human to confirm, as SL-4's admission to Cycle 2 was.

**Cycle 3 close-out — SL-12 (recorded with its Done record, v1.31).** SL-12 reached evidence-ready on 2026-09-15 at 10:57:50 (−06:00), the Cycle's second day. Under the rule above, it closes inside Cycle 3 if v1.31 merges by the end of Sunday 2026-09-20 (−06:00), and the register's re-planning count is **0** on that condition. A later merge is a re-planning event, recorded here with the actual facts in the same merge. The planning decision named the risks, and both played out:

- **Eval convergence.** It took five measurement rounds and three frozen holdouts, and the Accountable decided the path once, under option A (§6).
- **The advisor's session.** It took one sign-in and two authorizations.

Calendar time outside the working windows went mostly to an agent usage-limit interruption overnight, recorded separately in §6 because it is neither engineering nor a decision boundary.

**One thing the planning weighs, stated plainly.** SL-6's hosted evidence needs **real pilot-conversation legacy state**, which is neither synthetic nor seedable. The four RS-2 conditions in §4 are **distinct**: a named-conversation equivalence run; meaningful variation including a real same-thread takeover / `human_active` if one exists; a real `unknown` or `conflicting` result that persists and surfaces through Portfolio; and hosted containment. **A normal real takeover is not the unknown/conflicting condition.** Reads are read-only and Organization-scoped. Traditional Gu customer state is not manufactured, seeded or synthetically mutated to obtain any of those states. If the pilot set lacks takeover variation, **an all-agreement result is weak evidence and must be recorded as such**. If it lacks a real `unknown` or `conflicting` state, **that RS-2 condition remains unmet** — it is not satisfied by substituting a takeover, and the Definition of Done is not weakened to close the Slice.

**Two things the human weighed, stated plainly rather than buried — recorded as they were put before the decision.** First, **SL-4's RS-2 evidence needs elapsed observation time**: its own contract says a multi-day posture history and replay reconstruction can only be demonstrated over elapsed time, and that observation is recorded separately from engineering elapsed but still lands inside calendar elapsed to Done. Six remaining days is workable for a `3–5 days` engineering estimate but is **not generous** once multi-day observation is added, so the Cycle may legitimately need to be re-planned rather than quietly stretched — §5 already requires that re-planning be explicit. Second, **new calibration evidence exists that did not when SL-4's confidence was frozen**: its recorded rationale cites a usable dataset of one Slice (SL-1), and the dataset is now three (SL-1, SL-2, SL-3), two of them behavior Slices, all three showing the **same high bias** in the frozen engineering estimate. That is **information for the planning decision, not a change to the frozen estimate or to the rationale recorded at READY** — Methodology §10.4 freezes the estimate, and re-opening it is not something this decision does either. The human's decision, taken with both points in view, was to start legitimate work now rather than idle the only READY Slice until Cycle 3.

**Cycle 2 close-out — SL-4 (recorded with its Done record, v1.24).** SL-4 reached evidence-ready on 2026-09-12 at 20:19:23 (−06:00), inside the window. The rule above asks that the work *close* by 2026-09-13. This plan gives that date no clock time, and the only timezone it names is the team's local calendar — stated for the Cycle 3 cadence, to which Cycle 2 was built to hand over on Monday 2026-09-14. Read that way, **SL-4 closes inside Cycle 2 if its Done record merges by the end of Sunday 2026-09-13 (−06:00)**, and the register's re-planning count is **0**. **If the merge lands later, that is a re-planning event**: it is recorded here with the actual facts in the same merge — working time separated from observation and human/environment wait — the window is not stretched, the frozen estimate is not re-opened, confidence is not changed retrospectively, and neither Release Scope nor evidence is weakened to fit the date.

**Why the window opens on a Saturday — a deliberate transition.** Cycle 1's original planned window was **2026-09-03 → 2026-09-10** and **remains recorded as planned**; it is not rewritten and was not an error. Its only Planned Slice, SL-1, reached Done on **2026-09-05**, so its planned work was exhausted early and capacity opened. Cycle 2 therefore starts when capacity actually became available rather than idling until Monday, and ends Sunday 2026-09-13 so the cadence lands on a natural calendar boundary — instead of creating a two-day micro-Cycle merely to align the calendar.

**Calendar cadence from Cycle 3.** Beginning with **Cycle 3**, the default planning window is **Monday through Sunday** in the team's local calendar and timezone. This is an **operating default of this plan, not a Methodology invariant** — Methodology §12.1 holds Cycle length to be revisable from evidence, and later calibration (§17.1) may justify changing length or cadence explicitly.

**Cycle 1 — sequenced plan.** Window **2026-09-03 → 2026-09-10**, approximately one week, opening with this planning decision. The case-B prerequisite is sequenced **first**, inside the Cycle, as named prerequisite / setup work. Under Section 18 proportionality it is deliberately **not** a Slice of its own: issuing two scoped read identities is configuration, not Slice-sized work.

| Order | Work | Kind |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Revalidate the current provisioning scope (§4) against live legacy topology — **before either identity is issued**, so each is scoped to what the sources actually require today | prerequisite / setup |
| 2 | Issue the Firestore / GCP read identity with the revalidated scopes, no write authority | prerequisite / setup |
| 3 | Issue the Mongo Atlas read identity, read-only, scoped to the appointment operational record | prerequisite / setup |
| 4 | Make the secret material securely available to the authorized setup path — no value in Git, logs, PRs, fixtures or evidence | prerequisite / setup |
| 5 | **SL-1 Gateway reads v1 (bootstrap)** | Slice |

**How provisioning time is accounted.** Items 1–4 are **pre-execution** work and sit outside SL-1's frozen 3–5 day agent-assisted engineering estimate. Time a human or external party blocks on them is recorded separately as **human / external wait** (Methodology §17.1, metric 3). It does **not** count toward SL-1's **calendar elapsed**, which §17.1 defines as running *from execution start* to evidence-ready / Done — and SL-1's execution starts only once it is Executable, which cannot happen before these items are complete. Folding prerequisite time into either the engineering figure or the calendar figure would corrupt the first calibration data point this initiative produces.

**If the prerequisite slips.** Should provisioning take long enough to make the Cycle target unrealistic, the Cycle is **re-planned explicitly** — the estimate is not quietly enlarged and the Cycle is not quietly extended. The re-planning event and its cause are recorded in this register.

**Prerequisite satisfied — 2026-09-04.** All four sequenced items are complete, so SL-1's case-B dependency is met and the Slice is Executable (Planned, confirmed Accountable, prerequisite satisfied, capacity available — nothing else is in flight). Cycle 1 still opens 2026-09-03 with the planning decision; the prerequisite closed the following day.

| Item | Delivered |
|---|---|
| 1 — revalidate scope | Done before issuance; two corrections applied above |
| 2 — Firestore / GCP identity | `gu-os-sl1-reader` service accounts with `roles/datastore.viewer`, read-only, in both the stage (`unggafb`) and production (`ungga-full`) projects. Project-level read blast radius as TD-5 accepts; containment stays with the Gu OS collection allowlist and the per-read Organization binding check |
| 3 — Mongo Atlas identity | `gu-os-sl1-reader`, read-only, scoped to one cluster |
| 4 — secret material available | Present to the authorized setup path. For this bootstrap the key files stay in the working tree, git-ignored, and are referenced **by file path**, so no key material sits inside an environment value; the Atlas URI lives in the git-ignored local environment file. This is the arrangement chosen for *this* material, not a general secrets policy. No secret value appears in Git, logs, PRs, fixtures or evidence |

**Human-accepted temporary least-privilege deviation (Mongo).** The delivered identity grants database-level `read` on two databases (`bot`, `gu2`) rather than a collection-level `find` on `appointments` alone. Both databases carry collections SL-1 never queries — `bot` also holds the conversation store this Slice deliberately reads from Firestore instead, and `gu2` holds reminder, template-retry, ads and bug-informer collections outside the capability surface.

This is **accepted explicitly by the human accountable for SL-1**, on 2026-09-04, as a temporary bootstrap deviation. It is **not a defect, and not an autonomous engineering decision** — the analysis was surfaced with a recommendation, and the acceptance is the human's. The accepted rationale: the grant is read-only; it reaches a single cluster; SL-1 remains shadow-only; Gu OS still exposes only the bounded capability surface plus its code allowlist; no generic CRUD tool is introduced; and the direct bootstrap adapters are temporary, with **C6 remaining the hard gate before SL-9 and any prospect-facing effect**.

**Human revalidation under the corrected physical fact — 2026-09-04.** The original acceptance was written believing the appointment source was `bot.appointments`. It is `gu2.appointments`, confirmed with the Traditional Gu team. The human accountable for SL-1 **revalidated the deviation explicitly against the corrected fact**, and the revalidation is what governs:

- the appointment source SL-1 requires is **`gu2.appointments`**;
- the current read-only identity **may continue to cover `bot` and `gu2`** for this bootstrap;
- **no Mongo identity reprovisioning is required** merely because the source is `gu2` — the existing identity already reaches it;
- this **remains a temporary bootstrap deviation**;
- the **C6 retirement boundary is unchanged**.

Stated precisely, so the two things are not conflated: **access to `gu2.appointments` is what SL-1 requires** — a grant confined to `bot` would not reach the appointment source at all. **The deviation is the broader database-level `read` across `bot` and `gu2`**, rather than the least-privilege minimum, and that remains the human-accepted temporary condition. If it is ever narrowed *before* C6 — which the revalidation does not require — the minimum required Mongo grant is a collection-level `find` on **`gu2.appointments`**.

**Retirement condition:** revisit and retire the direct credential path **no later than the C6 transition, before assisted effects**. That is the same boundary TD-5 already sets for the bootstrap adapters themselves, so the credential and the adapter path retire together rather than the credential outliving what justified it.

**Production boundary, stated precisely.** Production **IAM/credential provisioning did occur**: a `gu-os-sl1-reader` service account now exists in the `ungga-full` production project with `roles/datastore.viewer`. That is a real change to production identity configuration and should not be described as "production untouched". What did **not** occur: **no Gu OS production runtime or data read**, **no production deployment**, and **no business or prospect-facing effect**.

**The production key, before and after the hosted evidence run.** When the prerequisite closed on 2026-09-04 the key was **issued and valid but deliberately unwired** — nothing in the setup path referenced it — so that the window in which a project-wide production Firestore reader is actually exercised stayed as short as TD-5's time-boxed framing intends. Wiring it was a step inside the Slice, not a new prerequisite.

It was **subsequently exercised, once and only for the authorized SA-1.2 / SA-1.3 evidence path**, on the explicit authorization of the human Accountable. That path is read-only: Traditional Gu production was read, nothing was written to any legacy store, and there was **no Gu OS production deployment and no Gu OS production runtime** — the gateway code executed in the operator's verifier process against Gu OS *staging* state. The run is recorded in [`sl1-hosted-evidence.md`](sl1-hosted-evidence.md).

**Completion — the Mongo side, 2026-09-04.** The paragraph above is written entirely in Firestore terms, because Firestore has two projects and the stage/production distinction is meaningful there. Mongo has **one** Atlas cluster and no stage equivalent, so every Mongo read SL-1 performs is, by construction, a read of that single production cluster. The shape-discovery reads that produced SL-1's recorded source contracts were such reads: read-only `find` and `estimatedDocumentCount`, sampling field names and counts, with no value retained, nothing written and no prospect-facing effect.

**Those reads were within approved SL-1 execution authority**, not an expansion of it. Methodology §14.2 separates behavior/authority mode from Release Scope — `shadow` means no external effects, and RS-3 is defined by what a **Gu OS** production *release* adds (production authorization, schema preflight, controlled deploy, post-release verification, canary/rollback), none of which describes reading a source system Gu OS does not own. Technical Plan §8 goes further and *requires* "a staging pass with pilot credentials", which can only mean the pilot's real records. The case-B prerequisite provisioned this identity for exactly this purpose, and SL-1's Definition of Done requires a contract fixture for `appointment_get`, which cannot be recorded without reading the appointment source. Reading it was required work.

**Recorded as a narrow documentation ambiguity, not a breach.** What the governing sources do not state is how the word "production" applies to a **legacy source system** Gu OS reads but does not own or deploy to. Every definition in the Methodology and the release-path playbook is written for Gu OS's own deployment target. That gap is why this paragraph was originally silent on Mongo. It is worth a later methodology refinement — a sentence distinguishing *Gu OS production release* from *reading an external system's production data* — and is logged here rather than resolved unilaterally. Detail in [`sl1-legacy-read-credentials.md`](sl1-legacy-read-credentials.md) §5.

**SL-1 actuals — what the evidence supports, and what it does not.**

**Where evidence-ready falls, and why not earlier.** Completing the RS-2 hosted run (20:12:24 −06:00, `ranAt` in its artifact) is *not* the boundary. This plan said so itself at v1.5 — hosted evidence complete, still not Done, because the deterministic CI layer RS-1 requires was outstanding — and the frozen estimate was written to cover "PR/CI iterations through evidence". So the anchor is the first reviewed implementation head that carried every substantive repair **and** satisfied the complete required evidence set.

The anchors are defensible timestamps, not recollection:

| Boundary | Timestamp (−06:00) | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Execution start | 2026-09-04 **14:41:29** | `git reflog` — branch created from `main` |
| Last substantive pre-closure repair | 2026-09-04 **21:28:07** | committer date of the reviewed head `7634eca…`, which is what the amend that folded in the accepted evidence-metadata policy actually produced |
| **Evidence-ready** | 2026-09-04 **21:33:03** | successful deterministic CI completion for that exact head |

Calendar elapsed follows directly: **6 h 52 m** from execution start to evidence-ready, and **6 h 47 m** to the last substantive repair before closure.

**Metrics 2 and 3 are deliberately not given as numbers.** Splitting that window into agent-assisted engineering versus human/external wait would require timestamps that do not exist: commit times record when work *landed*, not when it *resumed* after a human reply, and four human decision boundaries fall inside the window — the authorization to read production and select a lead, the encryption-key provisioning stop, the evidence-metadata policy decision, and the PR review. Each is visible as a gap between commits, but no gap separates thinking from waiting. Inventing a split would corrupt the first data point in a calibration series whose whole purpose is honesty about estimate bias.

What can be stated rigorously is the bound and nothing beyond it: **agent-assisted engineering elapsed ≤ total calendar elapsed to evidence-ready (6 h 52 m)**. On that basis the frozen **3–5 day** estimate was **materially conservative — biased high**. The size of that bias is deliberately left unquantified: metric 2 is not separately measured, and this methodology establishes no hours-per-"day" conversion for an estimate expressed in days, so any ratio would be arithmetic on two undefined quantities. This is `n = 1`: per §17.1 it begins calibrating bias, and must not be read as a productivity multiplier.

**Pre-execution provisioning is excluded, as planned.** Issuing the two read identities and making their material available happened before the branch existed and is recorded as prerequisite work in this section, not as SL-1 engineering or SL-1 calendar time. The staging encryption-key provisioning was a human setup action inside the execution window; it is one of the four boundaries above and is likewise not engineering time.

**Metric 5 — re-planning events: 0.** The Cycle was never re-planned, the estimate was never re-opened, and no Slice scope changed. Three physical-source and design corrections were surfaced during execution (the Mongo database, the appointment key topology, the production-boundary record); under §15 these are documentation-owned defects repaired in the owning artifact, not re-planning.

**Metric 6 — reopen after Done: 1.** Two distinct events, and the distinction is exactly what this metric exists to keep clean.

*Before Done*, PR review found three implementation defects — a product path that accepted unproven credentials, adapter caches that shadowed credential rotation, and appointment containment proven from only the first record. All **code-owned** under §15, repaired with regression coverage. Not a reopen: the Slice had not closed.

*After Done*, final review found two more, and SL-1 was **reopened**. Both are **code-owned** (§15) and neither touches scope, contract, acceptance meaning or Release Scope:

- **`appointment_get` could silently drop a same-store record.** Cross-store pairing on property + date + hour assigned `pair.firestore = view`, so two Firestore records sharing that key overwrote one another — and the same for Mongo. The evidence established that the two stores share no per-appointment identifier; it never established that the key is unique *within* a store, and no source invariant enforces it. Repaired fail-closed: a same-store duplicate key now refuses as `pairing_ambiguous` rather than inventing a match.
- **The bounded Mongo read could return a truncated set as complete.** `.limit(200)` with no overflow signal, and nothing bounds appointments per deal. Both readers now fetch one past the bound and the capability refuses as `result_too_large`; the Firestore side, which was unbounded, is bounded the same way.

Deterministic coverage was added for both, including that no record from a consulted store disappears in the success case and that a result exactly at the bound is returned in full. The degraded mode where no Mongo credential is bound is unchanged and still covered: the capability answers from Firestore and records `storesConsulted.mongo = false`, so a single-store answer is never presented as complete across both. **RS-2 is preserved** — the evidence requirement itself did not change — and the frozen estimate is untouched. Not a re-planning event: nothing about scope or contract moved.

**Metric 7 — Release Scope: RS-2 declared, RS-2 required.** No mid-flight change.

**Metric 8 — new verification capability: yes, two.** A hosted verification target for a legacy source system, which the Supabase-only harness could not reach, and the Organization-scoped credential bootstrap that proves a credential before it is trusted. Both were anticipated by the Definition of Done rather than discovered late, following SL-0's `test:rls` precedent.

Metrics are recorded for the first 3–5 real Slices to calibrate estimate bias and variance (Methodology §17.1); they do not establish a productivity multiplier. SL-0 predates the model, so R1's calibration series begins at SL-1.

## 6. Done records

> **Scope note (v1.20).** From **SL-4 onward**, a Done record additionally carries the §2 **implemented-architecture documentation impact** assessment. The four records below — **SL-0, SL-1, SL-2 and SL-3** — closed before that control existed and are **deliberately left exactly as written**. They are not reinterpreted, not re-evaluated against it, and the field is not back-filled: doing so would fabricate evidence of an assessment nobody was asked to make. The documentation defects that motivated the control were repaired directly by the architecture documentation coherence audit, in its own PRs.

### SL-0 — Done 2026-09-01 (recorded with Technical Plan v1.4)

- **Release Scope achieved:** RS-2 (hosted).
- **Environment reached:** deterministic local + CI, then the **`Gu-OS-Stage`** hosted environment. **Production was not reached.**
- **Evidence:** authoritative record in [`technical-plan.md`](technical-plan.md) §9, "Execution status — SL-0". In summary: the frozen 87-migration chain applies cleanly and the cross-tenant suite passes **36 checks** against a real PostgreSQL 16 (pgvector); a mutation check confirms the restrictive UPDATE guard is load-bearing; the same chain applied in full through `00084` in `Gu-OS-Stage`, where every hosted security invariant matched the deterministic assertions.
- **Verified:** Alebrixe Organization resolvable via the normalized bare owner UID, with the raw `users/<uid>` form retained as provenance only and not resolving as a routing key; exactly one Organization and one binding after apply *and* after an idempotent rerun; explicit seat mapping (Mariana `owner`, Alejandro `advisor`), neither mutated by the rerun; JSONB discovery source byte-identical before and after bootstrap; a falsifiable negative control proving that neither legacy discovery state nor `is_ungga_admin` confers membership; hosted cross-tenant read and write verified with real authenticated user JWTs rather than the privileged service credential.
- **Not exercised:** **production** — no migration applied, no data written, no connection made. Production rollout is the separate **Gate B**, and a read-only production schema-state preflight remains mandatory before `00080`–`00084` are applied there. Also not exercised: any gateway read of live legacy data (that is SL-1), and any prospect-facing effect (shadow stage, per Technical Plan §5).

### SL-1 — Done 2026-09-05, reopened and re-closed 2026-09-05

- **Release Scope achieved:** **RS-2 (hosted)** — as declared at READY, unchanged.
- **Environment reached:** deterministic local + CI, then **Gu OS `Gu-OS-Stage`** for hosted state, reading **Traditional Gu production** (`ungga-full`) read-only through the SL-1 read identity. **Gu OS production was not reached**: no deployment, no migration, no read. There is no deployed Gu OS application runtime in staging, so the gateway code executed in the operator's verification process against staging state.
- **Evidence:** authoritative record in [`sl1-hosted-evidence.md`](sl1-hosted-evidence.md), with machine-readable artifacts under [`evidence/`](evidence/). Credential scopes and their retirement condition in [`sl1-legacy-read-credentials.md`](sl1-legacy-read-credentials.md).

**Slice Acceptance Contract — all seven assertions evidenced.**

| ID | Evidenced by |
|---|---|
| SA-1.1 | `test:legacy-gateway` — four capabilities normalized against recorded contract fixtures, each result carrying provenance |
| SA-1.2 | hosted run of 2026-09-05: a real Alebrixe lead read with provenance **and** freshness (`edited_time`, age recorded), through the Organization-scoped credential path |
| SA-1.3 | deterministic thread-aware coverage plus the same hosted run — 50 items, every item naming its thread and carrying a delivery status |
| SA-1.4 | injected fixture mismatch fires the drift alarm and refuses; an additive field does not |
| SA-1.5 | credential scopes documented, including the accepted whole-database Firestore read and its time-boxed shadow-only bound |
| SA-1.6 | four refusal paths, each asserting the reader recorded **zero reads**; containment carried the hosted run, where `bindingState` was `unbound` |
| SA-1.7 | closed four-capability surface, no generic CRUD parameter, no effect-shaped method, no reachable prospect-facing effect |

**Definition of Done — satisfied.** The four capabilities exist behind the bounded tool surface with fixtures and selftests wired into `test:selftests`; Organization-scoped secret handling was built inside the Slice and exercised end to end, with the `pending_test → active` lifecycle observed as a transition in hosted staging; the hosted verification capability for a legacy read target was built inside the Slice; hosted evidence is recorded naming the environment reached; the drift alarm is demonstrated firing; credential scopes are documented; and this record states what was intentionally not exercised.

**Reopened once, after Done.** Final review found two code-owned defects in
`appointment_get`, both of which would have made an already-recorded claim
false rather than merely incomplete: a same-store duplicate pairing key silently
discarded a record, and the bounded Mongo read could return a truncated set as
complete. Both are repaired fail-closed — `pairing_ambiguous` and
`result_too_large` — with deterministic coverage, and the Firestore side is now
bounded the same way. The Slice Acceptance Contract, Definition of Done and
Release Scope are unchanged; what changed is that the capability now returns
every record from every store it actually consults, up to the bounded limit, or
refuses. The degraded mode is untouched: with no Mongo credential bound it still
answers and marks `storesConsulted.mongo = false`. Recorded as metric 6 = 1 in §5.

**Verified beyond the minimum, because review demanded it.** Product reads resolve **only** `active` credentials, with the connection-check exception named rather than flagged; credential rotation retires the cached driver client through a non-secret fingerprint, closing the Mongo pool; and every appointment in a result set must share one resolvable owner contained to the calling Organization before any of it is returned.

**Not exercised — deliberately, and not to be read as proven:**

- **`appointment_get` and `property_get_details` against real hosted data.** The approved DoD does not require it and the Slice contract disclaims it; both remain fixture-verified. The Mongo *credential* was proven; the Mongo *capability* was not.
- **Any prospect-facing effect.** Shadow stage: the surface is read-only and has no write path.
- **Gu OS production.** Gate B is unchanged, and the read-only production schema-state preflight before `00080`–`00084` remains mandatory.
- **Advisor threads and delivery-status writeback.** Not present on any of the 80 Alebrixe leads examined. SL-6 should not assume advisor threads are populated for this pilot; SL-9 should verify the §15.7 writeback on recent traffic before depending on it.

**Deterministic CI evidence.** RS-1's CI layer is required for RS-2 and was green on the reviewed head, and must be green again for the head that lands the reopen repair. **GitHub owns that verdict, not this document** (Methodology §19.1): the closure commit recording this Done state necessarily changes the head, CI runs again there, and **a red result on the landing commit reopens this record.** Stating it that way keeps the record falsifiable rather than self-certifying.

**Retirement condition carried forward.** The direct bootstrap adapters and both read credentials retire together **no later than the C6 transition, before assisted effects** — the boundary TD-5 sets, unchanged by anything in this Slice.

No Done records exist for SL-5…SL-13.

### SL-2 — Done 2026-09-06

- **Release Scope achieved:** **RS-2 (hosted)** — as declared at READY, unchanged.
- **Environment reached:** deterministic local + CI, then Gu OS **staging** (Supabase project `wdtjqlbsxwiijasicint`), reading **Traditional Gu production** (`ungga-full`) read-only through the SL-1 read identity. **Gu OS production was not reached**: no deployment, no migration, no read. As at SL-1 there is no deployed Gu OS application runtime in staging, so admission executed in the operator's verification process against staging state.
- **Evidence:** machine-readable artifacts under [`evidence/`](evidence/) — `sl2-hosted-admission-2026-09-06.json`, `sl2-hosted-admission-redelivery-2026-09-06.json`, `sl2-hosted-admission-lead3-2026-09-06.json`, `sl2-hosted-admission-admitted-2026-09-06.json` and `sl2-admission-eval-2026-09-06.json`. All carry digests, counts, dispositions, provenance and policy attribution only: no raw lead identifier, no Organization id, no message text.

**Later observation (2026-09-18) — not a Done amendment.** TD-8 specified `contact/legacy-lead dedup via bindings` as part of admission's deterministic executor. The realized SL-2 implementation omitted that seam, and the frozen Slice contract above did not detect it. The omission is classified and repaired prospectively as **SL-15** (§4, §8 Q18 alternative 3). **None of the ten assertions, the Definition of Done, or this Done verdict is rewritten.**

**Slice Acceptance Contract — all ten assertions evidenced.**

| ID | Evidenced by |
|---|---|
| SA-2.1 | hosted runs of 2026-09-06: real Traditional Gu production leads read through the SL-1 gateway and evaluated by governed admission to recorded dispositions, each carrying gateway provenance, freshness and the effective policy version `platform-default@1` |
| SA-2.2 | hosted run of 2026-09-06 against an admitted real lead: **exactly one** `lead_opportunity` Case for `context_jsonb.source_event_id`, its id equal to the one admission returned, Organization-owned, `runtime_authority = legacy`, no `current_step`, no `next_action_at`; the source event `completed` with a matching decision and attribution and `admitted_case_id` equal to that Case; all three owed facts exactly once under `source_ref = source_events:<id>`; exactly one `admission_disposition` narration. 25/25 checks |
| SA-2.3 | deterministic coverage plus the eval set — an ambiguous opener produces no Case and stays open to clarification; a null judgment behaves as ambiguity |
| SA-2.4 | deterministic coverage of every crash boundary, the mid-materialisation race and the fenced-helper contract; **plus hosted evidence** — a redelivery of the same real event returned the original settled outcome with `source_events` unchanged at one row and `claim_epoch` unchanged |
| SA-2.5 | deterministic test: a platform hard bound beats a maximally permissive published policy and a maximally confident model judgment |
| SA-2.6 | deterministic test plus adversarial eval fixtures — an excluded category is not auto-admitted, and a trusted source cannot carry one past the exclusion |
| SA-2.7 | deterministic tests: platform baseline applied and attributed when no policy is published; a draft never becomes authority; an invalid published policy fails closed rather than falling back to the broader baseline |
| SA-2.8 | deterministic refusal tests with zero writes, **and observed against production data** — Lead 1 of the bounded run was refused `ownership_not_contained` because its legacy owner is not the one bound to the pilot Organization |
| SA-2.9 | deterministic test: with `relationship_ops` off, no disposition, no Case, no write |
| SA-2.10 | deterministic test: no prospect-facing effect is reachable, asserted both structurally over the module source and behaviourally over the tables an admitted run writes |

**Definition of Done — satisfied, clause by clause.**

- **§2 shared baseline in full.** Type-check, lint and all validators green; module selftests wired into `test:selftests` and green; flags off ⇒ inert, deterministically tested; **correlation-coverage check** present — the admission model call runs inside a bound AI-usage context and the check drives the real executor; docs-sync note is this record.
- **Cross-tenant negative suite green.** `npm run test:rls` at **75 checks**, extended in this Slice with the `organization_policies` and `source_events` policies, the claim-fencing and lease-ownership semantics, and the admission artifact-identity indexes.
- **Recorded eval/scenario set with its failure-rate bar stated before implementation** (Methodology §14.1). Bar frozen at **≤15% semantic failure and zero adversarial bound breaches** before implementation closed, and never moved. Final run: **17/18 semantic matched, 5.6%**, 3 adversarial with **0** breaches.
- **RS-2 hosted evidence for SA-2.1 and SA-2.2 against real pilot data.** Recorded above.
- **Admission disposition counts observable** (Technical Plan §7). Verified by query against staging: `deferred_clarification / ambiguous_objective: 2`, `admitted / clear_objective: 1`.

**Bounded production access, accounted exactly.** Reconstructed from the run artifacts rather than recalled:

| | |
|---|---|
| Distinct leads **explicitly supplied** and tested before discovery | **3** (the original ceiling, fully consumed) |
| Distinct owner-scoped lead records inspected in discovery, metadata only | **25 / 25** (the three above excluded; no global scan) |
| Distinct conversations inspected in discovery | **5 / 5** (a subset of those 25) |
| Additional **discovered** leads passed to Admission | **1 / 2** — stopped on the first success |
| **Distinct leads passed to Admission in total** | **4** |
| Tenancy refusals | **1** — `ownership_not_contained`, before any inbox row existed |
| Admission dispositions produced | **3** — `deferred_clarification` ×2, `admitted` ×1 |

**Zero Traditional Gu writes. No global or unbounded scan.** Three of the four leads that reached Admission were not admitted, each for a reason the contract requires: one refused by the tenancy gate, one with no actionable objective, one whose objective had been explicitly withdrawn. Separately disclosed: the governed credential bootstrap performs its own read-only connection check that samples one arbitrary `leads` document — no lead identity was selected, retained or used from it.

**What the eval set now contains.** 21 scenarios, of which **3 are real recorded openings** captured under that authorization, redacted, and labelled by human/domain review independently of the model's own output. They span the three outcomes that matter: an actionable objective, an ambiguous opener, and an objective explicitly withdrawn.

**Open observation, deliberately not acted on.** An **explicit withdrawal** currently resolves to the same `deferred_clarification` / `ambiguous_objective` as a merely ambiguous opener, and "clarification remains open" is arguably the wrong shape for a prospect who has declined. This is recorded for the governing contract and the eval to settle; no runtime behavior was changed on the strength of the observation alone.

**Not exercised — deliberately, and not to be read as proven:**

- **Gu OS production.** Nothing was deployed, migrated or read there.
- **Hosted AI-usage correlation.** `ai_usage_events.organization_id` and its index are hosted-observed, and correlation *behavior* is proven by the deterministic executor-level selftest — but the hosted run had `AI_USAGE_METERING_ENABLED` unset, so the meter dropped the event by design and the table holds no row. No governing acceptance or DoD clause requires hosted AI-usage correlation.
- **Index, constraint and trigger presence through the product API.** PostgREST exposes no catalog, so the verifier's preflight cannot prove them and does not claim to. For this target they were established separately by a read-only catalog probe and by the governed delivery record.
- **`assisted` and `live` admission modes, runtime-authority transfer, and any prospect-facing effect.** Out of scope for a shadow Slice and never enabled.
