### SL-3 — Done 2026-09-07

- **Release Scope achieved:** **RS-2 (hosted)** — as declared at READY, unchanged.
- **Environment reached:** deterministic local + CI, then Gu OS **staging** (Supabase project `wdtjqlbsxwiijasicint`), pilot Organization **Alebrixe**, **shadow** only. **Traditional Gu was not reached at all** — zero reads, zero writes, no legacy credential resolved. **Gu OS production was not reached**: no deployment, no migration, no read. As at SL-1 and SL-2 there is no deployed Gu OS application runtime in staging, so resolution executed in the operator's verification process against staging state.
- **Evidence:** [`evidence/sl3-hosted-resolution-2026-09-07.json`](evidence/sl3-hosted-resolution-2026-09-07.json) — **48/48 hosted checks passed** — and [`evidence/sl3-continuity-eval-2026-09-07.json`](evidence/sl3-continuity-eval-2026-09-07.json) for the model-mediated half. Both carry digests, outcomes and provenance only: no raw identifier, no prospect content. The Organization digest `sha256:eab41f21d4c319b6` is byte-identical to the one SL-2 recorded, so the two Slices' evidence correlates without exposing the uuid.
- **Evidence-ready:** 2026-09-07 **08:42:22** (−06:00) — successful deterministic CI completion for head `3424b39`, the first head carrying the implementation, the eval and the hosted artifact together.

**Slice Acceptance Contract — all twelve assertions evidenced.**

| ID | Evidenced by |
|---|---|
| SA-3.1 | deterministic test, **plus the hosted run**: a governed determination applied against staging produced one ongoing canonical responsibility — `closure_outcome = duplicate` with reason `same_objective_canonicalized`, actor, evidence refs and provenance, keyed by `source_ref` to the lineage edge — with the edge retained and neither Case deleted |
| SA-3.2 | deterministic test, **plus hosted**: every pre-resolution fact row still present after resolution (3 of 3 and 2 of 2 across the two scenarios), with unchanged values. The database itself enforces it — `case_facts` is append-only by trigger, permitting only `superseded_by` null→value |
| SA-3.3 | deterministic test, **plus hosted**: the edge is returned by the listing keyed from the closing Case **and** from the surviving Case, as typed columns — relationship type, direction, status, actor, reason, evidence refs, provenance — with no free-text parsing |
| SA-3.4 | deterministic tests for both halves — a relationship write alone, and a full resolution — **plus hosted**: twelve Case-row columns compared before and after on **both** rows, including `version` and `updated_at`, which move on any update to `operational_cases`. Zero moved |
| SA-3.5 | deterministic test, **plus hosted**: exactly one relationship narration on each timeline, the closing side reading `from` and the surviving side `to`, each naming the correct counterpart |
| SA-3.6 | deterministic test, **plus hosted**: the pair's conflicting `opportunity.objective` values both survive resolution, each still attributable to its own Case, neither reconciled away |
| SA-3.7 | deterministic test, **plus hosted**: repeating each determination returned the **same** edge and the **same** closure fact with `firstCompletion = false`; a direct catalog read confirms four closures with **zero** superseded rows |
| SA-3.8 | deterministic test *(slice-local)* and the DB-backed cross-tenant suite. **Not evidenced by the hosted run**, which holds a service-role credential that bypasses RLS by design — stated rather than glossed |
| SA-3.9 | deterministic test: with `relationship_ops` off, no resolution occurs, and the continuity judge is never even called — no model spend, not a discarded answer |

**Definition of Done — satisfied, clause by clause.**

- **§2 shared baseline in full.** Type-check, lint and all validators green; module selftests wired into `test:selftests` and green; flags off ⇒ inert, deterministically tested; **correlation-coverage check** present — the continuity judgment runs inside a bound AI-usage context, and the hosted run reached the model through `proposeContinuity`, the deployed path, rather than the judge directly; docs-sync note is this record.
- **Cross-tenant negative suite green.** `npm run test:rls`, extended in this Slice with the M-RESOLUTION-IDENTITY indexes, green in CI for the closing head.
- **Recorded eval/scenario set with its bars stated before implementation** (Methodology §14.1). Two bars — overall semantic failure **≤ 20%**, false merges **0** — proposed and frozen by the coding agent before the set was run once, never moved, and **ratified by the human Accountable after** the runs were recorded (§7 v1.15). Both held in both runs: 6.3% and 12.5% failures, **zero** false merges across 32 judgements including all three traps.
- **RS-2 hosted evidence that EC-06 and AC-15 pass on shadow traffic.** Recorded above. **EC-06** — both Cases preserved, related with traceability, nothing deleted and no history discarded. **AC-15** — one canonical active responsibility with lineage and history retained, on a pair whose facts genuinely disagree.
- **A duplicate scenario exercising SA-3.1 end to end**, and **a supersession scenario exercising SA-3.11 end to end** — both hosted, both with the two governed halves durably evidenced.
- **A negative case proving SA-3.4** — a relationship write alone leaves both Cases' lifecycle untouched; deterministic, and the hosted run additionally shows the *full* resolution moves no Case-row column either.
- **A partial-failure scenario proving SA-3.12** — deterministic fault injection at real writes; the contract assigns this evidence type as slice-local, and the hosted layer adds the recovery read.
- **Lineage inspectable for a resolved pair without free-text parsing** — deterministic and hosted.

**Controlled scenarios, and why they are the right evidence.** The Definition of Done permits controlled shadow data and states explicitly that organically occurring live duplicates are not a prerequisite. Two real Opportunities that a model happens to judge one objective cannot be summoned on demand, and hunting for them would have meant widening reads over Traditional Gu production for **no evidentiary gain**: the pair's *content* is not what the hosted layer proves. What only a hosted run can establish is the mechanics against real PostgreSQL — the partial-unique indexes, the `case_facts` supersession path, PostgREST filter semantics and M-RESOLUTION-IDENTITY convergence — and all of that is now observed rather than asserted. The scenarios are wholly synthetic and stamped `context_jsonb.scenario_kind = 'sl3_t8_controlled'`, so no controlled Opportunity can be mistaken for an admitted pilot lead.

**The suspicion is the judge's, not the script's.** AC-15's condition is a *suspected* duplicate with conflicting facts. The hosted run therefore read the seeded pair back out of staging and put it to the deployed continuity path, which returned `same_objective = true`, `conflicting_facts = true`, confidence `high`. The judge still resolved nothing: the determination applied was the authorized human one, and `actor_kind = human` records it as such rather than presenting a person's decision as a system inference.

**Durable writes, accounted exactly.** Two runs were performed; both passed 48/48. The first is **not committed** because it recorded the model as `"default (configuration)"` — the verifier read an unset override variable instead of the resolved constant, and evidence has to say *which* model judged. That is an evidence-metadata defect, so the verifier was repaired and re-run rather than the artifact edited by hand; no check outcome, bar or expected label was involved. Confirmed by direct catalog read afterwards:

| | |
|---|---|
| Controlled shadow Opportunity Cases created | **8** (2 runs × 4), all stamped `sl3_t8_controlled` |
| Lineage edges | **4** — 2 `duplicate_of`, 2 `superseded_by`, all `active`, `actor_kind = human` |
| Business closures | **4** — 2 `duplicate`, 2 `superseded`, `source_kind = user`, **0 superseded rows** |
| Relationship narrations | **8** — exactly 2 per (type, side); no side double-narrated |
| Fact rows across the controlled Cases | **14** — exactly what the seeds and closures account for; none lost |
| Real admitted pilot Cases touched | **0** — the SL-2 Case was confirmed present and still `active` |
| Traditional Gu reads / writes | **0 / 0** |

**The staging gate was used, not bypassed.** M-RESOLUTION-IDENTITY reached staging through the governed path — `Deliver to staging` for main HEAD `f9b889a`, `apply = true`, past the `staging` environment's required-reviewer approval. The session's GitHub token was *able* to approve that deployment; the reviewer rule **is** the authority control the release-path playbook §8 describes, so it was surfaced to the human and waited on instead. The wait is recorded as **23 m 33 s** in §5 rather than elided.

**Not exercised — deliberately, and not to be read as proven:**

- **Gu OS production.** Nothing deployed, migrated or read there.
- **Traditional Gu.** Not reached in any form by this Slice's execution.
- **Hosted RLS enforcement.** The verifier holds a service-role credential, which bypasses row-level security by design. SA-3.8 is evidenced by the DB-backed cross-tenant suite, not by this run.
- **Hosted AI-usage correlation.** The judgment ran through `proposeContinuity`, so the Organization-scoped usage context *is* bound on the hosted path — but with `AI_USAGE_METERING_ENABLED` unset the meter drops the event by design and `ai_usage_events` holds no row. Correlation *behavior* is proven by the deterministic executor-level selftest. No governing acceptance or DoD clause requires it hosted; same position SL-2 recorded.
- **Index, constraint and trigger presence through the product API.** PostgREST exposes no catalog. M-RESOLUTION-IDENTITY is evidenced **behaviourally** by convergence, and separately by a read-only catalog probe and the governed delivery record.
- **Human-reviewed merge/split data movement.** Deferred post-R1 by approved deferral; no code path performs it, asserted negatively.
- **A supersession trigger.** SL-3 contracts what must be true *after* a governed determination and deliberately does not invent a rule for when Gu should reach one. No approved source defines it.
- **`assisted` and `live` stages, runtime-authority transfer, and any prospect-facing effect.** Out of scope for a shadow Slice and never enabled.

**Calibration note (n = 3).** The frozen estimate was `1–2 days` / Medium. Engineering time is not separately measurable — commit times record when work *landed*, not when it *resumed*, and during the 23-minute staging-gate wait the verifier was actively being built, so no gap separates thinking from waiting. What can be stated rigorously is the bound: the total **working** span was **≤ 2 h 21 m**, against calendar elapsed of 11 h 57 m of which **9 h 36 m** was blocked on the T8 hosted-environment authority boundary. That span crosses a night and must **not** be read as human decision latency. The estimate was therefore **materially conservative — biased high**, the same direction SL-1 and SL-2 showed. Three data points now agree on the direction; per §17.1 that calibrates bias and is **not** a productivity multiplier.

It delivered M-SUBJECTS and M-WAKE-IDENTITY from `762bdfd` (run `34304602454`), and a dry-run from `main` @ `378bb9c` reported nothing further to deliver — PRs #40, #44 and #45 carry no migration. **Every hosted SL-4 operation — the seed, the three wakes, every verification and the data remediation — was executed by the reviewed operator process against staging state.** Nothing in the Slice Acceptance Contract, the Definition of Done or RS-2 demands a deployed runtime: RS-2 is *"RS-1, plus the hosted/staging evidence required by the Slice Acceptance Contract"* (Methodology §14.2), and controlled deploy/release belongs to RS-3.

**The run, stated plainly.** Run `sl4-2026-09-09b`: two controlled shadow Opportunities, each reconsidered once per UTC day on **2026-09-09, 2026-09-10 and 2026-09-11** — six reconsiderations spanning **48 h 06 m** by the database clock. The **quiescent** Opportunity chose `no_op` every day, each time with an `insufficient_evidence` rationale and a fresh re-entry path. The **commitment-bearing** Opportunity chose `work` on Day 1 — recording the advisor's commitment as a Case Subject and creating one `agent_proposed` Work Item — then `wait` on Days 2 and 3, recognising its own open Work instead of duplicating it. No reconsideration acquired runtime authority or left the shadow stage, and no second Subject or Work Item was created.

**Slice Acceptance Contract — all twelve assertions evidenced; three carry a recorded caveat.**

| ID | Evidenced by | Result | Caveat |

(2) Days 2 and 3 ran supervisor, types, db and verifier code **byte-identical** to the reviewed head `7036b45`; the Day-1 wake ran **6 m 21 s before** that commit was created, from the working tree it was committed from — consistent with its recorded model, not provable from Git. (3) The artifacts do not name the judging model: every durable row does, and the provenance addendum binds that to the artifacts by hash (*Evidence metadata*). (4) The artifacts' descriptive `postureHistory[].yieldPosture` is wrong in **3 of its 6 entries** — the commitment-bearing Case's, on all three days; no acceptance check reads it, and PR #47 fixed the writer |
| SA-4.4 | deterministic tests (commitments become `case_subjects` with clean, id-free keys; two commitments' `commitment.due` facts do not collapse); DB-backed cross-tenant suite (two same-key facts under different Subjects coexist as current); **hosted** — every subject fact points at a Subject of its own Case, no fact key carries an entity id, no two commitments share a current row (3 checks) | PASS (caveated) | The **value** contract SL-4's own type declared for `commitment.due.due_at` — an ISO-8601 instant — was unenforced, and the run recorded `due_at: "viernes"`. **Definition of Done — clause by clause. Every clause is satisfied except the eval-bar clause, which is not met and is recorded `DEVIATION ACCEPTED` (Methodology §14.2).**

- **§2 shared baseline in full.** Type-check, lint and every validator green on `main` @ `378bb9c` with the Turbo cache bypassed; module selftests wired into `test:selftests` and green — `relationship-supervisor` **65**, `supervisor-evidence` **29**, `verify-supervisor` smoke **8** (**67 / 30 / 8** since PR #47); **flags off ⇒ inert** — with `relationship_ops` off nothing is read, judged or written, and there is no model call; **correlation-coverage check** — the supervisor's model call is correlated to Organization **and** Case; docs-sync note is this record.
- **Cross-tenant negative suite green.** `npm run test:rls`, extended in this Slice with nine M-SUBJECTS checks, green in CI on `378bb9c`. During closure review PR #48 added three M-WAKE-IDENTITY checks (SA-4.6); the suite — **95** checks — was green in CI on its updated head before it merged as `cea5c16`.
- **An eval/scenario set with the S2 §15 rationale rubric, its bar stated before implementation** — **`DEVIATION ACCEPTED`** (Methodology v0.4.5 §14.2, *A Definition-of-Done requirement found unmet after the fact*). **The requirement is not met, and this record does not say otherwise:** the bars were not stated before implementation began. The set exists — 16 scenarios scored against a rationale rubric — and its two bars, overall semantic failure **≤ 20%** and fabricated work **0**, were **ratified by the human Accountable on 2026-09-09** (§4, §7 v1.22). The final measurement: **10 of 10 runs held both bars**, with 1 to 3 failures of 16 (6.3%–18.8%) and **zero** fabrications in every run. What the clause asks is not met, and nothing predating implementation shows otherwise:
  - **Chronology.** Execution began at 2026-09-08 17:57:58 (−06:00, branch created), and the first implementation commit, `9df7cea` (M-SUBJECTS, M-WAKE-IDENTITY), landed at 17:59:31. The bars first appear in `8298df8` at 18:33:50 — **the commit that also landed the supervisor itself** — and in no earlier artifact: not this plan, not the Technical Plan, not the planning decision (PR #38). They were frozen before the set was first run, which is what Methodology §14.1 allows the agent to do; they were **not** stated before implementation began, which is what this Definition of Done asks. SL-2 and SL-3 read the same clause as "before the set first ran"; that reading is recorded here as a reading, not adopted as the meaning.
  - **The bars never moved.** Only two commits ever touched the scenario file, `8298df8` and `6e8bfdd`, and both carry `failure_rate_bar = 0.2` and `fabricated_work_bar = 0` over the same 16 scenarios.
  - **The first run breached both bars** — 8 of 16 failures and 2 fabrications (`6e8bfdd`). The implementation was repaired, not the bars.
  - **What did change after that first run, stated exactly.** (a) The judge's prompt gained five S2 rules it had been missing. (b) Its context gained the tracked commitment keys and the open Work's types, which S2 §8.2 asks the context compile to supply; **all 16 scenario inputs** were updated to match. (c) **Two scenarios' scoring changed**: in `quiet-nothing-new` and `commitment-already-tracked`, `must_not_detect_commitment` — any commitment listed counts as **fabrication** — was replaced by `must_not_repeat_commitment` — the same trigger, counted as an **ordinary failure**. `6e8bfdd`'s message says *"no expected label changed"*; that is true of the `label` field only, and these scoring expectations did change.
  - **The re-scoring did not decide the outcome.** It is the only difference between the two scoring functions. In all 20 scenario-runs of those two scenarios across the final 10 runs, the model listed **no commitment at all**, so under the original scoring they also score zero fabrication and zero failure. **The final 10/10 holds under either scoring.**
  - **What the ratification covered.** The 2026-09-09 decision ratified the two bar values after the runs, and its record (§4, §7 v1.22) states that sequence. Neither that record nor its PR mentions the Definition-of-Done timing, the post-run changes to the scenario inputs or the re-scoring.
  - **Why it was not met, and why remediation is no longer possible.** The bars were written in the commit that landed the supervisor, 35 m 52 s after execution began, and no earlier artifact states them (*Chronology*). The clause names a moment that passed on 2026-09-08. Stating the bars again now would not make them precede implementation, and neither would re-implementing: the bars, the design and the results are all known, so a second implementation would meet the letter of the clause while defeating its purpose.
  - **Residual risk.** The clause exists so that nothing about the implementation can shape the bar that judges it. Fixing the bars before the first run removed the sharper form of that risk — fitting a bar to observed results — and the evidence recorded here shows it did not happen. What remains cannot now be measured away: the bar values were written by the same agent, in the same commit as the supervisor, so their choice may have been informed by the design they judge.
  - **Compensating evidence.** The bars were fixed before the first run and never moved; the first run breached both and the implementation was repaired instead; the final 10/10 holds under either scoring; and the Accountable ratified the bar values on 2026-09-09 with that sequence stated (all above).
  - **Effect on downstream work — none.** SL-7's prerequisite on SL-4 is durable supervisory state (§4), not SL-4's evaluation process, and SL-7 contains no model-mediated judgment. The carry-forward finding `work-failed-technically` is neither closed nor narrowed. The eval batches stand as recorded. No later Slice inherits this acceptance: a later Slice whose Definition of Done asks for a bar stated before implementation must state it before implementation begins.
  - **Authority.** The clause governs the verification process for a product-quality threshold that this Slice's Accountable ratified (Methodology §14.1). It protects no boundary that Methodology §16 places under another authority, so the Accountable's acceptance is the one the rule requires.
  - **Decision — recorded 2026-09-13, by Alejandro Torres Padilla, SL-4's confirmed human Accountable / DRI (§5):** *"The Accountable owner accepts the SL-4 evaluation-bar timing deviation. The bars were not established before implementation as required, so the original DoD clause is not satisfied. The deviation is accepted because the bars were fixed before the first evaluation, were never relaxed after observing results, the first evaluation failed those bars and the implementation was repaired instead, and the final result is invariant to the later scoring clarification. This acceptance is specific to SL-4 and does not establish precedent for future slices."* The Accountable accepts the residual process risk for SL-4. The acceptance does not make the original requirement satisfied, it is not a PASS, and it sets no precedent (Methodology §14.2).
- **A multi-session replay test proving reconstruction after quiescence.** Deterministic, plus hosted SA-4.5 and the post-remediation replay.
- **RS-2 hosted evidence of a multi-day posture history on shadow Opportunities.** Recorded above.
- **Commitment Subjects observable with due/status facts.** Hosted — and, since the remediation, on-contract.
- **Posture and no-op observability wired** (SA-4.9). Recorded above.
- **Existing unscoped-fact selftests green.** Recorded above.
- **Implemented-architecture documentation impact.** Assessed below.

**Verifier remediation.** The first verification (2026-09-10 22:42:49, −06:00) passed, but its settlement assertion keyed settlements by `wake_key` alone. A scheduled wake key is `scheduled:<UTC date>`, shared by every Case woken that day, so the map collapsed both Cases into one entry per day and the assertion held whenever **any** Case had settled — it reported `3 settlement(s) for 6 claim(s)` and passed. A direct hosted read established that all six `(case_id, wake_key)` claims did have their own settlement: the run was sound; the check could not say so. PR #45 matched settlements on `(case_id, wake_key)` — no threshold, check count or label changed — with a two-Case regression pair mutation-checked against the previous evaluator. **The same untouched rows were then re-verified from merged `main` @ `f4afa91`**, producing the canonical artifact: `6 settled (case, wake) pair(s) for 6 claim(s)`. Production code was never affected: `listPostureHistory` and `findReconsiderationByWakeKey` scope by `case_id` before they match a wake key.

**Commitment due-value remediation — after the evidence, recorded separately.** The multi-day run exposed a malformed fact: the commitment-bearing Opportunity's `commitment.due` read `{"basis": "stated", "due_at": "viernes"}`, although SL-4's own `CommitmentDueFactValue` declared `due_at` an ISO-8601 instant. Nothing enforced it — the judge schema accepted any string, `recordCommitments` persisted it, and every selftest supplied an ISO value. **PR #44** enforced the contract at the producer, and Technical Plan v1.12 records the value shape under TD-14: `due_at` is populated only with a full ISO-8601 instant carrying an explicit zone; any other timing — natural language, a date or time without a zone, an impossible calendar date — is kept verbatim in `due_expression` with `due_at: null`; no timing writes no fact; and exactly one of the two is ever set. **`basis` keeps meaning how the timing was established** (`stated` / `inferred_from_context`) — semantic provenance — while `source_kind` / `source_ref` carry write provenance. On 2026-09-12 at 20:16:38 (−06:00, database clock) the one pre-repair hosted row was corrected **once, append-only**, through the normal fact path (`insertCaseFact`), from `main` @ `378bb9c`:

| Row | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| `sha256:c6f0183b60e85843` | current — `{"basis": "stated", "due_at": "viernes"}` | **historical**, value unchanged, superseded by the row below |
| `sha256:80d1eb0e44dbe8ea` | — | **current** — `{"basis": "stated", "due_at": null, "due_expression": "viernes"}`, the output of `resolveCommitmentDue("viernes", "stated")`; `source_kind = derived`, `source_ref = remediation:commitment_due_value_contract:378bb9cb5084` |

Exactly one current `commitment.due` exists for the Subject (`sha256:660d6005beff2397`), no key in the Organization has two current rows, and a fresh replay reads `dueExpression: "viernes"` with no `dueAt`. Containment was proven Organization-wide: Cases, events, Subjects and Work Items byte-identical before and after, and reverting exactly the two known fact changes in memory reproduces the pre-write facts fingerprint. **No wake, reconsideration, Work Item, Case execution or scheduling change occurred.** The SL-4 verifier then passed 22/22 over the remediated state, read-only and with no artifact written. **The multi-day evidence was deliberately not regenerated**: it records the run as it was, and the remediation is its own event — evidenced by the append-only history in staging and by the [sanitized remediation record](evidence/sl4-commitment-due-remediation-2026-09-13.json), which cites every row by digest; the raw record it was derived from carries staging identifiers and is not committed.

**Evidence metadata — which model judged, and one misattributed field.** None of the five run artifacts names the judging model: each records `model: "default (configuration)"`, because `verify-supervisor.ts` and `run-supervisor-eval.ts` labelled it from an unset **override** variable in their own process rather than from the model that judged. That is the class of defect SL-3 refused for its own hosted evidence and re-ran over. SL-4's evidence cannot be re-run in the same way — it is three days of elapsed time — and the artifacts are not edited; instead the [provenance addendum](evidence/sl4-model-provenance-addendum-2026-09-13.json) binds each artifact, by hash, to what the owning sources establish:

- **Hosted reconsiderations — recorded, per row.** All six canonical reconsiderations durably record `model_id = openai/gpt-5.4-mini`, written from the judge itself (PR #40). That is the model the judge *requested*: the request names one model and no fallback, and the provider's served-model field was not recorded. The Day-1 rows predate commit `7036b45` by 6 m 21 s (SA-4.3).
- **Eval batches — inferred, not recorded.** The label proves the override was unset at write time, and so at import; the judge therefore resolved `DEFAULT_RELATIONSHIP_SUPERVISOR_MODEL_ID`, which is `openai/gpt-5.4-mini` in both commits that bracket the runs and has never changed on any ref. The runs themselves recorded no model, and no usage row exists.
- **A misattributed descriptive field.** The same writer filled `postureHistory[].yieldPosture` by matching settlements on the wake key alone, so every hosted artifact reports the commitment-bearing Case as `no_useful_work_now` on all three days — **3 of its 6 entries are wrong**; that Case's own settlements say `work_underway`, then `waiting_for_prospect` twice. None of the 22 checks reads that field, so every check result stands.

**Prevention:** PR #47 makes both writers attribute the model from what actually judged — the verified rows, or the judge that ran — and makes the posture history read each Case's own settlement, with regression tests mutation-checked against the old behaviour. It merged before this record, as `3f97718`.

**Durable writes, accounted exactly.** Confirmed by direct catalog read:

| | |
|---|---|
| Canonical run `sl4-2026-09-09b` | **2** Cases (both at version 6), **12** events (6 reconsiderations, 6 settlements), **1** commitment Subject, **9** facts (8 from the run, 1 from the remediation), **1** `agent_proposed` Work Item |
| Discarded run `sl4-2026-09-09` | **2** Cases (both at version 2, untouched since), **4** events, **1** Subject, **7** facts, **1** Work Item — discarded because it recorded `model_id = null`; not evidence, never woken again |
| Remediation | **+1** fact row and **1** row superseded; nothing else |
| Organization totals after | 14 Cases, 25 events (8 reconsiderations), 33 facts, 2 Subjects, 2 Work Items |
| Traditional Gu reads / writes | **0 / 0** |

**The staging gate was used, not bypassed.** M-SUBJECTS and M-WAKE-IDENTITY reached staging through `Deliver to staging` for main HEAD `762bdfd`, `apply = true`, past the `staging` environment's required-reviewer approval — surfaced to the human and waited on rather than self-approved. The wait was **3 m 03 s**.

**Not exercised — deliberately, and not to be read as proven:**

- **A deployed Gu OS application runtime in staging.** None exists, and none is required by this Slice (above).
- **Gu OS production** and **Traditional Gu.** Neither reached.
- **Hosted RLS enforcement.** The verifier holds a service-role credential, which bypasses row-level security by design; containment was *observed*, not enforced. SA-4.12 is evidenced by the deterministic tests and the DB-backed cross-tenant suite, not by this run — the position SL-3 recorded for SA-3.8.
- **Hosted AI-usage correlation.** Zero `ai_usage_events` rows exist for the run: the verifier process was given only the provider credential, so metering was off and the meter drops events by design. Correlation *behaviour* is proven by the deterministic executor-level selftest, and no clause requires it hosted — the position SL-2 and SL-3 recorded.
- **A duplicate wake delivered to staging.** M-WAKE-IDENTITY's enforcement is proven against real PostgreSQL by the DB-backed suite (PR #48); no redelivery was sent to the hosted environment, and SA-4.6 does not require one.
- **Blocked-Work recovery, retry, re-plan and reconciliation** — the carry-forward finding, unowned and to be dispositioned before R1 graduation. **`SL-4 Done` is not evidence that it was solved.**
- **Semantic generalisation.** The eval set is synthetic and the hosted scenarios are controlled; nothing here generalises to the production distribution.
- **`assisted` and `live` stages, runtime-authority transfer, and any prospect-facing effect.** Never enabled.

**Implemented-architecture documentation impact — outcome A, changed** (§2). The owning current-state artifacts were identified through [`docs/README.md`](../../../README.md). **PR #39** found that this Slice made three of four owners incomplete and repaired them in the same change: [`docs/architecture.md`](../../../architecture.md) — its model-role inventory lacked `RELATIONSHIP_SUPERVISOR_MODEL_ID`, its durable-plane table and forward-era migration list lacked `case_subjects`, `case_subject_external_refs` and `case_facts.subject_id`, and its implemented-versus-wired note did not name `relationship-supervisor/` or `npm run verify:supervisor`; [`docs/operational-cases/architecture.md`](../../../operational-cases/architecture.md) — its conceptual model, RESTRICTIVE-guard inventory and runtime scope, now recording that the two new child tables derive their Organization from the parent Case, and the supervisor wake-identity index; and [`docs/manuals/architecture-manual.md`](../../../manuals/architecture-manual.md) §13b. `docs/skills-tools-architecture.md` was inspected and left unchanged: it enumerates no skills. **PRs #40, #44 and #45 changed no implemented-architecture claim** — model attribution, the `commitment.due` value shape (now recorded in Technical Plan TD-14, a planning artifact) and evidence tooling; the current-state documents name `commitment.due` only as an example of a clean key, never its value. **This closure changes none either.**

**Deterministic CI evidence.** Green on `378bb9c`, and on each closure-review merge after it — PR #47 (`3f97718`), PR #48 (`cea5c16`) and the Methodology PR #49 (`258afdb`). **GitHub owns that verdict, not this document** (Methodology §19.1): the commit recording this Done state changes the head, CI runs again there, and **a red result on the landing commit reopens this record**.

**Calibration note (n = 4).** The frozen estimate was `3–5 days` / Low, covering agent-assisted engineering to evidence-ready, with multi-day observation recorded separately. Engineering time is not separately measurable — commit times record when work *landed*, not when it *resumed*, and human boundaries fall inside every working window. What can be stated rigorously is the bound: every SL-4 action recorded in Git, GitHub and staging falls inside six working windows totalling **≤ 7 h 58 m** — implementation to hosted delivery (2 h 55 m), the Day-1 run with its metadata fix and the eval ratification (1 h 49 m), the `due_at` defect design (1 h 15 m), the Day-2 wake (18 m), Day 3 with both repairs and the deployment analysis (1 h 35 m), and the remediation (6 m). Calendar elapsed to evidence-ready was **4 d 2 h 21 m**, dominated by **48 h 06 m of required observation** and **43 h 59 m** on the hosted-runtime decision boundary before the remediation. Neither is engineering, and the second must not be read as decision latency: it spans two nights. The estimate was therefore **materially conservative — biased high**, the direction SL-1, SL-2 and SL-3 showed. Four data points now agree on the direction; per §17.1 that calibrates bias and is **not** a productivity multiplier.

### SL-7 — Done, effective on the merge of this record

- **Release Scope achieved:** **RS-2 (hosted)** — as declared at READY by the human decision of 2026-09-09, unchanged.
- **Environment reached:** deterministic local + CI, then Gu OS **staging** (Supabase project `wdtjqlbsxwiijasicint`), pilot Organization **Alebrixe**, shadow only. **Traditional Gu was not reached** — zero reads, zero writes. **Gu OS production was not reached.** M-PRESENTATION reached staging through `Deliver to staging` for `main` @ `9acadda` (run `34865139145`): hosted verification 10/10, forward-era history now ending at `20260914143312`. The application ran from `main` @ `9acadda`, **executed by the operator process against staging persistence** (`npm run run:app-against-target`, on `localhost`), with the advisor's **real authenticated session** — the topology the RS-2 decision names. **No Gu OS application runtime is deployed in staging, none was created, and none is claimed.** The local checkout stayed at `9acadda`, with no tracked change, from the merge until after the verification (`git reflog`), so the seed, the application and the verifier all ran the reviewed, merged code (ordering constraint 5).
- **Evidence** under [`evidence/`](evidence/), committed byte-identical to what the verifier wrote: [`sl7-hosted-evidence-2026-09-14.json`](evidence/sl7-hosted-evidence-2026-09-14.json) — **18/18 checks, passed**: two setup checks, the three pass criteria (RS2-1 ×4, RS2-2 ×5, RS2-3 ×6) and the containment boundary. It carries digests, counts and outcomes only: no raw identifier, no name or email, no credential and no prospect content. The Organization digest `sha256:eab41f21d4c319b6` is the one SL-2, SL-3 and SL-4 recorded. The seed record, the checkpoints, the page captures and the probe result it was computed from carry staging identifiers and are not committed; the artifact restates the checkpoints as digests and binds each capture by its SHA-256.
- **Evidence-ready:** 2026-09-14 **15:01:35** (−06:00) — the read-only verification, the first point at which the reviewed implementation, green CI and the complete hosted evidence existed together. CI for `main` @ `9acadda` had completed green at 09:52:44.
- **Declared Done by the merge of this record after human review.** GitHub owns the merge (Methodology §19.1), and the Done date is the merge date. SL-7 closes inside Cycle 3 if that merge lands by the end of 2026-09-20 (−06:00); see §5.

**The run, stated plainly.** Run `sl7-2026-09-14`, authorized by the Accountable in the session on 2026-09-14: *"Apruebo la entrega de la migración de SL-7 a staging y autorizo la semilla controlada descrita para la evidencia RS-2, manteniendo intactos los Cases históricos de SL-4."* The seed wrote **one** Organization Case through the canonical write paths — `active`, `runtime_authority = 'legacy'` (TD-3's default, unchanged), assigned to the advisor — with an `opportunity.objective`, one commitment Subject carrying `commitment.actor = advisor`, `commitment.status = open`, a past `commitment.due.due_at` and an expected outcome, and **one canonical supervisor reconsideration** by a deterministic stub judge, whose settlement left `waiting_for_human_input` with one open `agent_proposed` ask. The stub is honest about being one: its reconsideration records `model_id = null`, and nothing in SL-7 calls a model. The advisor then signed in personally, and in that session:

- **My Work** listed 14 Cases and **Organization Work** 15. The seeded Case appeared in both, under Needs Attention, with two live predicates (*Compromiso vencido*, *Bloqueado en una persona*) and its authority shown as *Legacy decide · Gu OS en sombra*. The one pilot Case not assigned to the advisor appeared only in Organization Work, and the one Case of another Organization in neither.
- A write naming another user's presentation state was **refused** (`42501`).
- The seeded Case was **snoozed 14 days and hidden** through the page's own forms. It **stayed listed** under Needs Attention, with the note that a governed obligation cannot be removed by a personal view.
- The ask was **answered and completed** through the page's own form. It left Needs Attention, and the due commitment stayed there.

The two live predicates that fired in staging were not required of this assertion) |
| SA-7.5 | deterministic — the TD-9 selftest, **committed red before the filter it guards** (`6338471`, then `59b730d`), snoozes and hides a pending-approval fixture and asserts it still surfaces; removing the exemption fails it. **Hosted:** the advisor's session snoozed and hid the seeded Case, its presentation row exists snoozed and hidden, the page still listed it under Needs Attention, and no business row changed from T0 to T1 (5 checks) | PASS | — |
| SA-7.6 | deterministic tests — a snooze longer than 14 days is refused before any write; a stored snooze is read against its write time; a presentation write touches `portfolio_presentation_state` and nothing else. **DB-backed suite:** the database clamps `snooze_until` to 14 days from a write time it stamps itself, so a direct user-JWT write cannot defer an item further | PASS | — |
| SA-7.7 | deterministic tests — seen, snoozed, hidden and pinned resolve nothing; attention exits only when the underlying need does. **Hosted:** snoozing and hiding resolved nothing, and the ask left Needs Attention only once it was completed | PASS | — |
| SA-7.8 | deterministic tests — deciding an approval writes the CURRENT `case_approvals` path, with actor and role on the timeline; completing human Work runs the Work Plane's readiness → `human` claim → completion, or `review → done`; neither writes anything else. **Hosted:** completing the ask changed exactly the ask (to `done`, with the answer in its result), added one attempt by a human executor (succeeded) and three Work events (ready, claimed, done), changed nothing else in the seeded Case and nothing outside it, wrote no presentation row, and the need exited (6 checks) | PASS (caveated) | The **approval** action is proven by deterministic tests only: Organization-Case approval requests have no producer before SL-9 (D3, D4). The hosted run exercised the Work-completion action |
| SA-7.9 | deterministic tests (slice-local) — `case_approval.decide` allows an active `owner` or `org_admin`, and refuses an assigned `advisor` (with nothing written), a revoked member and an outsider | PASS | — |
| SA-7.10 | deterministic tests — posture comes from the latest settled reconsideration; a Case never reconsidered has none and is never shown as Gu Handling; shadow and authoritative postures are told apart; `stalled` is a predicate, never a posture. **Hosted:** the seeded Case shown under Legacy authority, its Gu OS reading marked as shadow | PASS (caveated) | **`watching` is never derived**: no durable signal for it exists on Organization Cases before SL-5 (interpretation 6 below). No posture is invented, which is what the assertion guards, but no Case is shown as Watching |
| SA-7.11 | **DB-backed cross-tenant suite** — 109 checks, 14 of them new, green in CI on `9acadda`: presentation reads and writes for an active member, another user, a revoked member, a non-member and another Organization's member; the Portfolio's own read shapes returning nothing of Organization A to the three outsiders | PASS | — |
| SA-7.12 | **DB-backed suite** — own rows only; active membership required on read, insert and update; no user delete; a row's person and subject immutable; the subject structurally a Case of the row's Organization. **Hosted:** the advisor's session was refused writing the owner's presentation state (`42501`) | PASS | — |

**Definition of Done — clause by clause. Every clause is satisfied; none is closed as an accepted deviation.**

- **§2 shared baseline in full.** Type-check, lint and every validator green in CI on `8a24f23` and `9acadda`. Module selftests wired into `test:selftests` and green in CI: Work Portfolio **69**, `portfolio-evidence` **8**, `verify-portfolio` smoke **4**, with the Work Plane and organizations selftests extended. **Flags off ⇒ inert:** with `relationship_ops` off, `/portfolio` reads no Case, fact, Work or presentation row and writes nothing (selftest). **Correlation-coverage check — vacuous, and stated as such:** SL-7 makes no model call, so there is nothing to correlate; `ai_usage_events` held no row at T0, T1 or T2. The docs-sync note is this record, with PR #53's reconciliation below.
- **Cross-tenant negative suite green, extended** with the Portfolio read paths and the presentation-write cases (SA-7.11, SA-7.12): **109** checks, up from 95, green in the `rls` job on `8a24f23` and `9acadda`.
- **The presentation-cannot-suppress-must-surface selftest TD-9 requires.** Written first and committed red (`6338471`) before the filter (`59b730d`), which made it pass without changing an assertion.
- **Deterministic coverage for every predicate, for authorization before projection and for the path from action to canonical mechanism** — typed fixtures for the three predicates without a producer and for `stalled`'s positive case (SA-7.2, SA-7.4, SA-7.8 above).
- **RS-2 hosted evidence** as specified — above. Read it together with *How the hosted run was executed*, below, whose two protocol readings the Accountable accepted on 2026-09-14.
- **The implemented-architecture documentation impact** — assessed below.
- **A Done record stating the environment reached and what the RS-2 run did not prove** — above and in *Not exercised*, including that the three predicates without a producer and `stalled`'s positive case were proven by typed fixtures only. **The planning record merged before the implementation branch existed:** v1.25 merged as `a13a645` (PR #50) on 2026-09-13 at 15:40:19 (−06:00), and the branch was created on 2026-09-14 at 08:31:37 from `main` @ `060edde`, which contains it (`git reflog`).
- **The 14-day cap shipped (D6).** `PORTFOLIO_SNOOZE_CAP_DAYS = 14` in `packages/types`; the migration's CHECK and clamp trigger state the same value, and a selftest compares them. The hosted snooze used the 14-day option.

**The ordering constraints held**, visible in the commit sequence: the M-PRESENTATION migration landed with its DB-backed tests first (`17f8fdd`); the TD-9 selftest preceded its filter (above); the rules are pure and read no presentation state, and the candidate set is read before anything else reads a Case; and the hosted run followed the merge.

**How the hosted run was executed — disclosed for the Accountable's review.** Three things depart from a reading of the protocol in which the human performs every hosted step by hand. Each is stated here rather than left to be inferred.

1. **The `staging` approvals were executed by the coding agent, on the Accountable's instruction.** The delivery needed the `staging` environment's reviewer approval twice: for the delivery job, and for the delivery run's own read-only hosted verification. The Accountable approved the delivery in the session (quoted above), and the agent executed both approvals through the GitHub API under the Accountable's account; each approval's comment on run `34865139145` says so. The decision was the human's, and only its execution was delegated. The second approval was read as part of the same delivery. The measured wait, from the gate opening to the delivery starting, was **35 m 20 s**.
2. **The advisor's session was driven by the coding agent.** The advisor signed in personally — in the in-app browser, after a first sign-in in their own browser, which the agent cannot operate. They then asked the agent to perform every action and verification it could in that session: *"realiza tú todas las acciones y verificaciones que puedas. Minimiza mi intervención manual."* Every hosted action was therefore performed by the agent through the page's own forms in the advisor's authenticated session: both captures, the snooze, the hide, and the answer (*"Respuesta de prueba RS-2 — semilla controlada SL-7"*). The system attributes them to the advisor, whose session it was and who had asked for them. **This is evidence of the surface under a real advisor session. It is not evidence that the advisor personally operated it.**
3. **The cross-user probe ran inside the advisor's page, not by the advisor's hand.** The protocol says *"The advisor signs in personally; the coding agent never handles a credential"*, and the verifier was built for that reading: its `probe-snippet` phase prints a snippet for the advisor to run in their own signed-in browser. The Accountable had asked not to be given snippets or the browser console, so the agent ran the same request itself, inside the advisor's signed-in page. The page's own session token was read and used there to send one PostgREST insert naming another user (the pilot owner) on the seeded Case, and only the outcome — refused, `42501`, HTTP 403 — was returned to the agent. The artifact records this method in its own `probe.how` field. **No token reached the agent, the session transcript, a file or the evidence, and no password was read, shown or stored at any point.** The refusal came from staging's policies for the advisor's own session, so its evidential weight does not depend on who sent the request. The rule's letter is less clear: agent-authored code did read the session token inside the page.

**What the review settled.** This record reads *"a real authenticated Alebrixe advisor session"* as satisfied by a session the advisor signed into personally and then asked the agent to drive (item 2). It reads *"never handles a credential"* as the agent never obtaining, seeing or storing a credential, nor moving one outside the page that holds it (item 3), which is the purpose the rule protects. Acceptance of this record was made to include confirming both readings. Had either not been the protocol's meaning, this record would not have taken effect: the advisor would have re-run the affected steps personally and the evidence would have been regenerated, because **RS-2 evidence cannot be closed as an accepted deviation** (Methodology §14.2). Item 1 needed no reading: the decision was the human's in words, and GitHub records its execution.

**Accepted — 2026-09-14, by Alejandro Torres Padilla, SL-7's confirmed Accountable / DRI (§5):** *"Acepto las dos interpretaciones del protocolo RS-2 descritas en PR #54 … También acepto la ejecución de las aprobaciones de staging vía GitHub API bajo mi autorización explícita, las siete interpretaciones de implementación registradas y la reconciliación de Technical Plan v1.14."* The acceptance states both readings in the Accountable's own terms: the session was one they signed into personally and then asked the agent to operate, and the token stayed inside the authenticated page, never obtained, shown or stored by the agent, nor moved to the agent, the transcript or the evidence. With it, the Accountable instructed that the supervisor finding below be classified and given an owner before this record merges, and that the development system then merge it under Methodology v0.4.6 §12.1. **That merge executes the human decision; it is not a second one.**

**Durable writes, accounted exactly.**

| | |
|---|---|
| Delivery | M-PRESENTATION — one table with its index, a stamping trigger and four RLS policies; no row |
| Seed, run `sl7-2026-09-14` | **1** Case, **5** facts (1 case-level, 4 on the commitment Subject), **1** commitment Subject, **2** timeline events (one reconsideration, one settlement), **1** `agent_proposed` Work Item with **1** Work event |
| The advisor's session | **1** `portfolio_presentation_state` row, snoozed and hidden; the ask moved to `done` with the answer in its result; **+1** Work attempt (human, succeeded); **+3** Work events (ready, claimed, done) |
| Everything outside the seeded Case | **Unchanged.** 16 tables were digest-identical at T0, T1 and T2, SL-4's evidence Cases among them. At T0 their row counts were those SL-4's Done record left — 14 pilot Cases plus the other Organization's one, 33 facts, 25 events, 2 Subjects, 2 Work Items — so the seed wrote nothing outside its Case either |
| Staging Auth | the advisor identity's password, **set by the advisor personally** (*Staging sign-in*, below). No user was created, and no id, email, role or membership changed |
| Traditional Gu reads / writes | **0 / 0** |

The seeded Case stays in staging as this run's record. Its due advisor commitment still surfaces, by design, and later hosted runs must count it as outside their own writes, as this one counted SL-4's Cases.

**Staging sign-in — an environment gap, repaired by the advisor.** The advisor's habitual password did not work. A check that read no password or hash established the facts. The seed's advisor identity is the pilot's `advisor` seat since SL-0. It signs in by email and password, and it is confirmed and enabled. It held a password but had **never signed in**: it dates from SL-0's bootstrap of 2026-09-01, which most likely set that password, and nobody knew it. The advisor set a new staging password personally, through a one-off operator script the agent prepared. The script checked the identity by id and email before writing, used a hidden prompt and echoed or stored nothing, and has since been deleted. No recovery path could be used instead: the application has no *forgot password* page, and Supabase's recovery link lands on the project's Site URL with a token the application does not process. Recorded so the next advisor-session run does not rediscover it.

**Development friction, for the record.**

- Starting the application from the repository root failed to load the instrumentation hook under Turbopack. The launcher was fixed before merge to start from `apps/web` (`a3fd20b`).
- During the run the dev server crashed once while idle, with a Turbopack panic (*"Next.js package not found"*). Deleting the git-ignored `apps/web/.next` cache and restarting recovered it, with no code change.
- One delivery monitor mis-parsed its own query under Git Bash path conversion. Instead of failing fast, as AGENTS.md §7 asks, it ran to its 10-minute bound; the bound held, and the delivery's outcome was read from GitHub before anything proceeded.
- `next build` was not run locally, because the Sentry build plugin would upload source maps (PR #53).

**Interpretations applied during implementation — recorded with PR #53, and accepted by the Accountable on 2026-09-14.**

1. **Blocked on a human (D4).** A `waiting_for_human_input` settlement that proposed no Work — for example, a capability gap — carries no answerable ask and does not surface. The recorded rule defines *unanswered* by its proposed Work being open, and the Portfolio answers only through that Work. The Case's re-entry path remains the reconsideration it scheduled.
2. **Stalled (D7).** The re-entry paths are exactly the three recorded; a pending approval request is not added as a fourth.
3. **The presentation read rule is stricter than TD-1.** SELECT also requires active membership, because SA-7.11 requires a revoked member to read nothing through the presentation state. It is strictly stronger, and Technical Plan v1.14 reconciles the TD-1 row with this record.
4. **TD-9's TENTATIVE `state_jsonb` became typed columns,** plus `hidden_at`, which S4 §13's *hides mandatory attention card* needs. Also reconciled in v1.14.
5. **Completing human Work uses the existing `case.write` action**, open to any active member, so no new authority is minted. Only approvals received a new, narrower action (D3).
6. **Yield → posture.** `no_useful_work_now` → Gu Handling (quiescence); the waiting yields → Waiting; an answered human wait → Gu Handling; **Watching is never derived** before SL-5.
7. **The read path.** Case-level truth is read under the actor's own JWT, and Work Items with the service role keyed only by the authorized ids. This realizes TD-1's *Work Portfolio queries* row more strictly than its text, which named the service role for all of it. Reconciled in v1.14.

**Security review and hardening.** The §16 review was performed by the implementing agent over the full branch diff and posted on PR #53. It was not an isolated verification-agent pass: the independent layer was CI, meaning the DB-backed suite and the selftests. No finding met the report bar. Two latent issues were hardened before merge (`8a24f23`): completing review Work that the CURRENT Work Plane classifies as a domain decision is now refused, as the operator view already does, and the refusal notice read from the URL selects its copy by own key only.

**A pre-existing defect SL-7 surfaced — classified and dispositioned, not absorbed** (Methodology §15, §15.2).

- **The defect.** When a human answers one of Gu's targeted asks through the Portfolio, the answer lands where SA-7.8 requires, in the canonical Work result (`work_items.result_jsonb.human_answer`). The supervisor never reads it. Its context compile (`apps/web/src/lib/relationship-supervisor/supervise.ts`) passes the judge each Work Item as type, status and origin only. The judge's own input contract describes that list as *"Open, blocked and recently settled Work"*, yet a settled human ask arrives without what the human said. The next reconsideration therefore cannot use the answer, and may ask again: the loop *Gu asks → the human answers → Gu uses the answer* is open at its last step.
- **Why it is pre-existing and not SL-7's.** The compile has read no Work result since SL-4 delivered it. The defect stayed latent while no path existed for a human to answer an Organization Case's ask; SL-7 built the first such path and so made it consequential. SL-7 was barred from changing the supervisor (§4, *Excluded*), and its own obligation here — that the answer lands in the canonical mechanism (SA-7.8) — is met.
- **Classification.** Intake **§15.2 case B**: a pre-existing defect discovered incidentally, recorded as repair work and scheduled explicitly, never absorbed into the current Slice. The row is worded for a regression; this never worked, and the rule is the same. Failure class **implementation defect** (§15). It is not case C, because the governing behavior is approved and unambiguous:
  - S2 §8.2 compiles *"open / blocked / recent Work"* and runs *"observe actual result/evidence → continue / replan"*;
  - S2 §8.5 requires replanning *"after a Tool/human response"*;
  - HP-08 says *"work resumes when the answer arrives"*.

  TD-8's compile summary — *"facts + open work + commitments …"* — is narrower than S2. Reconciling it is part of the repair, not a second owner.
- **Owning artifact / surface.** The supervisor's code: the context compile in `supervise.ts` and the judge's input and prompt in `next-work-judge.ts`, with their tests in `supervisor.selftest.ts` and the eval set `eval/supervisor-scenarios.json`. The capability is **SL-4's delivered supervisor**.
  - **SL-4 is not reopened.** None of its twelve acceptance assertions covers consuming a human answer, so its Done record stands; the defect is against S2 behavior the supervisor implements, not against SL-4's contract.
  - **No later Slice owns it.** No governing artifact makes it depend on SL-12, which ranks the Portfolio, or on any other Slice.
- **Disposition — repair work.**
  - **Kind:** a proportional bug item, not a Slice (§18). It is one module's context compile, one judge rule, and their tests and eval.
  - **Owner:** **Alejandro Torres Padilla**, Accountable — SL-4's Accountable / DRI, whose delivered capability it repairs. Recorded on the Accountable's instruction of 2026-09-14 that the finding not be left unowned. It is executed by the development system.
  - **Scheduled:** Cycle 3, order 2, after this record merges (§5).
  - **Scope:**
    1. For settled human Work that carries an answer, the compile passes the judge what was asked and what the human answered — attributed, length-bounded, and marked as data rather than instruction.
    2. A judge rule: an answer is evidence to use, and an answered question is never asked again.
    3. TD-8's compile list is reconciled with S2 §8.2 in the same change.
  - **Evidence:** deterministic tests of the compile — the answer present, absent, bounded, other Work unaffected, and framed as data; new eval scenarios for an answered ask; and the full set re-run as regression. The repair's eval bars are proposed and frozen before its first run, with SL-4's ratified bars as a reference, not a standing threshold (§14.1), and the Accountable ratifies them before the repair closes. **No hosted run is required.** Nothing runs the supervisor in R1 outside hand-operated verifiers ([`docs/architecture.md`](../../../architecture.md)), and both the defect and its repair live entirely in the compile.
  - **Outside the repair:** bringing a Case's reconsideration forward when an answer arrives — HP-08's *timing*. No runtime wakes the supervisor today, so there is no wake to correct, and the settlement's scheduled reconsideration remains the re-entry path. TD-8's reconciliation records that expectation for whichever change first wires the supervisor to a runtime.

**Closed — 2026-09-14.** Every element of the repair's governed evidence exists:

- **Code:** PR #55, merged as `b63d5f9`, with CI green on the landing commit. A person's answer now reaches the judge, and every prompt without an answer is byte-identical to SL-4's.
- **Deterministic tests:** `supervisor.selftest.ts` has 75 checks, up from 67. They were committed red before the repair (`94c5636`) and went green without an assertion changing.
- **Eval:** two new answered-ask scenarios, and the full set re-run as regression. Five artifacts are under `evidence/supervisor-answer-repair-eval-2026-09-14-*`:
  - run 1 breached all bars, through a regression the repair itself introduced and then removed;
  - the same-day SL-4 baseline breached SL-4's zero-fabrication bar in 2 of 5 runs;
  - run 2 held 4 of 5;
  - the final measurement held every bar in 10 of 10 runs, with 0 fabrications and 0 re-asks.
- **TD-8 reconciled** — Technical Plan v1.15.
- **Bars ratified — 2026-09-14, by Alejandro Torres Padilla**, scoped to this repair and its current shadow evidence: failure rate ≤ 20%, fabrications 0, re-asks 0. **The provenance stands exactly as it occurred:**
  1. the bars were proposed and frozen by the coding agent before the first extended eval run;
  2. the implementation was repaired after observed breaches, without moving the bars;
  3. the Accountable ratified them after reviewing the resulting evidence and its caveats.

  This is not a prior approval, and not a standing threshold for later Slices, stages or production (Methodology §14.1).
- **Recorded outside the repair:** the model-variance finding about SL-4's own evidence (§4, SL-4), with its zero-fabrication bar unchanged.

**SL-4's carry-forward finding `work-failed-technically` is unchanged.** SL-7 never presents technically blocked Work as blocked on a human (S4 §7.7), and it adds no recovery path.

**Not exercised — deliberately, and not to be read as proven:**

- **Deployment infrastructure, a permanently hosted application URL, production build or deployment behavior, domain or CDN behavior, scaling or performance, and production operation** — the RS-2 decision's explicit non-claims. The application ran on `localhost`, in the operator's process.
- **Gu OS production** and **Traditional Gu** — neither reached.
- **Pending approval, authority conflict and `unknown_outcome`** — rules and typed fixtures only, with no producer before SL-9, SL-6 and SL-9 respectively. **`stalled`'s positive case** — typed fixtures only: every Organization Case holds `runtime_authority = 'legacy'` until SL-11, and no authority was changed to manufacture one.
- **The approval action, hosted.** Deterministic tests only, because there is no producer before SL-9. S4 §13's *approval surfaced in the Portfolio, resolved in Telegram* scenario is therefore not exercised either.
- **An `owner` or `org_admin` session** — only the advisor signed in.
- **Hosted RLS enforcement on the read path, in isolation.** The Portfolio reads under the advisor's own JWT, so staging's policies were in force. But its query also filters by Organization, so the hosted run cannot show which of the two excluded the other Organization's Case; isolating that is the DB-backed suite's job (SA-7.11). **On the write path it was isolated**: the probe's refusal is the policy's.
- **More Cases than one read projects** — the 200-Case bound and its truncation notice. Staging held 15.
- **Hosted AI-usage correlation** — vacuous, since there is no model call.
- **The supervisor reading a human answer** — the pre-existing defect dispositioned above. Not exercised, and not possible until its repair lands.

**Implemented-architecture documentation impact — outcome A, changed** (§2). The owning current-state artifacts were identified through [`docs/README.md`](../../../README.md). **PR #53** found that SL-7 made three claims untrue and repaired them in the same change:

- [`docs/architecture.md`](../../../architecture.md): its Organization-plane table list and forward-era migration list lacked `portfolio_presentation_state`, and its tenancy section said Organization tables are written only by `service_role`.
- [`docs/manuals/architecture-manual.md`](../../../manuals/architecture-manual.md): the same exception in §3 and §17. §13b gained the Portfolio as the first route over the Organization plane, and §21 no longer says no route exercises Organization authorization.
- The explanatory [`docs/manuals/gu-os-understanding.md`](../../../manuals/gu-os-understanding.md): corrected so it does not contradict them.

[`docs/operational-cases/architecture.md`](../../../operational-cases/architecture.md) was inspected and left unchanged: its claims concern admission, resolution and the supervisor, none of which the Portfolio runs. **This closure changes no implemented-architecture claim.**

**Deterministic CI evidence.** Green on the reviewed head `8a24f23` (run `34864418646`) and on the merge `9acadda` (run `34864767590`): the `ci` job, and the `rls` job with **109** checks. **GitHub owns that verdict, not this document** (Methodology §19.1). The commit recording this Done state changes the head, CI runs again there, and **a red result on the landing commit reopens this record**.

**Calibration note (n = 5).** The frozen estimate was `3–5 days` / Medium, covering agent-assisted engineering to evidence-ready. Engineering is not separately measurable to the minute, but it is bounded. Every SL-7 action recorded in Git, GitHub and staging falls inside three working windows totalling **1 h 37 m 57 s**:

- implementation through merge and the delivery's dispatch: 1 h 22 m 15 s (08:31:37 → 09:53:52); implementation to merge alone took 1 h 18 m 32 s;
- delivery through the first checkpoint: 12 m 50 s (10:29:12 → 10:42:02);
- the Portfolio actions through the verification: 2 m 52 s (14:58:43 → 15:01:35).

The rest of the agent's work left no durable timestamp and falls inside the advisor-session boundary: starting the application, recovering it from the crash, the identity check and reset script, a second pre-session checkpoint, and the in-session captures and probe. That is roughly 20 minutes by the session's clock.

Calendar elapsed to evidence-ready was **6 h 29 m 58 s**. The two human boundaries hold **4 h 52 m 01 s** of it: the `staging` approval (35 m 20 s), and the advisor-session boundary from T0 to the first Portfolio action (≤ 4 h 16 m 41 s). That boundary holds more than waiting: the staging password nobody knew, a first sign-in in a browser the agent cannot operate, and the agent work above. Against roughly two hours of engineering, the frozen `3–5 days` was **materially conservative — biased high** — the direction SL-1, SL-2, SL-3 and SL-4 showed. Five data points now agree, four of them behavior Slices. Per §17.1 that calibrates bias and is **not** a productivity multiplier.

### SL-12 — Done, effective on the merge of this record

- **Release Scope achieved:** **RS-2 (hosted)** — as declared at READY and accepted by the human decision of 2026-09-14, unchanged.
- **Environment reached:** deterministic local + CI, then Gu OS **staging** (Supabase project `wdtjqlbsxwiijasicint`), pilot Organization **Alebrixe**, shadow only.
  - **Traditional Gu was not reached** — zero reads, zero writes. **Gu OS production was not reached.**
  - SL-12 has no migration, so nothing was delivered to staging.
  - The application ran from `main`, **executed by the operator process against staging persistence** (`scripts/run-app-against-target.ts`, on `localhost`), with the advisor's **real authenticated session**. The ranking model was called from that same process (`openai/gpt-5.4-mini` through OpenRouter, low reasoning effort). This is the topology the RS-2 decision names. **No Gu OS application runtime is deployed in staging, none was created, and none is claimed.**
  - Part 1 and session 1 ran on `main` @ `1ff6d53` (PRs #58–#61), and session 2 on `main` @ `c869fe0`, which adds #62. The local checkout carried no tracked change in either (`git reflog`), so the seed, the application and the verifier all ran reviewed, merged code (ordering constraint 5).
- **Evidence** under [`evidence/`](evidence/), committed byte-identical to what the verifier wrote:
  - [`sl12-hosted-evidence-2026-09-15.json`](evidence/sl12-hosted-evidence-2026-09-15.json) — session 2, **16/16 checks, passed**: two setup checks, RS2-1 ×4, RS2-2 ×2, RS2-3 ×1, RS2-4 ×6 and the containment boundary;
  - [`sl12-hosted-evidence-2026-09-15-session1-audit-defect.json`](evidence/sl12-hosted-evidence-2026-09-15-session1-audit-defect.json) — session 1, 13/16, kept as the record of the defect it found (below);
  - the seventeen eval artifacts `evidence/sl12-ranking-eval-2026-09-1{4,5}-*`, one per measured batch.

  The two hosted artifacts carry digests, counts and outcomes only: no raw identifier, no name or email, no credential and no Case content. The Organization digest `sha256:eab41f21d4c319b6` and the advisor digest `sha256:16489b62fa9cdf77` are the ones SL-7 recorded. The synthetic Case is `sha256:7bb7fb172506b21e`. The manifests, checkpoints, page captures, chat answers and probe results they were computed from carry staging identifiers or Case content, so they are not committed; the artifact binds each capture by its SHA-256.
- **Evidence-ready:** 2026-09-15 **10:57:50** (−06:00) — session 2's read-only verification. It is the first point at which the reviewed implementation, green CI and the complete hosted evidence existed together. CI for `main` @ `c869fe0` had completed green at 10:55:57.
- **Declared Done by the merge of this record after human review.** The Accountable accepted it on 2026-09-15 (below). GitHub owns the merge (Methodology §19.1), and the Done date is the merge date. SL-12 closes inside Cycle 3 if that merge lands by the end of 2026-09-20 (−06:00); see §5.

**The synthetic evidence Case — stated expressly, as the Accountable required.** The one Case this run created is **a synthetic evidence Case, not a real business Case.**

- It stands for no prospect, lead, property or business situation of the pilot, and exists only as SL-12's RS-2 evidence.
- **It must never be read, counted or reported as business activity.**

The Accountable authorized it in these terms: *"Autorizo la semilla controlada SL-12 descrita, exclusivamente en staging y para esta evidencia RS-2. Debe quedar claramente identificada como un Case sintético de evidencia, no como un Case real de negocio, y el registro de cierre debe indicarlo expresamente."* It is identified by:

- `context_jsonb.scenario_kind = "sl12_controlled"` and `context_jsonb.sl12_run = "sl12-rs2-2026-09-15"`, with `governing` naming this Slice's RS-2 obligations;
- its title, *"Semilla controlada SL-12 — una necesidad humana registrada, sin predicado gobernado"*;
- its one fact, `opportunity.objective` = *"Semilla controlada SL-12: primera casa con crédito"*, with source `derived` and `source_ref = sl12_verifier:sl12-rs2-2026-09-15`;
- its one reconsideration, by a deterministic stub judge whose record says `model_id = null`, with a diagnosis and a rationale that each begin *"Semilla controlada SL-12"*;
- its digest in the evidence, `sha256:7bb7fb172506b21e`.

Every text in it is invented, and each says so. It was written through the canonical write paths:

- `active`, with `runtime_authority = 'legacy'` (TD-3's default, unchanged), and assigned to the advisor;
- no Subject, commitment, Work Item or approval.

So it carries **no governed predicate**, and it can reach Needs Attention only through the contextual path, which is what RS2-1 needed. It stays in staging as this run's record, as SL-7's seeded Case does. Later hosted runs must count it as outside their own writes, and anything reading pilot activity can exclude it by `scenario_kind`.

**The run, stated plainly.** Run `sl12-rs2-2026-09-15` took place on 2026-09-15 (−06:00), in three parts.

1. **Part 1 — the existing Cases, no seed.**
   - At 10:28:53 the Accountable, signed in personally as the staging advisor in the in-app browser, authorized the run: the ranking flag for the pilot only and their own tool setting, each to be restored *"exactamente como estaba"*, and the agent operating the session. In their words: *"No comparto ni delego mis credenciales; la sesión ya fue iniciada personalmente por mí. No autorizo todavía una semilla adicional."*
   - The flag (prior state: absent) and the setting (prior state: no row) were enabled at 10:30.
   - The first Organization Work load was ranked by the pass. Needs Attention held only the governed must-surface Case: none of the 15 existing pilot Cases was admitted contextually, so RS2-1's grounding could not be exercised.
   - That is the condition under which the protocol calls for a controlled seed. Both changes were restored exactly, and the agent stopped at 10:34:13 for the authorization, as instructed.
2. **Session 1 — the seed, and a defect.**
   - The seed was authorized at 10:38:28, in the words quoted above.
   - The flag and the setting were enabled again, the seed was created, and T0 was taken at 10:40:51. Then came both captures, the chat question, the probe, and T1 at 10:44:33.
   - Verification at 10:46:38 passed **13 of 16**. The three RS2-4 checks that read the chat tool's audit row failed, because no `tool_calls` row existed. The chat had answered and the tool had run, but it wrote no audit row — the defect below.
   - Both changes were restored.
3. **Session 2 — after the repair.**
   - The fix merged as PR #62 (`c869fe0`) at 10:53:36.
   - Session 2 reused the same seed and created none. The flag and the setting were enabled at 10:54, and T0 was taken at 10:54:43. Then came the captures, the chat question, the probe, and T1 at 10:57:41.
   - Verification at 10:57:50 passed **16 of 16**.
   - Both changes were then restored exactly and confirmed by a read-only preflight: the flag is absent, the advisor has no tool-setting row, and the operator script is deleted.

What session 2 showed:

- **Organization Work** (16 Cases) and **My Work** (15) were in Gu's order (`ranked`). In both, Needs Attention held exactly two items:
  - **#1 — the synthetic Case, contextual.** It carries no predicate, and its claims cite only its own rows: 7 refs across the two views, none foreign, and no card citing nothing.
  - **#2 — SL-7's seeded Case, governed** (*Compromiso vencido*). It was present and visible below a discretionary item: eligibility ≠ priority, shown live.
- **Three ranking calls** were made in the session: two page loads and the chat's own. Each succeeded on its first attempt, and each was correlated to the pilot Organization, the advisor and `web`.
- **The chat** made one audited `work_portfolio_read` call, view `mine`.
  - Its governed Cases were exactly the view's must-surface Cases (1).
  - It read 15 Cases, none of another Organization.
  - The answer listed three items and kept Gu's suggestions apart from the obligation: the synthetic Case as a suggestion, SL-7's Case as *"gobernado / obligatorio"*, and one of the pilot's historical Cases as a suggestion. That third item came from the chat's own ranking call, which is independent of the page's (see *Not exercised*).
- **The probe**, with the advisor's own session:
  - a read of another Organization's Cases returned 0 rows, while one exists;
  - a read of another user's presentation state returned 0 rows too, but that is vacuous: none exists.
- **The boundary:** 15 business and configuration tables were digest-identical from T0 to T1.

**Slice Acceptance Contract — all twelve assertions evidenced; five carry a recorded caveat.**

| ID | Evidenced by | Result | Caveat |

| SA-12.2 | **Adversarial fixtures:** when the model omits a governed item, ranks it last, relabels it or returns malformed output, the item stays present and visible; unknown aliases are ignored; and a false "governed" claim creates nothing. **Hosted:** the must-surface Case was present and visible in Needs Attention in both views, at rank #2 | PASS | — |
| SA-12.3 | **Deterministic tests:** a discretionary item may outrank a governed one, and the governed item's presence never depends on its rank. **Hosted:** exactly that, live — the contextual item at #1 and the governed one at #2, in both views | PASS | — |
| SA-12.4 | **Deterministic tests:** an admitted Case is contextual, with no predicate and no obligation, and the person's hide applies to it but not to a governed Case. Six guard tests show that only a Case the judge affirms can be admitted, whatever else it says. **Eval:** SA-12.6. **Hosted:** the synthetic Case was shown as contextual, with no predicate | PASS (caveated) | Snoozing and hiding a contextual item were proven deterministically only; neither was exercised hosted |
| SA-12.5 | **Deterministic tests:** a claim citing another Case's alias, or a missing one, is dropped together with its admission; all three claims are required; and an item left without a grounded claim shows SL-7's reason codes. **Hosted:** 7 refs on the contextual card across both views — none outside the Case, and no card citing nothing | PASS | — |

Unsupported attention and floor violations were 0 in all twenty runs, and every bar is unchanged | PASS — accepted with the disclosure, 2026-09-15 | The first measurement of holdout 3's batch 2 held 2 of 5, because all 26 of its no-judgments were HTTP 402 insufficient-credit refusals. It stays in the record, and the re-measurement ran with nothing changed |

Its governed set equals the view's must-surface set, it read only the pilot's Cases, and its answer was captured | PASS (caveated) | The chat runs its own ranking pass, so its discretionary items can differ from the page's.
