## 8. Open questions surfaced by this migration

Recorded rather than silently resolved, per Methodology §15 and the migration instruction. **None of these is a change to approved R1 content** — each is an ambiguity the Slice format made visible.

| # | Question | Why it matters | Proposed disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | **Acceptance-identifier namespace collision.** `AC-1…AC-10` are *architecture contracts* (Architecture Analysis §6–§15), while S1 and S2 use zero-padded `AC-01…AC-nn` for *Spec acceptance scenarios*. So `AC-7` and `AC-07` are different things in different documents, and S3 references `AC-2 / AC-7 / AC-8` meaning the architecture contracts. | Slice traceability depends on identifiers being unambiguous. A future Slice contract citing "AC-7" is genuinely ambiguous. | Human decision. This plan disambiguates in prose everywhere (writing "architecture contract AC-1" or "S2 AC-20"). A durable fix would be a naming convention, which is a documentation-governance decision outside this migration. |
| Q2 | **SL-5's Release Scope is not derivable.** Its title says "(prod)" and its DoD retires polling, but Technical Plan §5 places SL-5 inside the **shadow** stage (SL-0…SL-8b, no prospect-facing effects). "Prod" appears to mean the production *wake-up path* rather than a production *release*. | RS-2 versus RS-3 changes the Done boundary and the authority required. | Left as `TBD at elaboration`. Needs confirmation from the Technical Plan owner; not resolvable without redesigning meaning. |
| Q3 | **Where the RS-3 boundary falls for the assisted stage.** SL-9 and SL-10 produce real external effects visible in legacy stores (a `wamid`-correlated prospect message; an appointment round-trip). Whether that constitutes RS-3 "production" under Methodology §14.2, or RS-2 hosted evidence in a pilot-scoped environment, is not stated by any current source. | This determines whether the playbook's production release path (§7 — explicit authorization, read-only preflight, canary, rollback) is engaged for those Slices. | Left as `TBD at elaboration`. This is a release-authority question for a human, and deliberately not decided here. |
| Q5 | ~~**SL-7's Release Scope is not derivable, and the reading that would make it RS-2 needs infrastructure no artifact owns.**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-09 — human decision.** The premise was half right and half too strong. Right: there is still **no deployed Gu OS application runtime** and no ADR, Slice or plan owns creating one — re-verified against all eleven ADRs and every R1 artifact, where each mention is an observation of state, never an assignment. Too strong: the claim that the RS-2 reading *requires* admitting a deployed-runtime prerequisite. It does not. The **application itself can run in the operator's process against hosted staging state with a real authenticated advisor session** — the same shape SL-1, SL-2 and SL-3 already used for RS-2, extended from a script to the application. | Underneath sat an **owning-artifact defect, not a product question**: the Technical Plan said both things at once — §9's *(deterministic)* title and TD-9's selftest-enforced guard against its own DoD sentence *"advisor sees…"*. Patching this Slice Plan would have compensated for a Technical Plan ambiguity. | **Resolved in two artifacts, each owning its half.** The **Technical Plan (v1.11)** clarifies that *deterministic floor* describes the **guarantees**, not the evidence method, and that the advisor-facing outcome **may additionally** be exercised through hosted integration evidence. The **Slice Plan** then records the resulting decision: **SL-7 is RS-2**, evidenced through the real application in the operator's process against staging with a real authenticated Alebrixe advisor session, **without** creating a deployed staging runtime and **without** a deployment-enabling Slice. The minimum evidence and the explicit non-claims are in §4. **S4 is unchanged**: the decision settles evidence method, not product semantics. |
| Q6 | **Chat transcripts after a membership is revoked** — a **governed security/product question**, recorded 2026-09-15 by the Accountable's decision with SL-12's closure (§6). A Work Portfolio summary that `work_portfolio_read` returns to web chat persists in that person's own chat history, as SL-1's legacy reads already do. Revoking the membership stops new reads (SA-12.11), but not access to transcripts written while the person was authorized. **No approved contract establishes retention semantics** for such transcripts, so this is **not classified as a defect today**, and it does not block SL-12. | With more seats, or with live authority, over an Organization's Cases, a former member keeping readable summaries of those Cases becomes a tenancy and privacy question. Today no artifact decides it either way. | **Human decision.** Resolve it before that retention policy is needed for a broader multi-seat or live rollout. Until then it stays open, and nothing in R1 settles it by implementation. |
| Q7 | ~~**Two exempt introspection tools leave no `tool_calls` row.**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-15 — human decision; implemented by Cycle 3 order 5, closed with v1.34 (§6).** Recorded 2026-09-15 by order 4's sweep, as the Accountable instructed (§6). `get_user_preferences` and `list_enabled_tools` are the two tools `tool-audit-ownership.ts` exempts: their handlers write no row, and the graph is meant to write it. But the graph writes rows only on the confirmation path, and these low-risk tools always auto-execute, so neither ever leaves a row. | The exemption was deliberate, and repairing it changes either its meaning or the graph: make the two tools write their own row, or have the graph also audit listed tools that auto-execute. Both are small, but the confirmed sweep scope excluded exempt tools. | **Decided:** *"`get_user_preferences` y `list_enabled_tools` también deben dejar exactamente una fila `tool_calls`. Al ser low-risk/auto-execute, haz que sus handlers sean self-auditing, siguiendo el mismo runner/patrón de las demás low-risk tools, y elimínalas de la excepción correspondiente. No amplíes el graph para auditar genéricamente todas las low-risk tools."* |
| Q8 | ~~**An approved call to a self-auditing tool leaves two `tool_calls` rows.**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-15 — human decision; implemented by Cycle 3 order 5, closed with v1.34 (§6).** Recorded 2026-09-15 by order 4's sweep (§6). Nine tools write their own row and also require confirmation: `operational_case_create`, `operational_case_update_state`, `telegram_send_message_to_contact`, `gmail_send_email`, `generate_document_from_template`, `ungga_publish_listing`, `easybroker_create_listing`, `easybroker_upload_images` and `easybroker_publish_listing`. When a person approves one, the graph closes its confirmation row as executed, and the tool writes its own row as well. This is over-recording, not missing audit. It is established by reading the code, not observed in a hosted run. | Two execution rows for one external effect — a message sent, a listing published — make an audit or a count ambiguous. Which row is canonical is an audit-semantics choice the code never made. | **Decided:** *"La semántica canónica es: one logical tool invocation = one `tool_calls` row. Para una tool que requiere confirmación, la fila creada para esa confirmación es la fila canónica de la ejecución y debe evolucionar hasta `executed` o `failed`; la tool no debe crear una segunda fila. Cuando esa misma tool se autoejecute legítimamente sin una confirmation row existente, debe crear su propia fila. Implementa la forma más limpia de reutilizar/cerrar el record existente sin debilitar la autorización."* |
| Q9 | ~~**`tool_calls` rows are writable by their own user.**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-15 — human decision; implemented by Cycle 3 order 5, closed with v1.34 (§6).** Recorded 2026-09-15 by order 4's security review (§6); pre-existing since migration `00001`. The table's only RLS policy, *"Users can manage own tool calls"*, is `FOR ALL`. It grants every authenticated user **write authority** — insert, update and delete, not only select — over the rows whose session is theirs. Where no `WITH CHECK` is given, PostgreSQL reuses the applicable `USING` expression for the rows a write creates or changes, so the one ownership condition governs every command. **The risk is that authority itself:** the subject of an audit record may create, alter or erase it. The absence of a `WITH CHECK` is not, on its own, the defect. The application writes these rows with the service role. | Architecture treats `tool_calls` as the record of what actually ran, and hosted evidence has read it as such (SL-12's RS2-4). A record its subject may write is not tamper-evident for that subject's own actions. The governed effect ledger that SL-9 introduces is a separate table. | **Decided:** *"`tool_calls` debe ser read-own pero not user-writable. Los usuarios autenticados pueden leer las filas de sus propias sesiones, pero no insertar, modificar ni borrar audit rows. Las escrituras deben quedar en el application/service-role path."* The decision requires a forward migration, plus RLS tests proving own-read, cross-user denial, and denial of user INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE, and that the authorized writer still works. |
| Q10 | ~~**SL-14's eval does not meet its own Definition of Done, and the bars are ratified.**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-16 — human decision. A governed contract correction / narrow prospective re-scope of SL-14's own eval DoD: explicitly NOT a `DEVIATION ACCEPTED`, NOT a change to any ratified bar, and NOT a reinterpretation of any run already measured (§4).** **First measured 2026-09-15**: the stranded-failure and re-ask bars held in all twenty runs on both sets — the failure SL-4's carry-forward finding recorded and the one this Slice exists to remove — while the main set held every bar in 8 of 10 runs and the independent holdout in 4 of 10, breaching on ordinary failure rate and, in 2 of 10, on the zero blind-retry bar. **RESTATED 2026-09-16 on new evidence, with the bars untouched and the earlier record intact (§4).** The Accountable's instruction of 2026-09-15 — *"Si un eval falla, repara la implementación manteniendo los bars fijos y continúa autónomamente"* — was carried out. Four repairs landed: three verifier/scorer defects under the 2026-09-14 ruling, each with regression and mutation evidence, and one **structural** implementation repair replacing a fourth round of prompt wording. Re-measured at `205b1f1`: **the blind-retry, stranded-failure and re-ask bars now hold in all twenty runs on both sets**, ordinary rate holds in all twenty, the fresh independent holdout holds **every bar in 10 of 10**, and the main set holds every bar in **9 of 10**. **What remains is one run**: `attention-already-consumed` proposed internal work where its `must_not_propose_work` allows none. A no-model audit proves its prompt — and those of all 19 main scenarios with nothing blocked — **byte-identical to the judge frozen at `2a12441`**, so SL-14 is structurally unable to have caused it and SA-14.1 forbids it from touching that prompt to repair it. The same-day baseline recorded the pre-SL-14 judge failing that scenario in **0 of 10** runs. | Methodology §14.1: a ratified acceptance threshold is not ordinary engineering authority, so the shortfall is neither absorbed nor answered by moving a bar. **The engineering paths are now genuinely exhausted, which is the difference from the 2026-09-15 statement of this question.** Every repair available without crossing an authority boundary has been made, and the residual failure sits on a prompt this Slice is contractually forbidden to change. Repairing it would mean editing a prompt SA-14.1 freezes and SL-4's own closed evidence covers — reopening a Done Slice — or changing the Supervisor model, whose economics are a governed decision, or weakening a ratified bar. None is engineering authority. | **DECIDED 2026-09-16 — the Accountable rejected option (a) and chose (c), narrowly.** In their words: *"Do not close Q10 as DEVIATION ACCEPTED. Resolve Q10 by a narrow prospective re-scope of SL-14's eval/DoD contract, not by weakening any ratified bar and not by rewriting the evidence already observed."* The decision identifies the defect as the **contract boundary**, not the implementation: SA-14.1 forbids SL-14 to change the behavior while the DoD made it solely answerable for that behavior's statistics. The corrected rule — full text, conditions and limits in §4 — holds every bar value, keeps SL-14-owned behavior and the fresh independent holdout closure-gating, keeps byte-identical pre-SL-14 scenarios as mandatory regression evidence, and attributes a failure on them to the pre-existing SL-4-era finding **only** on mechanical proof that the model input is unchanged and the behavior is outside SL-14's responsibility. It **cannot mask a regression**, is **deterministic code with seven mutation tests**, and was **frozen by commit before any closure evidence existed** because it was written after evidence had been seen. The decision also refuses to erase what it re-scopes: the residual SL-4-era judge-quality weakness on `attention-already-consumed`, `conflicting-availability`, `capability-not-available` and `work-succeeded-with-negative-discovery` is **carried forward as Q11** for disposition before R1 graduation, is **not declared acceptable production quality**, and **SL-4 is not reopened and the Supervisor model is not changed**. SL-14 becomes Done only if the corrected DoD, the Slice Acceptance Contract and RS-2 are all satisfied. **CLOSED OUT 2026-09-16 — all three are satisfied and SL-14 is Done** (§4, §6). The rule this question produced was corrected once more, per assertion rather than per scenario, by the Accountable's resolution of Q12; under it the closure evidence at `4298818` is **0 of 20 runs closure-gating on both sets**. Q11 remains open. |
| Q11 | **Residual SL-4-era Supervisor judge quality on situations no later Slice may repair — carried forward 2026-09-16 by the Accountable's resolution of Q10, and explicitly NOT closed by it.** Four main-set situations whose Work lists have nothing blocked fail stochastically under the Supervisor judge, and the no-model byte-identity audit proves their prompts unchanged since `2a12441`: `attention-already-consumed` fabricated work in 1 of 10 runs at `205b1f1`; `work-succeeded-with-negative-discovery` failed **6 of 10** runs under the pre-SL-14 judge itself; `conflicting-availability` and `capability-not-available` fail intermittently under both. The same-day baseline additionally recorded the pre-SL-14 judge breaching the **zero re-ask bar in 2 of 10** runs on SL-4's own `answer-changes-the-plan`. These are recorded in the sets' `recorded.knownWeakness` and were surfaced more sharply, not caused, by SL-14's extended set. **A fifth weakness joins them on 2026-09-16, measured and then deliberately left unrepaired: the judge RE-LISTS A COMMITMENT the prompt tells it is already tracked.** The prompt has forbidden it since SL-4 — *"NEVER list a commitment that already appears under 'Commitments already tracked', and never reuse one of the tracked keys below"* — and holdout 6 measured the instruction ignored in **10 of 100 calls**, 9 of 10 runs on the one situation where the promise's own Work was blocked. An attribution probe against the judge frozen at `2a12441`, on the same situation with the Work not blocked and the prompts asserted byte-identical, shows the **pre-SL-14 judge doing it in 4 of 18 calls and the current judge in 5 of 18**, so the weakness is SL-4-era; the amplification under a blocked promise is on a prompt SL-14 changed and is recorded in §4 as SL-14 cannot disclaim it. **Two failure shapes, with different costs.** Under the TRACKED key it is inert: `recordCommitments` is idempotent on that key and creates nothing. Under a NEW key for the same promise it is a **second subject for one obligation** — `comparison_prepared` against the tracked `send_comparison` on `commitment-already-tracked`, and 1 of 6 calls in the key probe — and no string comparison can recognize it. **A deterministic repair for the first shape was implemented, measured and REVERTED by the Accountable's decision of 2026-09-16** (`3c5e239`, reverted; both commits kept): it dropped an already-tracked key at the parser instead of asking the model to remember, changed no prompt byte, and dropped **0 commitments in 125 calls** while `commitment-already-tracked` still failed once through the new-key shape it cannot see. It is Q11 work, not SL-14's, and SL-14's final implementation state does not contain it. | Q10's resolution moves who answers for these failures; it does **not** declare them acceptable production quality. Each sits on a prompt SA-14.1 freezes for SL-14 and SL-4's closed evidence covers, so the repair paths all cross an authority boundary: editing an SL-4-era prompt (reopening a Done Slice and its eval), or changing the Supervisor model, whose economics are governed. A zero-bar breach reachable by the *pre-SL-14* judge is the clearest statement that the weakness predates this Slice. | **Human decision, deliberately deferred and bounded.** It blocks no Slice today, and it **must be dispositioned before R1 graduation**, on the same footing SL-4's `work-failed-technically` carry-forward finding had — by exactly one of: accepting it as a recorded production limitation with its scope stated; creating a bounded Slice to repair the SL-4-era judge behavior, which reopens SL-4's eval; or deciding the Supervisor model/economics change that the shadow stage has so far not needed. None of these is engineering authority, and none is chosen here. **The commitment re-listing weakness added on 2026-09-16 is dispositioned the same way and on the Accountable's explicit instruction**: its deterministic repair was reverted out of SL-14 because a repair belonging to this finding does not become SL-14's by being cheap, and whichever disposition Q11 receives must cover both failure shapes — the inert one a key comparison can close, and the new-key duplicate it cannot. **Q11 STAYS OPEN past SL-14's closure, and SL-14's Done record says so expressly** (§6). **The sharpest evidence for it arrived last, on 2026-09-16.** Two measurements of the **same implementation** `4298818`, hours apart, with the supervisor runtime byte-identical, all 96 comparable prompts byte-identical to `70f5f1e`, and the scorer proven verdict-neutral against 350 recorded scenario-runs: the main set produced **20 failures in 250 calls** in the first and **29 in the second**, with the shape moving underneath — `blocked-same-failure-after-a-retry` from 1 of 10 to **7 of 10**, `work-succeeded-with-negative-discovery` from 2 to 6, `unverified-budget-blocks-match` from 4 to 0. **There is no mechanism in that but the model.** The ratified bars held in all twenty runs regardless, which is what a bar with a 20% tolerance is for and is why this closes a Slice without settling a question; the run-to-run spread is nonetheless larger than any single number in this plan suggests, and a disposition that only repairs named scenarios would not answer it. |
| Q12 | ~~**SL-14's corrected closure rule attributes per SCENARIO, and the one breach left attributes per ASSERTION. One run of twenty blocks the Slice.**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-16 — human decision, option (b) narrowly: attribution is corrected prospectively from scenario-level to assertion-level. A contract-modeling correction, not a bar change and not a reinterpretation of holdout 7 (§4).** Measured at `4298818`, the state proposed as final (§4): every bar SL-14 exists to close holds in **all twenty runs on both sets** — blind retries, stranded failures, re-asks and ordinary rate all within bar — the main set holds every bar in **10 of 10** with **0 of 10** closure-gating, and holdout 7, the fresh unobserved instrument, holds in **9 of 10**. The single gating run is a **fabrication**: `h7-waiting-on-the-appraiser-visit-not-technical` reported a commitment where the situation offers only *"el valuador dijo que pasa cuando pueda"* — a vague intention, the class SL-4's own `vague-intention-is-not-a-commitment` already tests. **The assertion is correctly authored and the judge was wrong**, so no verifier repair applies. **The mechanical facts:** it is the only scenario in holdout 7 whose prompt a no-model audit proves **byte-identical to the judge frozen at `2a12441`**; it offers no recoverable alias, so the strict JSON schema never applies and `response_format` is unchanged too, leaving **nothing in the request that SL-14 controls different**; and a same-day probe of 10 calls per judge on that one scenario gives **0 of 10 for the pre-SL-14 judge against 2 of 10 for this one**, a difference not significant at that n and with no mechanism behind it. **Why the rule gates it anyway:** the scenario ALSO carries `not_recoverable: true`, an SL-14 expectation — which the judge **satisfied in all ten runs**, offering no recovery to Work blocked on a person — and `mayAttribute` classifies the whole scenario as SL-14's, so an SL-4-era assertion riding inside it becomes closure-gating. | This is Q10's contract-boundary defect **one level finer**, and it could not have been seen when Q10 was resolved: no evidence had yet separated a scenario's ownership from an assertion's. Q10's rule was deliberately written per scenario and fail-closed, which is why it refuses here — the refusal is the rule working, not a bug in it. **Every engineering path is again exhausted, and each crosses a boundary:** repairing the judge means editing a prompt SA-14.1 freezes and SL-4's closed evidence covers; **extending the rule to per-assertion attribution after seeing this result and applying it to this evidence is fitting the criterion to the outcome**, which the Accountable's own resolution of Q10 forbids; re-running holdout 7 asks an observed instrument for a better roll; and authoring holdout 8 is not available because holdout 7 exposed **no SL-14-owned defect** to repair. Note also the asymmetry the Accountable may weigh: holdouts 4, 5 and 6 carried zero-bar assertions from outside SL-14 too, and this is the first time one of them decided a verdict. | **OPEN — human decision, and the smallest one is a choice among four.** (a) **Accept the evidence as satisfying the corrected DoD**, recording this single run as a §8 Q11 instance on mechanically unchanged input — the narrowest reading, and it must be recorded as a decision about *this* evidence, not as a rule that quietly re-scopes future ones. (b) **Correct the rule prospectively to attribute per assertion**, freeze it before evidence as Q10's was, and require a fresh holdout — which costs another freeze-and-measure cycle and, unlike Q10, would be correcting a rule after its result was seen. (c) **Hold the DoD as written** — every run must hold every bar on both sets — and leave SL-14 not Done pending a Q11 disposition that repairs the SL-4-era judge, which reopens SL-4's eval. (d) **Decide the Supervisor model/economics change** the shadow stage has so far not needed. None is engineering authority and none is chosen here. **RS-2 is unaffected by this question**, since none of the four dispositions changes the judge, so the hosted evidence runs against `4298818` independently of how this resolves. **DECIDED 2026-09-16 — the Accountable chose (b), narrowly.** In their words: *"Correct the Q10 attribution rule prospectively from scenario-level ownership to assertion-level ownership. This is a contract-modeling correction, not a bar change and not a reinterpretation of holdout 7."* **Holdout 7 is preserved exactly as observed** — 9 of 10 under the rule that governed it, the fabrication still recorded as a real SL-4-era judge failure, the run still closure-gating at the time it was measured, and the set neither re-run nor re-scored. The corrected rule's full text, conditions and limits are in §4: an SL-14-owned **assertion** is always closure-gating, including both bars this Slice created; a pre-SL-14 assertion attributes to Q11 only on mechanical proof that the prompt is byte-identical to `2a12441`; **a mixed scenario is evaluated assertion by assertion in both directions**; a failed identity proof collapses the rule to gating everything; and the rate bar keeps its meaning as a rate under **both** denominators, so one SL-14-answerable ordinary inaccuracy does not gate. It is deterministic code with **nine mutation tests, all caught**, and was **frozen by commit before any evidence existed under it** — the sharper requirement here than for Q10, because this rule was written after seeing the result it changes. **One fresh independent holdout, holdout 8, was authored and frozen before the rule ran**, since a corrected rule must not be first exercised on an instrument whose result was already read. The decision explicitly refuses the adjacent widenings: no judge, prompt, model, scorer threshold or bar is tuned against holdout 7, and this is not authorization for another open-ended eval cycle. **Q11 stays open for R1 graduation**, and SL-4 is not reopened. |
| Q13 | ~~**RS-2's two hosted obligations collide on one Case in one day, and closing the gap needs either a second controlled seed or a day.**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-16 — human decision, option (a): one additional controlled seed authorized. Obligation 1 ran the same day and is SATISFIED (§4, `evidence/sl14-hosted-evidence-2026-09-16-recovery.json`).** Executed 2026-09-16 against `4298818` (§4): obligation **2** — the production judge over technically blocked Work — is satisfied and is this Slice's strongest hosted result, the judge declining to retry Work whose attempts were spent and handing it to a person instead; obligation **3** — containment — is satisfied. Obligation **1**, the retry mechanism, was **refused `case_busy`**: the judge's own `reconsider_in_hours: 24` set `next_action_at` to **2026-09-17T21:27:47Z**, and the kernel will not re-lease a Case before its scheduled reconsideration. The order cannot simply be swapped — recovering first leaves nothing blocked for the judge to reconsider — and rescheduling the Case was treated as unavailable, because the verifier deliberately never configures the environment it observes. | The verifier's own usage assumes a **multi-day** run (*"wake once per day for at least 3 distinct UTC days, then recover"*), which SL-4's RS-2 was and this one is not. Two ways close it, and the cheap one touches a recorded boundary: seeding a **second controlled Case** today would satisfy obligation 1 within the hour, and every substantive constraint would hold — synthetic content only, stamped `sl4_controlled`, no authority change, and a retry of nothing but that run's own seeded item — but the boundary as written is *no write outside the seeded Case*, and a second seeded Case is a write outside the first. The authorization granted was for *"the single controlled SL-14 synthetic seed"*. **An agent deciding that its own reading of a boundary is the generous one is the failure mode the boundary exists to prevent**, which is why this is asked rather than assumed. | **OPEN — human decision, and the smallest one is a yes or no.** Either (a) **authorize one additional controlled seed** in the pilot Organization so obligation 1 completes today, under the same recorded boundaries and with the same synthetic content, or (b) **wait**, and run `--phase recover` then `--phase verify` on the existing run label after **2026-09-17T21:27:47Z** — a bounded wait with a deterministic terminal condition, costing one day and no evidence. Nothing else about RS-2 is outstanding, and the choice does not interact with §8 Q12: no disposition of either question changes the judge, so the hosted evidence stands against `4298818` whichever way both resolve. **DECIDED 2026-09-16 — the Accountable authorized one additional controlled seed**, under the same recorded boundaries and the same synthetic content. It was executed as run label `sl14-rs2-2026-09-16b`, and **obligation 1 is satisfied**: `prepare_comparison` was driven to `blocked` / `max_attempts_exhausted` through the Work Plane's own claim-and-fail path and then recovered by retry — the same item, `blocked → ready` attributed to the Supervisor, attempt history intact and one more window (3/4), no duplicate, and the decision reconstructable from the reconsideration alone. **The boundary was not widened by the answer**: the second seed is its own Case with its own run label, nothing outside it was written, no authority changed, and the only Work retried was that run's own seed. |
| Q14 | **Ratify TD-13 `LegacyServiceAuth` v1 — the authentication every cross-repo contract rides on.** Surfaced 2026-09-16 as the one decision blocking two of the three contracts, and made ratifiable on 2026-09-17: [`ADR-111`](../../../adr/ADR-111-legacy-service-auth-v1.md) now specifies the protocol to the level where two teams in three languages can implement it without talking — canonical signing string and its exact separators, path and query canonicalization, raw-body hashing with parsed-and-re-serialized bodies **failing**, signature encoding, duplicate and malformed header behavior, constant-time comparison, skew, and two-key overlapping rotation. **The mechanism was not reopened**; per-service, per-purpose HMAC-SHA256 is unchanged from TD-13. Two things are new. **The key binding is the security substance:** `key_id` resolves service, purpose, Gu OS Organization and permitted legacy owner scope **server-side**, so a valid signature buys authentication and nothing else — a payload Organization claim is at most a consistency check, never authority. And a **`legacy-read` purpose is added** for the Gu OS → Traditional Gu direction, because TD-13 was written as though the boundary were one-directional and C6 runs the other way. Interoperability is proved against a shared fixture, [`td-13-legacy-service-auth-v1.test-vectors.json`](td-13-legacy-service-auth-v1.test-vectors.json) — complete signing strings in escaped **and** base64 form, so a line-separator disagreement fails at the fixture rather than at first integration — guarded here by `npm run test:td-13-test-vectors` (43 checks, including that the signature moves when any signed field or the secret moves). **No shared runtime package** couples the repositories. | **This is an authentication contract across a trust boundary between two systems and two teams, so it is a consequential security decision and not engineering authority** (root `AGENTS.md` §5, §9). It gates C1 and C2 together, and C6's authentication as well — C6's payloads are already code, but a bounded read API without authentication is not a boundary. Asking the Traditional Gu team to build against a TENTATIVE spec asks them to build twice. | **RESOLVED 2026-09-17 — RATIFIED as written at rev 2 by the Accountable.** *"Ratify ADR-111 / TD-13 LegacyServiceAuth v1 rev 2 as written. The HMAC mechanism, canonicalization rules, raw-body contract, server-side key binding, purpose scoping including `legacy-read`, Organization/legacy-source binding, rotation model and interoperability fixture are accepted. Do not reopen ADR-111 unless implementation later exposes a concrete contradiction with the ratified contract."* **TD-13 is therefore no longer TENTATIVE**, ADR-111 moves to **Accepted direction**, and the fixture is the interoperability source of truth. Two consequences are worth stating because ratification is easy to over-read. **It authorizes no code:** the Gu OS verifier, the raw-body middleware and the key registry remain SL-5 and SL-6 work, and **C1 still waits on Q15** — what ratification removed is the *authentication* blocker, which is why C6 moved from "payload implementable, authentication not" to fully specifiable and C2's auth dependency is settled. **And no key may be provisioned for real traffic until Q16 names an owner**, which is now the only open operational dependency of this contract. The reopening condition is deliberately narrow: a concrete implementation contradiction, in which case the contradiction is the trigger and this record is what gets repaired rather than worked around. **Prior history, retained: REVISED 2026-09-17 to rev 2 on the Accountable's pre-ratification review, which returned two named corrections and explicitly held the architecture unchanged.** Both were implementation-blocking and neither touched the mechanism, key-binding model, purposes, rotation model or existing vectors. **First, the timestamp grammar was unimplementable as written.** It lived in a Markdown table cell, where a pipe character must be backslash-escaped, so the source read `^(0\|[1-9][0-9]{0,11})$` — which matches no ordinary epoch, including the fixture's own — while rendering as a correct alternation. The repair is not just the pattern: **the whole grammar moved into a fenced block**, where no escaping applies and what is read is what is implemented, since the table was the defect's cause rather than its location. **Second, "sort by byte order" was ambiguous** against a host language's default string sort. The query alphabet is now an **enforced printable-ASCII precondition** (`0x21`–`0x7E`, rejected before canonicalization, which RFC 3986 already requires), under which a UTF-8 bytewise sort, a Python `str` sort and a JavaScript UTF-16 sort are provably the same ordering — and the record now names precisely where they would diverge without it, which is supplementary characters, because UTF-16 encodes those as surrogates that sort below `E000`–`FFFF`. **The fixture grew from 43 checks to 85** and now asserts the header grammar against every vector, rejects a representative malformed value per header, proves the escaped-pipe form really does reject an ordinary epoch, proves the default sort agrees with explicit bytewise ordering on the fixture's segments, proves the two orderings genuinely diverge outside ASCII so the precondition is load-bearing rather than decorative, and asserts the fixture's published grammar has not drifted from the ADR's. The deliberate strictness has a cost that was weighed and accepted — uppercase hex, a re-rendered timestamp, a normalized path or a re-serialized body all **fail** rather than being tolerated — and each leniency refused is a place two implementations could silently diverge. |
| Q15 | **Ratify the C1 cross-repo integration-event architecture — stable event identity and durable producer-side publication.** Surfaced and specified 2026-09-17 as [`ADR-112`](../../../adr/ADR-112-cross-repo-integration-events.md), from the audit §24 revalidation. Two decisions sit inside it. **First, entity identity is not event identity.** An entity id says *what* changed; an event id says *which mutation* happened. Where the provider already supplies a durable logical message identity it is reused rather than replaced — no invented UUID for a message Meta already named — but `assignment_change` and `appointment_change` get a stable `event_id` minted **once** per logical mutation and preserved across every retry. Identifying those by entity id, `assigned_at`, a database update time or arrival time would collapse two real mutations of the same lead or appointment into one, or split one delivery into two. **Second, publication must be durable on the producer side.** A business write followed by a best-effort POST has a loss window no retry closes: the write commits, the process dies, and nothing anywhere records that an event was owed. The record specifies one narrow `integration_events` table and a drain loop — **not an event-sourcing platform**: no projections, no replay-as-truth, no generic bus. **Atomicity is stated per store rather than averaged into a claim:** assignment is atomic today, written inside the same Firestore transaction as the assignment itself with organization scope structural in the path; **appointments are not**, because the canonical store is reached through the Atlas Data API, which is stateless HTTP and cannot join a transaction — so the residual window is named, given a bounded reconciliation sweep, and **explicitly not called exactly-once**. The record also normalizes `assignment_change` and `appointment_change` across the many legacy writers the audit found, preserving absence where legacy state genuinely cannot reconstruct a previous assignee instead of fabricating one. | **Cross-repo durable-event semantics are consequential architecture** (root `AGENTS.md` §4, §9): event identity and the durability boundary are the kind of choice that is cheap now and expensive after both sides have built. It decides work in **both** repositories — a new durable record and emission seams across thirteen assignment and fourteen appointment writers on their side, two additive `source_events` columns and the receiving route on ours — and it makes an honest at-least-once claim where a weaker record would have implied exactly-once. **The one requirement that is not a property today**: message ingress durability depends on Meta's redelivery, which only holds if the webhook acknowledges *after* the durable record exists, and the audit records it acknowledging before. | **RESOLVED 2026-09-17 — RATIFIED as written at rev 4 by the Accountable**, after three rounds of pre-ratification review that each held the architecture direction and each returned a named defect. **ADR-112 is now Accepted direction and is the governing C1 contract**, so with Q14 also ratified **all three cross-repo contracts are specified and settled** and the next lifecycle step for every one of them is Traditional Gu implementation planning — a human action across a team boundary, not readiness work this team can perform. Ratification authorizes **no implementation**: SL-5 is not READY, and the two additive `source_events` columns are unassigned to a migration number. **Two obligations land on Gu OS consumers and are the part worth carrying forward into SL-5, SL-8 and SL-10:** `unknown` is a third outcome state and must not be flattened into *no effect* or *failed*, and **there is no delivery order at all** — consumers stay idempotent on `event_id`, compare `occurred_at` to tell a stale mutation from a current one, and leave store disagreement to S2 EC-09 rather than to a timestamp. **What the three reviews are worth recording for the Methodology's sake:** each one caught a defect that read as reasonable in prose and was only provable against a mechanism — rev 1 claimed a guarantee its mechanism could not provide, rev 2 proved the right atomicity and then over-claimed what it licensed, and rev 3 asserted an ordering property that its own drainability rule contradicted. None was a wording preference, and none would have been caught by review of the intent alone. **Not to be reopened unless implementation exposes a concrete contradiction. Prior history, retained: REVISED 2026-09-17 to rev 3 on the Accountable's second pre-ratification review, which approved the architecture direction in full — entity/event identity, reused provider ids, stable internal event ids, durable producer-side publication, Firestore-transaction durability for assignment, appointment-document durability for appointments, at-least-once after the obligation is durable, coverage treated separately from durability, the bounded outbox, and the normalized assignment/appointment semantics — while withholding ratification on one precise semantic boundary.** **Rev 2 had proved the right thing and then over-claimed it.** It established that the canonical Mongo mutation and its event obligation are single-document atomic, and treated that as making the event *deliverable*. But a logical appointment operation is not only that write: it can also produce **Calendar and Firestore effects**, and the normalized payload reports both through `store_write_outcomes` and `calendar_effect`. At the instant the canonical mutation commits, some of those outcomes may still be unknown — so delivering then would mean **fabricating them**. Rev 3 separates the two boundaries: **§7A** the obligation becomes durable (atomic, and the only thing here that is), **§7B** the event becomes finalized and delivery-ready once every secondary outcome is observed or explicitly marked, with the drain touching nothing else, **§7C** recovery after a crash between them, and **§7D** the guarantee stated exactly. **The verified ordering makes this cheap rather than structural.** Re-reading `ungga-full` @ `gcp/main` while revising established the per-path store order now recorded in [`legacy-source-audit.md`](legacy-source-audit.md) §24.3: creation runs Calendar → Firestore → **Mongo last**, cancellation runs Firestore delete → Calendar delete → **Mongo tombstone last**, and the nine Mongo-only writers touch nothing else — so **thirteen of the fourteen writers already have every secondary outcome in hand at §7A and finalize in one write**. Exactly one has a real gap: the prospect-side reschedule writes Firestore, then **Mongo**, then Calendar, then **Mongo again** — and that second update, which the code already performs to set `rescheduled` and the status resets, is where finalization lands, at no extra request. **Recovery never guesses.** A process that dies between the boundaries leaves a `pending` entry that is discoverable by definition, and the sweep finalizes it by recording each unresolved outcome as **`unknown`** — never `written`, `deleted`, `failed` or `not_attempted`, each of which is a claim about what happened — with `finalized_by` distinguishing *the writer reported this* from *recovery defaulted it*. So Gu OS can tell "there was no Calendar effect" from "the Calendar effect was never observed", which is the orphaned-Calendar signal of audit §11.4 and must stay visible. **And the sequence counter is gone rather than repaired.** The review was right that rev 2's `integration_seq` raced: read-then-increment-then-push is not atomic as a unit, so two concurrent updates can both stamp the same `seq` while the counter advances twice, and nothing in the Data API returns a post-increment value to the `$push` that depends on it. Rather than reach for a mechanism, **§7E removes the dependence**, because both jobs it did are already done better — drain liveness is the pending array's own non-emptiness, and per-appointment ordering comes from the atomic `$push` append order, established by Mongo and precomputed by nobody. Drain exclusivity is specified as a single-document claim so the removal opens no double-delivery hole, and C1's lack of cross-entity ordering is now an explicit non-claim rather than an assumption a consumer might make. Guarded by a new deterministic selftest, `npm run test:c1-appointment-finalization`, which encodes the state machine, the per-path ordering table, recovery's refusal to assert outcomes it cannot know, and a reproduction of the race that justified the removal. **REVISED AGAIN 2026-09-17 to rev 4 on the Accountable's third review, which again approved the direction and required two concurrency semantics to be made unambiguous — and the first of them retracted a claim rev 3 had made.** **Rev 3 said the drain "preserves array order", and it does not.** Drainability is *finalized and unclaimed*, so a later entry that finalized in one write is deliverable while an earlier `pending` one is not — concretely, a reschedule awaiting its Calendar outcome, overtaken by the confirmation behind it. Append order records mutation order; it never established delivery order. **New §7F removes the per-appointment delivery-order guarantee rather than enforcing head-of-line delivery**, and the choice rests on repository evidence rather than preference: enforcing it would stall every later event for an appointment behind one unresolved entry, **no Gu OS consumer of these events exists** (`source_events` has one live consumer, the admission pipeline, handling `inbound_prospect_message` only), the inbox was never ordered (it copies the `telegram_webhook_updates` per-row insert-or-claim ledger, whose guarantee is at-most-one active processing per row), and **the product specs already treat these as triggers** — S1 §8.9 states progression is not a mandatory linear funnel and that Opportunities may move backward, hold multiple visits and cancel or reschedule, while S2 lists appointment and assignment changes among reconsideration triggers and makes duplicate delivery an idempotency requirement. **The consumer guidance is narrower than "last-writer-wins", and one ratified constraint bounds it:** `occurred_at` distinguishes a stale mutation from a current one, but it does *not* resolve disagreement between the two legacy stores, which **S2 EC-09** governs by requiring preserved uncertainty and provenance and explicitly rejecting generic last-read/last-write truth. Rev 3's looser wording would have appeared to license exactly what EC-09 forbids. **The second requirement was a genuine race:** the recovery sweep fires on age, not on proof of death, so it can reach an entry whose writer is merely slow — and both would then finalize the same `event_id` with different outcomes and different provenance. **Finalization is now a conditional compare-and-set** (§7B, §7C-bis) filtered on `$elemMatch: { event_id, state: "pending" }`, so MongoDB picks the winner and at most one transition takes effect. If the writer wins, recovery modifies nothing and **that is a success, not an error and not a reason to retry**. If recovery wins, **the late writer must not overwrite** — and the reason is sharper than tidiness: the event may already have been delivered, and because Gu OS deduplicates on `event_id`, a corrected redelivery would be **silently dropped**, leaving the producer believing it sent a correction Gu OS will never hold. The real late outcome is retained in a separate `late_outcome_observations` array as diagnostics, chiefly because a rising count means the sweep's age threshold is firing while writers are still working, which is the thing worth fixing; **delivering corrections would be a new event kind and a new decision**, not something to smuggle in by mutating a delivered event. The selftest grew to **100 checks** and both semantics are **mutation-tested in both directions**: removing the conditional transition produces 17 failures, and *adding* head-of-line delivery fails the §7F case, so neither the race protection nor the deliberate absence of ordering can regress silently. **Ratification accepted ADR-112 rev 4** as the C1 contract; with Q14 also ratified, C1 is specified end-to-end. **Prior history, retained: REVISED 2026-09-17 to rev 2 on the Accountable's first pre-ratification review, which held the architecture direction and returned two corrections.** **The first was a false guarantee, and it is the more serious of the two.** Rev 1 reasoned that the Atlas Data API cannot join a multi-document transaction, concluded that the business write and the integration record must therefore be sequential, named the resulting process-death window honestly — and then still called the result *at-least-once with a reconciliation backstop*. **That mechanism cannot provide at-least-once**: a mutation lost in that window leaves no record anywhere that an event was owed, so nothing downstream can recover it. The review was right, and the conclusion was also unnecessary, because rev 1 had asked for the wrong atomicity. **Every appointment mutation is a write to exactly one appointment document, and Mongo is atomic at the single document, including through the Data API** — so the obligation is now recorded **inside the appointment document** (`integration_seq` incremented, a `pending_integration_events` entry pushed) in the same update as the business change, and the drain promotes, delivers and pulls it. Either both exist or neither does. **At-least-once is now earned rather than asserted**, the residual risk is reclassified from durability to **coverage** — a writer bypassing the shared helper, which is enumerable against the fourteen named writers and enforced by a deterministic test — the reconciliation sweep is retained with its purpose corrected to **drain liveness** and its comparands named rather than resting on an unspecified last-modified field, and the native-driver migration rev 1 called the upgrade path is **no longer a prerequisite**. **The second correction was stale wording in the coordination document**, which called the Mongo `_id` the canonical appointment entity identity; the event-facing identity is the client-minted `appointment_id` business key, as ADR-112 §5 already had it. |
| Q16 | **Who provisions and rotates the `LegacyServiceAuth` credentials, in both estates.** Surfaced 2026-09-17 by [`ADR-111`](../../../adr/ADR-111-legacy-service-auth-v1.md) §12, which deliberately declines to answer it. Repository evidence establishes the **mechanism** on each side and not the **owner**: Traditional Gu holds secrets three different ways, and for the main service they are **baked into Cloud Run revisions at build time**, which is why ADR-111 makes two-key overlapping validity a requirement rather than a nicety — without it, rotation is a synchronized redeploy of two estates, which is an outage. Gu OS has the natural home already, `organization_tool_secrets` with its existing `traditional_gu_api` provider slot. What no artifact or configuration establishes is **who is accountable for a key that spans both estates**: who issues it, who holds the rotation schedule, and who acts when one must be revoked. **No secret value was inspected or is recorded anywhere in this work.** | A credential with no named owner is not rotated, and a key that is never rotated is the one that is still valid when it should not be. This is also the first shared operational artifact between the two teams, so the ambiguity is structural rather than an oversight by either. It blocks no Slice today and it must be settled before a key is provisioned for real traffic. | **OPEN — human decision, and it is one sentence: name the accountable owner (or one owner per estate) for issuing, holding and rotating these keys.** Deliberately bounded, and explicitly **not** a secrets-management redesign: the three existing mechanisms each hold a key adequately, and the contract requires only that two key ids can be valid at once. **DEFERRED 2026-09-17 by the Accountable, with the preferred shape recorded rather than the owner named**: *"Defer until revised ADR-111 is ratified. Once ratified, bring Q16 back. Preferred accountability shape is one accountable owner across the two estates, with local custodians/implementers allowed on each side."* The recorded preference settles the *shape* (single accountable owner, local custodians permitted) and deliberately leaves the *name*, which is the smaller remaining decision. **DEFERRAL RE-AFFIRMED 2026-09-17 with Q14's ratification, and the original sequencing reason has now lapsed.** The deferral was granted on the reasoning that an unratified protocol needs no keys; ADR-111 is now ratified, so that reasoning no longer applies, and the Accountable nonetheless chose to keep Q16 deferred — *"Keep Q16 deferred for now… Do not assign the person's name yet"* — with the shape recorded as above and now also written into [`ADR-111`](../../../adr/ADR-111-legacy-service-auth-v1.md) §12. **The accountability shape is settled; only the name is outstanding.** A single owner across both estates is what makes ADR-111 §8's overlapping-key rotation executable at all: two independent owners would have to synchronize a redeploy across two estates, which is precisely the outage the two-key window exists to avoid. This still blocks no Slice, and the constraint it carries is unchanged and now recorded in the ADR itself — **no key is provisioned for real traffic until the owner is named.** |
| Q17 | **How conversation-authority evidence reaches Gu OS — event-sourced through C1, or read on demand.** Surfaced 2026-09-17 by SL-6's elaboration (§9), which was asked to settle whether SL-6 can prove its resolver without C2 and found that **C2 was never the binding gate**. The router calling `/api/legacy/authority` produces router-side recording, which is C2's and SL-11's; **no part of SL-6's inspectable outcome needs it**, and the endpoint is buildable and provable Gu OS-side against the ratified ADR-111 fixture. What SL-6 genuinely cannot proceed without is an **observation of legacy takeover state**, and exactly one approved artifact names how it arrives: **TD-3**, which says legacy `bypass_bot` signals *"map in via ingestion (C1) as evidence."* That routes SL-6 through **C1**, which is ratified and unimplemented. The alternative is a **bounded read at resolve time** — mechanically available today, since the gateway's four existing capabilities are bootstrap adapters already reading Firestore and Mongo directly, and since the legacy inputs are readable state rather than only transitions: the per-lead `bypass_bot` with `last_owner_interaction_wba`, and the coarser per-number kill switch (audit §24.6). SL-1 excluded those fields **by name** (`allowlist.ts:113`) as *"conversation-authority signals consumed by SL-6"*, which is a scope boundary for SL-1 and not a prohibition on SL-6. | **This decides the staleness and tenancy semantics of an authority gate, so it is consequential architecture and not engineering authority** (root `AGENTS.md` §4, §5, §9; ADR-107 owns the doctrine and TD-3 the mechanism). The two paths are genuinely different, and neither is obviously right. **Event-sourced** gives transitions, so `last_human_activity_at` and `authority_source` populate naturally and authority changes record as case events — but a pre-effect gate then consults a **projection that can be stale**, which is the hazard ADR-107's fail-safe exists for, and it makes SL-6 wait for another team. **Read on demand** is point-in-time, which is what an authority gate actually wants, and it lets Gu OS compute the five-minute window from `last_owner_interaction_wba` itself rather than trusting the timing of legacy's scheduled sweep — but it adds a legacy read to every gate, depends on org credentials being present, sees no transitions, and widens the gateway's capability surface into authority-bearing data. There is also a **measurement consequence** the elaboration proved: *"the resolver's answer matches observed reality"* requires an observation independent of the resolver's inputs, so a C1-only path makes the comparison circular and the read is required for the evidence regardless of which path feeds the resolver. | **RESOLVED 2026-09-17 — human decision, option (c): both paths, with roles explicitly separated.** *"Amend TD-3 so conversation-authority evidence has two complementary paths, not two competing sources of truth."* The separation is the substance of the decision, and it is a division of **role**, not a precedence order: **C1 answers what changed, when, with what provenance, and what to wake; the bounded read answers what is operationally true right now for the authority gate.** Neither substitutes for the other. **The read is current gate truth.** One **bounded semantic** gateway capability exposes only the minimum inputs from current legacy reality — per-lead takeover / `bypass_bot`, `last_owner_interaction_wba`, the **distinct** per-Gu-number kill switch, source provenance, and read timestamp / freshness — and **raw Firestore or Mongo documents are not exposed**. The resolver uses the fresh read whenever current conversation authority matters, and on failure, ambiguity or conflicting evidence a consequential prospect-facing effect **remains fail-safe**: there is **no silent fallback to a stale C1 projection as though it were current truth**. Legacy's five-minute takeover/resume behavior may be reproduced as the **independent legacy-observation rule for SL-6 evidence**, and explicitly **must not become a permanent Gu OS product or architecture invariant** — it is an observation of how legacy behaves today, not a Gu OS rule. **C1, once live, remains required** for durable transition evidence, provenance and history, updating the conversation-authority projection on the binding, waking and reconsidering the Case, and operational visibility — but it **does not become the freshness authority for the point-in-time pre-effect gate**. **No last-write-wins reconciliation between them:** where their evidence materially conflicts, provenance is preserved and the conflict resolves or fails safe under ADR-107 rather than silently selecting whichever arrived last. **The measurement must not be circular:** the expected legacy `human_active` verdict is computed by an **independent, source-audited oracle** from the raw observed legacy inputs, the implementation under test is the Gu OS four-concept resolver, and while both may inspect the same point-in-time observation, the expected verdict **may not reuse the resolver or the same normalization/decision function under test**. Each resolution is **persisted** as the durable `authority_resolution` evidence SL-7 already expects — governed resolution identity, state, `detected_at` and provenance — and **logging alone is insufficient**. **The capability boundary is treated as a deliberate addition owned by TD-3 / SL-6, and history is not rewritten**: the ratified four-capability C6 contract did **not** already include it. The new capability may use the sanctioned `bootstrap_direct` adapter for the shadow/advisory path, and **before the read becomes load-bearing for prospect-facing effects it must have a bounded least-privilege Traditional Gu-side implementation behind the same replaceable gateway abstraction** — broad direct store credentials must not become the permanent authority path. That handover reconciles with C6 / TD-5 **without reopening ADR-111 or ADR-112** unless a genuine contract contradiction appears. **Scope held:** enforcement does not move forward from SL-11, `advisor_wa` observations never mint conversation authority, the per-lead takeover flag is never conflated with the per-number kill switch, Case existence never implies runtime authority, and this is **not** broadened into a generic legacy-state capability. **Recorded in [`technical-plan.md`](technical-plan.md) v1.25 TD-3, which owns the mechanism**, and SL-6's contract and readiness follow in §4 and §9. |
| Q18 | **Who may assert a canonical Contact for an already-admitted real Lead Opportunity, so a conversation binding can be attached without inventing identity?** Surfaced 2026-09-18 by the post-merge A–D revalidation of SL-6's T7 binding-path finding. The table, helper and resolver exist. A real pilot Lead→Case→conversation-binding→`authority_resolution`→Portfolio traverse does not. **The missing identity fact is a canonical `contacts.id`**, plus a governed `legacy_lead` identity binding that points at it. Current admission writes Case `context.legacy_lead_id` and an admission fact carrying the same opaque ref. It creates no Contact, no `legacy_lead` identity binding, and no conversation binding. `attachExternalConversationBinding()` requires `contactId`. `createContact` and `attachExternalIdentityBinding` have **zero non-test runtime callers**. The SL-1/SL-2 pre-read gate permits unbound leads. **Case + `legacy_lead_id` is insufficient** because ADR-106 keeps Prospect/Contact, Legacy Lead and Lead Opportunity distinct; audit §5.1 / SL-1 credentials record `lead_id` as a composite operational-context key (`prospect_phone + bot_phone + owner_phone`) that must be persisted opaquely and **must not be parsed** into person identity; TD-1 forbids legacy-lead mirror fields on `contacts` (one contact ↔ n `legacy_lead` bindings ↔ n Opportunities); and AC-4 §9.7 / SA-6.1 say legacy `lead_id` is a reference, never the Gu OS conversation identity. Minting a Contact would newly assert *"this person exists as this Contact, and this opaque lead_id belongs to them."* S1 §6.1 allows an "allowed provisional identity … under policy", but no such policy, minting rule or admission-time producer exists. TD-8 named "contact/legacy-lead dedup via bindings" as admission design; SL-2 did not implement it. | **This is a person-identity assertion, so it is consequential product / identity architecture and not engineering authority** (root `AGENTS.md` §4, §5, §9; S1 owns Contact vs Opportunity; ADR-106 owns the identity bridge; TD-1 / TD-4 own the tables; TD-8 named the omitted admission seam). It blocks T7 and SL-6 Done, not SL-6 readiness. **It is not automatically a C1 / SL-5 dependency.** Q17 established that SL-6's advisory/evidence outcome does not depend on C1; C1 remains the eventual producer for **new** inbound conversation transitions if that remains the approved architecture. Do not fabricate a Contact or write an ad-hoc SQL binding to make hosted evidence pass. Do not seed or mutate Traditional Gu customer state. | **RESOLVED 2026-09-18 — human decision, alternative 3.** The omitted `contact/legacy-lead dedup via bindings` seam belongs to **admission / TD-8**, not to SL-6 authority and not to C1 event transport. Repair it as a **new bounded R1 repair Slice, SL-15** — the next valid Slice identity. **Do not reopen or rewrite SL-2's historical Done record.** Classify the omission prospectively under Methodology §15.2 case B and §15 (missing/misordered work): TD-8 specified the seam; the realized SL-2 implementation omitted it; SL-2's frozen contract did not detect it. **The identity rule is the R1 Contact materialization rule in §4 / SL-15**, not an SL-6-owned bootstrap and not a C1 Contact mint. Alternatives 1 and 2 are refused: SL-6 does not own Contact identity, and assigning C1 would reopen Q17's scope distinction for historical/pilot Cases. C1 / SL-5 remains the eventual producer of **new** inbound conversation events/bindings and **consumes** the governed Contact rather than inventing it. T7 stays unstarted until SL-15's real binding path exists. The other T7 evidence prerequisites remain distinct. Q17 is not reopened. Q11 untouched. Q16 deferred. No real TD-13 key. No prospect-facing effect. |

Nothing in this section blocks review of the structure itself; Q2 and Q3 block only the Release Scope fields they name. **Q14, Q15 and Q16 are the whole remaining human ask for the cross-repo work**, added 2026-09-17 by the pass that made C1, C2, C6 and TD-13 implementable (§1). They are deliberately three and not more: every other question that pass raised was answerable from the repositories and was answered there. Q14 and Q15 are what the empty READY Horizon actually waits on — not a Slice, not a readiness pass, but two architecture ratifications — and Q16 follows Q14 rather than standing beside it. **All three were put to the Accountable on 2026-09-17, and by the end of that day Q14 and Q15 were both RATIFIED and Q16 remained deferred by choice.** Getting there took one deferral and three rounds of pre-ratification review, recorded in each row: every round held the architecture direction and returned a named, mechanically provable correction rather than a wording preference. **The cross-repo ask is therefore no longer a decision** — all three contracts are ratified, none is implemented, and Q16's single sentence is the only open dependency they carry. **Q17 was added later the same day and is a different kind of question.** It did not come from the cross-repo pass; it came from SL-6's elaboration (§9), which established that SL-6's recorded gate names the wrong contract and that the real one is the conversation-authority evidence path. It is the question the READY Horizon now waits on. **Q18 was added 2026-09-18 after PR #75 merged**, by the A–D revalidation of the T7 binding-path finding. It is a person-identity decision, not a C1 availability decision, and it blocks T7 / SL-6 Done rather than readiness. **Q18 is RESOLVED 2026-09-18 as alternative 3:** SL-15 repairs the omitted TD-8 admission seam; SL-2's Done record is not rewritten; Q17 is not reopened. **SL-15 is Planned in Cycle 3 as order 8** with Alejandro Torres Padilla as Accountable / DRI and is Executable (§5). **Q11 exists only because Q10's resolution refused to erase what it re-scoped**: moving who answers for a failure is not the same as deciding the failure is acceptable, and the second decision is still owed before R1 graduation. **Q6 did not arise from the migration.** It was surfaced by SL-12's security review and recorded here by the Accountable's decision, because this section is where R1 keeps questions that need a human decision. It blocks no Slice today. It must be resolved before a broader multi-seat or live rollout needs the policy. **Q7 and Q8** were surfaced by order 4's sweep and recorded here as the Accountable instructed. **Q9** was surfaced by the same change's security review. **The Accountable resolved all three on 2026-09-15**, as a bounded audit-semantics and security repair inside Cycle 3 that reopens neither SL-1 nor SL-12. Its implementation is order 5 (§5).

## 9. Definition-of-Ready evaluations

Readiness is a property of the Slice (Methodology §10.2). This section records the criterion-by-criterion evaluation behind each recorded readiness value, so a later reader can see *why* a Slice is or is not READY rather than only *that* it is. It assigns no Accountable / DRI, no Execution Cycle and no planning status.

### SL-2 — Admission (shadow) — evaluated 2026-09-05

| # | Definition-of-Ready criterion (Methodology §10.2) | Result | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Governing behavior / architectural intent sufficiently approved | **Pass** | S1 approved (AC-01/02/05/06, EC-01/02/03); AC-1 and AC-5 accepted architecture direction; ADR-106 and ADR-108 accepted; TD-2, TD-8 and M-SOURCE-EVENTS approved with Technical Plan **v1.6**, the governing plan after the C5 dependency-contract repair |
| 2 | No unresolved consequential product question inside Slice scope | **Pass** | Technical Plan §11 states the remaining OPEN items — fact-key vocabulary, `closure_reason` enums — are **slice-owned with no human gate**, and that no decision beyond the plan's 2026-08-31 approval needs a human. S1 already approves the `closure_outcome` taxonomy these hang from |
| 3 | Slice Acceptance Contract stated and testable | **Pass** | SA-2.1 … SA-2.10 (§4), each naming a governing source and an evidence type |
| 4 | The required evidence can be produced — or creating the verification capability is part of the Slice | **Pass** | Deterministic and integration coverage extend existing suites; the eval/scenario set is new verification capability and is explicitly inside the Slice's Definition of Done. The RS-2 hosted evidence has a **governed environment path identified by Technical Plan v1.6**, which treats staging/test-number routing as an environment/evidence lever; the exact controlled route is selected at execution, and any Traditional Gu production read requires separate explicit authorization. **Readiness grants no production access** |
| 5 | Release Scope declared | **Pass** | **RS-2 hosted**, declared in §4 and no longer `indicated` |
| 6 | Security / tenancy / authority / data / external-effect impact assessed | **Pass** | Seven-dimension risk table in §4, plus the environment dimension; external effects are **none** (shadow) |
| 7 | Estimate and estimate confidence recorded | **Pass** | `3–5 days`, **Low** confidence, with both uncertainty drivers named, **frozen at READY on 2026-09-05** (Methodology §10.4). Variance against it is recorded in §5 at Done, per the calibration dataset of Methodology §17.1 |
| 8 | Dependencies satisfy the §10.2 rule | **Pass** | **SL-0 and SL-1, both case A satisfied.** C5 is not an SL-2 readiness dependency: targeted revalidation against current legacy source established that its processing/routing enablement and hosted-evidence environment concerns do not gate this Slice, and Technical Plan **v1.6** repaired the §4/§9 contradiction at its owning artifact |

**Result: READY.** All eight criteria pass. Recorded 2026-09-05, and the estimate is frozen at that point (Methodology §10.4).

**READY is eligibility only.** SL-2 is not Planned, not Executable, has no Execution Cycle, no confirmed Accountable / DRI and no just-in-time Tasks. Cycle planning confirms inclusion and an Accountable; executability additionally requires capacity (Methodology §12.1).

**How criterion 8 was resolved — the sequence matters.** The first evidence pass recorded C5 as an unresolved dependency of undetermined class and left SL-2 NOT READY, which was the correct outcome on the evidence then available. A targeted revalidation against current legacy source (Technical Plan Appendix C) then showed the WBA compatibility gate governs whether legacy Gu keeps processing and replying, not whether the records this Slice reads are persisted. The **owning artifact was repaired first** — Technical Plan v1.6 — and readiness was recorded only afterwards. Readiness was never asserted over a live contradiction between governing sources.

### SL-3 — Duplicate & supersession flows — evaluated 2026-09-05

| # | Definition-of-Ready criterion (Methodology §10.2) | Result | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|

Canonicalization is settled by S1 §8.6. **Supersession and canonicalization were both re-examined.** The *structural* contract after a governed determination is specified by ADR-109 §§4/7/8 and AC-6 §11.4, and the *business closure* half is specified by S1 §8.10 and §8.16 with TD-8's `opportunity.closure` fact as its canonical mechanism — so SA-3.1 and SA-3.11 now contract **both**, and SA-3.4 keeps them separate as ADR-109 §4 requires. SL-2 is **case B**: ours, and its prerequisite is stated as a bounded contract without executing SL-3 to discover it — SL-2's own SA-2.2 and SA-2.4, **plus the settled `opportunity.closure` / `closure_reason` vocabulary** that this Slice's lifecycle operation consumes and that Technical Plan §11 assigns to SL-2/SL-8 with no human gate *— as assessed on 2026-09-05; the closure half of that sentence was later found to be a misassignment and is now SL-3's own in-Slice work by the human ownership decision of 2026-09-06 (§4). Criterion 8's Pass verdict is unchanged and is not revisited* |

**Result: READY.** All eight criteria pass. *As evaluated on 2026-09-05* the Slice was **READY but not Executable**, because the case-B prerequisite was not yet actually satisfied and Cycle planning still had to sequence SL-2 first (Methodology §12.1). **That is no longer the current fact: SL-2 reached Done on 2026-09-06 (§6) and the prerequisite is satisfied.** Criterion 8 was assessed on 2026-09-05 against a dependency statement that also named a closure/fact-vocabulary half, since found to have been **misassigned to SL-2** and, by the human ownership decision of 2026-09-06, owned in-Slice by SL-3 (§4). The readiness verdict is not revisited here — criterion 8's substance is unchanged, since that work was always inside SL-3's own acceptance contract and Definition of Done — and the correction is recorded where it belongs, in the dependency itself. This evaluation stays as the dated readiness record; executability is derived elsewhere and is not maintained here (§19.2).

**On lineage versus business closure — the review's decisive question.** Two successive reviews sharpened this Slice. The first found that the draft proved canonicalization but not supersession. The second found the deeper problem: **a relationship edge cannot by itself achieve "one canonical ongoing responsibility"**, because ADR-109 §4 forbids the relationship operation from closing anything, while S1 §8.6 requires the non-canonical Opportunity to *close* as duplicate. Persisting `duplicate_of` and calling the Slice done would have left two ongoing business responsibilities standing.

The approved sources resolve it without invention. S1 **§8.10** approves `duplicate` and `superseded` as closure outcomes with separate reason and evidence; S1 **§8.16** requires closing to leave evidence and auditability; **TD-8** names `opportunity.closure` (outcome/reason/evidence per §8.10) on the CURRENT `case_facts` supersession and provenance mechanics. So the complete flow is **two coordinated governed operations** — the lineage edge through the authorized relationship helper, and the business closure through the canonical fact mechanism — and SA-3.1, SA-3.4 and SA-3.11 now contract exactly that separation. This is outcome **A**: sources sufficient, no owning-artifact gap.

**What the sources still do not define, and this Slice does not invent:** when supersession should be chosen. ADR-109 §7 leaves survivor and reconciliation algorithms to downstream design, so SA-3.11 begins from a governed determination rather than manufacturing one. **Nor does the Slice assert a runtime transition**: S1 §8.7 keeps business closure and runtime status distinct, and no approved source ties one to the other.

**Estimate materiality.** The frozen `1–2 days` / Medium is **unchanged and remains valid**. TD-7 always assigned *"duplicate canonicalization + supersession flows"* to this Slice, and canonicalization has always meant one ongoing canonical responsibility — the closure half was implicit existing scope that the draft under-specified, not new work introduced now. It adds a second governed operation to coordinate, which is real but small: the `case_facts` mechanism is CURRENT and the fact key arrives from SL-2. Medium confidence already carried that dependency as its stated uncertainty driver. The estimate is **not re-dated or re-frozen**.

**On the open questions the stub carried.** Both are resolved from governing sources rather than invented. *"S1 EC-06 / AC-15 scenario mapping"* — EC-06 governs preserving both Cases until canonical resolution (SA-3.1, SA-3.2) and AC-15 governs the conflicting-facts case (SA-3.6). *"What lineage queryable must return"* — TD-7 and ADR-109 §3 already fix it: the typed relationship, direction, status, actor/reason, evidence refs and provenance, retrievable as structured data rather than parsed from free text (SA-3.3).

**Why the horizon question this reopens is worth stating plainly.** SL-7 becoming READY does not widen what can be worked on today: it is the second READY Slice, and both are gated — SL-4 on genuine elapsed time, SL-7 on SL-4. What it does change is that R1 now has a READY candidate that is **not** blocked on a Traditional Gu cross-repo contract, which every candidate examined in the v1.18 replenishment attempt was.
