### SL-6 — Bindings + authority (advisory) — evaluated 2026-09-17

**Why this pass ran.** With Q14 and Q15 ratified, the READY Horizon was still empty and the continuity loop (Methodology §12.3 case E) asked what could legitimately be progressed. SL-6 was elaborated ahead of any new Slice because it is an existing stub, because readiness replenishment needs no approval, and because §4 recorded **two entries that are in tension**: *"what 'resolver answers match observed reality' is measured against"* as an open elaboration question, and *"C2 advisory availability"* as a prerequisite. The pass was asked to settle whether SL-6 can prove the resolver against independently observed legacy reality while staying strictly log-only and **without** the Traditional Gu router calling `/api/legacy/authority`.

**The answer to the question as asked is yes, and it does not make the Slice READY.** C2 is not the binding constraint, and finding that out moved the blocker rather than removing it.

| # | Definition-of-Ready criterion (Methodology §10.2) | Result | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Governing behavior / architectural intent sufficiently approved | **Pass** | **ADR-107** is accepted and carries the doctrine: four authority concepts kept distinct, conflict fails safe, transport is never authority. **TD-3** and **TD-4** are approved with the Technical Plan, and TD-3's C2 precision (v1.19, from audit §24.6) names the legacy seam and pins the evidence vocabulary. **AC-4** carries the binding/authority lifecycle distinction |
| 2 | No unresolved consequential product question inside Slice scope | **FAIL** | **The conversation-authority evidence path is unsettled, and it is an architecture question rather than an engineering choice — recorded as §8 Q17.** TD-3 says legacy `bypass_bot` signals *"map in via ingestion (C1) as evidence"*. That is the only evidence path any approved artifact names, and it makes SL-6 depend on **C1**, which is not live. The alternative — a bounded read of the lead runtime document at resolve time — is mechanically available and arguably better for a pre-effect gate, but it is a change to TD-3 with real consequences, and choosing it silently to obtain readiness is exactly what this pass was told not to do |
| 3 | Slice Acceptance Contract stated and testable | **FAIL** | Not stated, and deliberately not invented. The contract's central assertion is the measurement criterion 2 blocks, so authoring SA-6.1…SA-6.n now would manufacture the false precision §4 warns against. What the measurement must be is settled below; what it may be measured against is not |
| 4 | Required evidence producible — or the verification capability is in-Slice | **Partial** | The deterministic half is producible today: the selftest harness and the DB-backed cross-tenant suite exist, and a resolver plus a table with CHECKs is ordinary work. The **live half is not**, for the same reason as criterion 2 — no Gu OS component is currently permitted to observe legacy authority state (SL-1 excluded it by name, `allowlist.ts:113`), and no other producer exists |
| 5 | Release Scope declared | **FAIL** | Not declared. Unlike SL-5 (§8 Q2) and SL-9 (§8 Q3), SL-6 carries no recorded Release Scope question, and the answer is not derivable: whether *"for pilot conversations"* means hosted evidence over pilot Organization data or a production advisory path depends on which evidence path Q17 settles |
| 6 | Security / tenancy / authority / data / external-effect impact assessed | **Partial** | The shape is clear and favourable. **External effects: none** — SL-6 is log-only inside the shadow stage, it changes no authority, and `runtime_authority` stays `legacy` throughout (see below). Two boundaries are already sharp in the sources and would be enforced structurally: `advisor_wa` rows carry null authority columns by CHECK, so off-thread evidence cannot mint authority (TD-4, audit §24.6 — the capture path contains no occurrence of the bypass field at all, which makes it a property of the source and not only a policy); and the per-lead takeover flag is never conflated with the per-number kill switch. What is **not** assessed is the tenancy and staleness profile of the evidence path, which is Q17's substance rather than a gap in this pass |
| 7 | Estimate and estimate confidence recorded | **FAIL** | Not recorded. An estimate frozen across an undecided evidence path would be a prediction about two different Slices |
| 8 | Dependencies satisfy the §10.2 rule | **FAIL** | **SL-2 is satisfied** (Done). The cross-repo dependency is where the recorded plan is wrong, and correcting it is this pass's main result. **`[L:C2 advisory]` is not the binding gate:** the router calling the endpoint produces router-side recording, which belongs to C2 and to SL-11's enforcement, and **no part of SL-6's inspectable outcome requires it**. The endpoint can be built, signed and proven Gu OS-side against the ratified ADR-111 fixture with nothing running on the other side. The binding gate is the **evidence path** of criterion 2, which as written is **C1**. Neither marker is changed here, because changing a governing dependency is Q17's to settle and not this evaluation's |

**Result: NOT READY.** Four criteria fail and two are partial. **The exact remaining blocker is §8 Q17 — the conversation-authority evidence path — and it is not C2.** Every other failing criterion is downstream of it: the acceptance contract, the Release Scope and the estimate all resolve differently depending on how Q17 settles, and none of them is independently blocked.

**What the pass settled — the measurement, which was the open question.** *"The resolver's authority answer matches observed reality"* can only mean one thing, and reaching it ruled out two readings that look plausible.

- **It cannot be measured against the resolver's own inputs.** If conversation authority reaches Gu OS through C1 events and the comparison then checks the resolver against those same events, the measurement is circular and proves only that the resolver reads its inputs. An independent observation of legacy is structurally required, which is why the measurement question and the evidence-path question turned out to be **one question and not two**.
- **It cannot be measured on runtime authority, because that answer is vacuous at SL-6.** `operational_cases.runtime_authority` exists (migration `00081`) and the admission path sets **`legacy`** on every relationship Case (`admit.ts:674`). **No code anywhere writes `gu_os`** — it appears only as an enum value, in selftest overrides, and in `verify-portfolio.ts:415`'s explicit note that no Organization Case holds it before SL-11. So the runtime half of the resolver returns `legacy` for every Case by construction, and a measurement built on it would pass without proving anything.
- **What is left is the substantive reading:** whether Gu OS's four-concept authority model, applied to independently observed legacy inputs, reaches the **same verdict legacy's own rule reaches** — where legacy's rule is a swept `bypass_bot` with `last_owner_interaction_wba` and a five-minute window (audit §24.6), and Gu OS's must additionally refuse to let off-thread `advisor_wa` evidence mint authority. That is a genuine semantic equivalence test on the interesting concept, `human_active`, and it is what SL-6 should measure.

**A second finding, recorded because it raises SL-6's real cost.** SL-6 owes SL-7 more than a log line. `must-surface.ts:428` carries the `authorityConflict()` rule with `snapshot.authority_conflict` hard-wired to `null` in live assembly (`snapshot.ts:352`), and `work-portfolio.ts:54` names SL-6 as its producer. The rule needs a **durable** `authority_resolution` ref with a `resolution_id`, a `state` and a `detected_at`, so SL-6 must persist authority resolutions rather than only emit them — and the SL-7 hand-off (§4) asks for the conflict path *proven live*, which needs a real conflict and therefore real authority evidence.

**Separation of the Gu OS-side work, as the elaboration was asked to produce.**

| Piece | Belongs to | Needs the other team? |
|---|---|---|
| `external_conversation_bindings` + the `advisor_wa` null-authority CHECK (TD-4) | **SL-6 only** | No |
| TD-3 conversation-authority fields, `resolveInteractionAuthority`, fail-safe semantics | **SL-6 only** | No |
| Durable `authority_resolution` records feeding SL-7's rule | **SL-6 only** | No |
| Authority-change case events (`payload.kind = "authority_changed"`, TD-11) | **SL-6 only** | No |
| The `apps/web/src/app/api/legacy/` route tree and the TD-13 verifier bridge | **Shared with SL-5 / C1** — Appendix D.1 records one bridge serving C1 inbound, C2 inbound and callbacks | No, to build and prove |
| Whatever populates conversation authority from legacy takeover state | **Q17** | **As written, yes — C1** |
| A gateway capability for lead runtime authority context, if Q17 chooses the read path | **SL-6**, as a capability-surface change | No — the existing four capabilities are bootstrap adapters reading Firestore and Mongo directly, so this needs no C6 endpoint |

**v1.52 correction of the dated first-pass assignment above.** The row that placed `authority_changed` case events in *"SL-6 only"* is left as the 2026-09-17 elaboration recorded it. It is **not** current contract: SL-6 must not fabricate that event from an advisory resolution. Ownership is deferred — see §4 and Technical Plan TD-3.

**What this pass deliberately did not do.** It did not weaken the outcome to obtain readiness, and the vacuous-measurement reading above is precisely the weakening that was available. It did not author an acceptance contract over an undecided measurement. It did not change SL-6's `[L:C2 advisory]` marker, even though it established the marker names the wrong contract, because a governing dependency is repaired by decision and not by an evaluation record. It did not move enforcement forward from SL-11, introduce any prospect-facing effect, or reopen ADR-111 or ADR-112. **Q11 was not worked.**

### SL-6 — Bindings + authority (advisory) — re-evaluated 2026-09-17 after §8 Q17

**The record above is kept exactly as measured.** It was correct under the state that governed it: the evidence path was undecided, and four criteria could not pass while it was. **The Accountable resolved Q17 the same day** — option (c), both paths with roles explicitly separated — and every criterion that failed did so *because* of Q17. This is the re-evaluation against the settled contract in §4, and it is a second dated record rather than an edit to the first.

| # | Definition-of-Ready criterion (Methodology §10.2) | Result | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Governing behavior / architectural intent sufficiently approved | **Pass** | Unchanged from the first pass, and Q17 **strengthened** it rather than adding to it. **ADR-107** is Accepted direction; **AC-4** §9 carries the four dimensions, the fail-safe and *"Pause speaking ≠ pause thinking ≠ pause the Case"*; **TD-3 / TD-4** are approved, now with Q17's evidence-path section (Technical Plan v1.25). The elaboration surfaced that **AC-1 §6.5 and AC-4 §9.5 already required re-reading current authoritative state before acting**, so Q17 reconciles TD-3's narrower ingestion-only wording with approved architecture instead of departing from it |
| 2 | No unresolved consequential product question inside Slice scope | **Pass** | **Q17 is RESOLVED** and it was the only one. Its resolution also closed the adjacent ambiguities rather than leaving them implicit: no last-write-wins between the paths (EC-09), no silent fallback to a stale projection, the five-minute window confined to the oracle as an observation and explicitly not a Gu OS invariant, and the `bootstrap_direct` → `legacy_read_api` handover condition stated before the read can be load-bearing for effects. What remains inside the Slice is engineering |
| 3 | Slice Acceptance Contract stated and testable | **Pass** | **SA-6.1 … SA-6.14** in §4, each naming a governing source and an evidence type. The central one, SA-6.9, is testable **because** Q17 made the oracle independent of the resolver — it carries a structural test that the two share no decision code, which is what keeps the equivalence claim from being circular. Deliberately absent: any assertion that enforcement works, or that the verdict generalizes beyond the pilot set |
| 4 | Required evidence producible — or the verification capability is in-Slice | **Pass** | The deterministic half runs on capability that exists: the selftest harness, the cross-tenant suite (SL-0), and the gateway's fixture and drift harness (SL-1), which the new capability extends rather than reinvents. The hosted half is the shape SL-1, SL-2, SL-3 and SL-7 already used — the application in the operator's process against staging with real pilot credentials. The **equivalence harness and the oracle are verification capability built inside the Slice**, which §10.2 admits, as SL-14's verifier extension did |
| 5 | Release Scope declared | **Pass** | **RS-2 hosted**, now **derivable** rather than open: shadow stage, no prospect-facing effect, and four Slices of precedent. Minimum evidence and explicit non-claims are in §4. This is the criterion Q17 most directly unblocked — it was not answerable while the evidence path could still have been C1-only |
| 6 | Security / tenancy / authority / data / external-effect impact assessed | **Pass** | Eight-dimension table in §4. **External effects: none.** The authority answer is inert by construction and SA-6.11 tests that nothing pauses, moves or suppresses. The one genuine widening is **authority-bearing data entering the gateway's read surface**, and it is bounded three ways: SA-6.4's semantic contract forbids raw documents, SA-6.14 structurally guards the window from becoming an invariant, and Q17 requires a least-privilege legacy-side implementation before the read is load-bearing for effects. Tenancy is Organization-scoped with the cross-tenant suite gating it (SA-6.13). **No ADR-111 key is provisioned** — §8 Q16 is untouched and this Slice does not need it, because the endpoint is proven against the fixture |
| 7 | Estimate and estimate confidence recorded | **Pass** | `4–6 days`, **Medium**, drivers named in §4: three first-times raise it above SL-7's range, typed consuming contracts and four capability precedents hold it off High, and §1's over-estimation finding argues for the lower bound. **Frozen at READY on 2026-09-17** (Methodology §10.4) |
| 8 | Dependencies satisfy the §10.2 rule | **Pass** | **SL-1, SL-2 and SL-7 all satisfied** (Done) — the gateway pattern, the admitted Cases and bindings, and the consuming must-surface rule respectively. **No `[L:Cn]` cross-repo contract gates this Slice.** That is Q17's correction, made at the layer that owns the dependency: C2 advisory is required for router-side recording and for SL-11's enforcement, and is not a prerequisite of SL-6's outcome. **This is the first R1 Slice to shed a cross-repo marker**, and it did so by decision rather than by reinterpretation |

**Result: READY.** All eight criteria pass, and the estimate is frozen at this point (Methodology §10.4). **The READY Horizon is no longer empty — for the first time since SL-12 closed on 2026-09-14.**

**Planned the same day.** Readiness assigns no Execution Cycle and no Accountable / DRI (Methodology §10.2, §12.1), and this evaluation assigned neither. The **Accountable confirmed SL-6 into Cycle 3 as order 7 on 2026-09-17** with himself as Accountable / DRI (§5), conditional on repository truth carrying no conflicting accountability fact — which the reconciliation recorded there confirmed it does not. **SL-6 is therefore Planned and Executable**: prerequisites actually satisfied, capacity free with orders 1–6 closed. Just-in-time Task planning follows with no further routine approval. **v1.51 recorded that the implementation branch would follow this record's merge**, the rule applied to SL-7, SL-12 and SL-14. **That calendar order did not occur** and is not rewritten: the T1–T6 commits were authored as descendants of `4b46293` before PR #74 merged. The correction is in v1.52 / §5.

**What the two passes together are worth recording.** The first pass is the more instructive one, and its verdict was not wasted. Replenishment had been re-tested three times since SL-12 and reported the same boundary each time — *every remaining Slice waits on another team* — which was true of the **set** and false of **SL-6 specifically**. What surfaced the error was not new information from either repository but elaborating the Slice far enough to ask what its acceptance assertion would actually be measured against, at which point the recorded gate and the real one turned out to be different contracts. **A dependency marker is a claim, and this one had never been tested.** The second finding compounds it: the two entries §4 carried — an open measurement question and a named prerequisite — were not two items of missing detail but **one question wearing two labels**, and neither could be answered without the other.

### SL-15 — Contact / legacy-lead identity seam (repair) — evaluated 2026-09-18

**Why this pass ran.** The Accountable resolved §8 Q18 as alternative 3 the same day and instructed the development system to create and elaborate the smallest bounded repair Slice through normal readiness rules. The Slice's existence, identity (SL-15), identity rule and repair boundary come from that decision. This pass tests Definition of Ready; it does not implement, and it does not admit the Slice to a Cycle.

| # | Definition-of-Ready criterion (Methodology §10.2) | Result | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Governing behavior / architectural intent sufficiently approved | **Pass** | **S1** (approved) §6.1 / §8.5.2 / EC-04; **ADR-106** Accepted direction; **AC-4** §9.7; **TD-1 / TD-4 / TD-8** approved. **The Slice's existence, alternative-3 classification and Contact materialization rule** are the Accountable's Q18 decision of 2026-09-18. Technical Plan **v1.31** records the TD-8 technical half; it decides nothing new |
| 2 | No unresolved consequential product question inside Slice scope | **Pass** | Q18 is **RESOLVED**. The identity rule answers when a Contact may be minted (provisional Organization-scoped anchor for one opaque admitted `legacy_lead` id) and what that assertion does not mean. Alternatives 1 and 2 are refused. What remains is engineering: the SQL/TS primitive after `bootstrap_organization`, the admission hook, and the backfill caller of `attachExternalConversationBinding`. Merge/master-data is excluded |
| 3 | Slice Acceptance Contract stated and testable | **Pass** | SA-15.1 … SA-15.11 (§4), each naming a governing source and an evidence type. All are deterministic except the hosted backfill half of SA-15.9 / SA-15.10 |
| 4 | The required evidence can be produced — or creating the verification capability is part of the Slice | **Pass** | Selftests, the cross-tenant suite and admission's existing harness exist. A hosted backfill verifier against an already-admitted real Case is verification capability built inside the Slice, as §10.2 admits |
| 5 | Release Scope declared | **Pass** | **RS-2 hosted**, derived from the inspectable outcome (a real governed-admitted Lead), SL-2's precedent and Methodology §14.2. Minimum evidence and non-claims are in §4 |
| 6 | Security / tenancy / authority / data / external-effect impact assessed | **Pass** | Nine-dimension risk table (§4). **External effects: none.** Identity writes are Organization-scoped and fail closed across tenants. A binding is never an authority grant. No new secret class |
| 7 | Estimate and estimate confidence recorded | **Pass** | `1–2 days`, **Medium**. Drivers named in §4. **Frozen at READY on 2026-09-18** |
| 8 | Dependencies satisfy the §10.2 rule | **Pass** | **SL-0 and SL-2 satisfied** (Done). The conversation-binding helper exists on `main` (`7a71351`). **No `[L:Cn]` contract.** Q18 resolved. Independent of C1 for Contact minting |

**Result: READY.** All eight criteria pass, and the estimate is frozen at this point (Methodology §10.4).

**READY is eligibility only.** *As evaluated on 2026-09-18* the Slice was READY and not Planned: the Q18 decision created the Slice and the identity rule; it did not itself admit the Slice to Cycle 3. **That is no longer the current planning fact:** the Accountable confirmed Cycle 3 order 8 and himself as Accountable / DRI the same day (§5). This evaluation stays as the dated readiness record; planning and executability are derived in §5 and are not maintained here (§19.2). T7 is not started.

**What this pass did, and what it deliberately did not.**

- **It took the Slice's existence, identity and identity rule from the decision.** It quoted the rule rather than paraphrasing it into a weaker one.
- **It classified the omission without rewriting SL-2 Done.** §6 keeps the 2026-09-06 verdict; a dated later observation points here.
- **It did not** absorb the work into SL-6, assign C1, reopen Q17, start T7, provision a TD-13 key, or broaden into Contact merge.
